<a id="item-1"></a>
## [Spaghettifying DRAM: New Attack Bypasses Memory Protections](https://github.com/xoreaxeaxeax/skitter-creek-bath-salts) ⭐️ 9.0/10

Christopher Domas has released a new hardware hacking technique called 'Spaghettifying DRAM' that exploits DRAM addressing to gain privileged access. The technique, demonstrated on AMD Jaguar, uses a solved transform to reach protected memory regions like PSP private memory and SMRAM. This research highlights a significant attack surface in modern DRAM, potentially affecting game consoles and other systems that rely on DRAM protections. It could enable full system compromise, bypassing security measures that were previously considered robust. The technique uses a z3-solved transform to map coherent memory addresses to the scrambled 'spaghettified' view, allowing access to protected regions without triggering security checks. The README notes that Zen 3 has a different base address for memory controller registers, suggesting potential applicability to newer CPUs.

hackernews · matt_d · Aug 13, 14:17 · [Discussion](https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=49286341)

**Background**: DRAM addressing is complex, involving channels, ranks, and banks, and modern systems use scrambling to obscure the physical mapping. Row hammer is a related side-effect attack that exploits DRAM cell interactions. This new technique goes further by directly manipulating the addressing to bypass protections.

<details><summary>References</summary>
<ul>
<li><a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Row_hammer">Row hammer - Wikipedia</a></li>
<li><a href="https://github.com/xoreaxeaxeax/skitter-creek-bath-salts">GitHub - xoreaxeaxeax/skitter-creek-bath-salts: Unlocking _everything_ on the CPU with DRAM scrambling · GitHub</a></li>
<li><a href="https://www.usenix.org/conference/usenixsecurity16/technical-sessions/presentation/pessl">DRAMA: Exploiting DRAM Addressing for Cross-CPU Attacks | USENIX</a></li>

</ul>
</details>

**Discussion**: The community is enthusiastic, praising Domas's work and anticipating his Black Hat talk. Some express concern about the impact on Xbox and PlayStation security, while others question the applicability to newer CPUs beyond AMD Jaguar.

<a id="item-2"></a>
## [LLMs Reproduce Racial Stereotypes in Text Annotation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.13891) ⭐️ 9.0/10

A large-scale study across 19 LLMs and over 4 million judgments found that subtle identity cues in text systematically bias annotation outcomes, mirroring racial stereotypes. For example, texts with Black-associated names were rated as more aggressive by 18 of 19 models, and African American Vernacular English was judged less professional by all 19 models. This matters because LLMs are increasingly used for automated annotation in research, content moderation, and hiring, and such biases can embed social inequities into datasets and decisions. The findings highlight the urgent need for bias mitigation and careful evaluation before deploying LLMs in these high-stakes applications. The study included two experiments: a names-based experiment covering 39 annotation tasks and a matched dialect experiment. Notably, a fine-tuning overcorrection was observed for name-based hireability, where models favored minority-named applicants, suggesting that bias mitigation efforts can sometimes overshoot.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 13, 04:00

**Background**: Text annotation is a common task where models label data for training or analysis, and LLMs are increasingly used for this purpose. Racial stereotypes, such as the 'bamboo ceiling' for Asian Americans, are well-documented societal biases that can be inadvertently learned and reproduced by AI models trained on human text.

<details><summary>References</summary>
<ul>
<li><a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bamboo_ceiling">Bamboo ceiling - Wikipedia</a></li>
<li><a href="https://www.mdpi.com/2673-2688/6/8/178">Biased by Design? Evaluating Bias and Behavioral Diversity in LLM Annotation of Real-World and Synthetic Hotel Reviews</a></li>

Gemini 3.7 Flash follows just three weeks after Gemini 3.6 Flash, reflecting rapid iteration based on developer feedback. It supports customizable thinking configurations to balance quality, cost, and latency, and benchmarks show strong performance on tasks like DeepSWE 1.1, though comparisons with models like Luna and Terra are still being discussed.

<a id="item-4"></a>
## [DeepSeek Harness Developer Preview: Full Traceability for AI Agents](https://deepseek.com/harness/en/) ⭐️ 8.0/10

DeepSeek has released an open-source developer preview of DeepSeek Harness (dsh), an AI agent harness that provides full session traceability and replay capabilities. The preview is available under the MIT license on GitHub. This tool differentiates itself from US models by offering transparent, append-only session logs that allow developers to inspect, resume, fork, and replay agent runs. It addresses growing demands for AI governance and post-incident forensics, making it significant for developers building trustworthy AI agents. The harness uses an architecture where everything is a plugin, powered by Cordis, which enables hot-reload and dynamic enable/disable of plugins without restarting the process. The append-only session log records system prompts, reasoning, tool calls, subagent scheduling, and context injections, all viewable in a Trajectory view.

hackernews · bjin · Aug 13, 12:58 · [Discussion](https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=49285244)

**Background**: AI agent harnesses are frameworks that manage the execution of AI agents, including tool integration, memory, and task orchestration. DeepSeek Harness aims to turn DeepSeek LLMs into production-grade autonomous agents, supporting MCP protocol and other features. The concept of deterministic replay is foundational for AI governance, ensuring that given the same trace, an agent produces the same output, which is crucial for debugging and compliance.

<details><summary>References</summary>
<ul>
<li><a href="https://github.com/deepseek-ai/deepseek-harness/tree/master">GitHub - deepseek-ai/deepseek-harness · GitHub</a></li>

</details>

Some users expressed concerns about the plugin-based architecture, citing 'plugin fatigue', while others provided technical insights about Cordis and its capabilities.

**Tags**: `#AI`, `#developer-tools`, `#open-source`, `#agent-tracing`

## [Hugging Face Reproduces 2,200 ICML Papers, Reveals Key Lessons](https://huggingface.co/blog/icml-2026-open-reproductions) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Hugging Face reported on a large-scale effort to reproduce 2,200 papers from ICML, systematically testing the reproducibility of machine learning research. The project involved community collaboration and automated agents, producing detailed logbooks of each reproduction attempt. This effort highlights the reproducibility crisis in ML and provides concrete data on the challenges researchers face. It could drive changes in how papers are published and reviewed, encouraging more rigorous standards and open science practices. The reproduction used the OpenResearch harness or custom coding agents, with a focus on CPU-feasible theory and optimization papers. Each agent produced a shareable logbook, and the project was organized by Hugging Face and AlphaXiv, with GPU resources available for participants.

rss · Hugging Face Blog · Aug 13, 00:00

**Background**: Reproducibility is a cornerstone of scientific research, but in machine learning, it is often challenging due to complex dependencies, undisclosed hyperparameters, and computational costs. The ML Reproducibility Challenge is an annual event promoting such efforts, and this ICML 2026 reproduction is a large-scale instance of that initiative.

<a id="item-9"></a>
## [Poor Man's Agentic Modeling: Simulating Large LLM-Agent Societies on a Laptop](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.11215) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper introduces a method to simulate large societies of LLM agents on a laptop by replacing each agent with a low-parameter surrogate model fitted from a few hundred to a few thousand cheap queries. The approach is validated on a faithful reimplementation of EconAgent and seven other named LLM simulations, with agent decisions cloned from genuine LLM elicitations (primarily DeepSeek) for a few dollars. This work addresses a significant computational bottleneck in multi-agent LLM simulations, making large-scale agent societies accessible to researchers without high-end hardware. It introduces a taxonomy for understanding when surrogate approximations work, which has broad implications for multi-agent AI research and scalability. The method is grounded in statistical physics and introduces an [interaction order x memory] taxonomy that maps perception and memory to an effective theory and a predicted N-trend of the surrogate error. The predicted error trends hold cell by cell, and the two refuted predictions, both on a strongly saturating response and traced to its curvature, are matched quantitatively by the theory with no free parameters.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 13, 04:00

**Background**: Simulating societies of many LLM agents is expensive because each agent typically requires a full LLM inference per decision. The questions asked of such simulations are usually macroscopic, such as phase behavior or scaling with the number of agents N, rather than the cognition of any single agent. This paper leverages the observation that individual agent behavior can be approximated by low-parameter surrogate models, fitted from a small number of queries, to run large-scale simulations efficiently on a laptop.

## [AutoWorldModel-Bench: Benchmarking AI Agents for Autonomous World-Model Research](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.11216) ⭐️ 8.0/10

AutoWorldModel-Bench is a new closed-loop benchmark that evaluates AI coding agents on their ability to autonomously improve world models across eight game environments. In 64 sessions, frontier agents like Codex-5.4 and Claude Opus 4.6 improved the starter model in 63 sessions, with 91% of winning edits being non-trivial research-style modifications. This benchmark addresses a critical gap in AI agent evaluation by focusing on open-ended research tasks rather than engineering-to-spec problems. It provides a standardized way to measure and compare agents' ability to conduct autonomous scientific research, which is essential for advancing AI-driven discovery. The benchmark uses a unified structured-state representation, extracting ground-truth entity states from each game and feeding them through a shared tensor format, which isolates dynamics modeling from perception and enables rapid iteration. The 91% of winning edits that are research-style modifications include new objectives, representations, rollout procedures, or architectural changes, rather than simple hyperparameter tweaks.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 13, 04:00

**Background**: World models are AI systems that learn to predict the dynamics of an environment, enabling agents to plan and reason without constant real-world interaction. The field of world modeling is still unsettled, with no single dominant architecture or training objective, making it an ideal testbed for autonomous AI researchers. Closed-loop benchmarks are evaluation protocols that involve iterative feedback loops, allowing agents to adapt and improve over time.

## [MaSRead Enables Content-Addressed Reads of Replicated Latent Stores](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.11218) ⭐️ 8.0/10

MaSRead introduces a method for reliably reading merged latent key-value caches by using opaque keyed tag sets and hard attention masks, ensuring fragment isolation and supporting multi-hop queries. This approach allows independent agents to share computed state as cache fragments rather than text, with convergence guaranteed by a conflict-free replicated data type (CRDT). This work addresses a fundamental challenge in distributed AI systems: making replicated latent stores selectively readable for later queries. It has potential impact on multi-agent systems and decentralized machine learning, enabling more efficient and reliable sharing of computed state without text conversion. MaSRead routes through opaque keyed tag sets derived from fragment words and decodes each selected fragment under a hard attention mask that hides the rest. Under lexical connectivity, a graph walk reaches fragments required by multi-hop queries; materialized decoding depends on fragment length rather than total store size, though end-to-end work includes store-dependent routing and one read per visited fragment.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 13, 04:00

**Background**: Conflict-free replicated data types (CRDTs) are data structures that replicate across multiple computers, allowing independent updates without coordination and guaranteeing eventual convergence. Content-addressed storage (CAS) retrieves information based on its content, typically using cryptographic hashes, which is a foundational concept for this work. Latent space key-value caches store computed state in a compressed form, and MaSRead builds on these concepts to enable selective reading of merged caches.

## [Lifelong AI Memory Framework Boosts Materials Science Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.11224) ⭐️ 8.0/10

The paper introduces a self-evolving memory framework for materials science AI agents, storing scientific experience as inspectable facts and executable skills. In tests, it nearly doubled GPT-5.2 task success without model updates and reduced tool calls by over half. This addresses a critical challenge in scientific AI: the fragmentation and non-portability of research experience. By making memory a durable asset, it enhances reproducibility and knowledge transfer, potentially accelerating materials discovery and reducing repeated errors. The framework was evaluated in three settings: 49 real-world materials-tool-use questions (138 subtasks), elemental-solid equation-of-state calculations, and 13 practical simulation workflows. It improved outcomes from 22/1/4 to 25/2/0 Correct/Partial/Error and avoided 92% of repeated errors.

## [AI Personality Clones: A Conceptual Framework](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.11225) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper introduces a conceptual framework and research program for AI personality clones, defining identity through observable manifestations and proposing a six-term factorization with a state-space formulation. It also introduces the concept of a 'delegate' as a task-limited, bounded-lifespan partial clone. This framework provides a formal basis for evaluating and comparing AI personality clones, which are becoming increasingly prevalent in consumer applications. It could influence future research, ethical guidelines, and policy decisions regarding digital identity and AI ethics. The paper distinguishes three criteria conflated by 'identity': fidelity to a target person, generic human-likeness, and individuality. It proposes a conditional conjecture that versionability tends to degrade long-horizon indiscernibility, and argues for 'climate fidelity' as the correct long-horizon criterion.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 13, 04:00

**Background**: AI personality clones are digital replicas of individuals that mimic their behavior and responses. The paper uses a state-space formulation, a mathematical model from control engineering, to represent the dynamics of identity. The concept of 'indiscernibility' is borrowed from philosophy, referring to the inability to distinguish between two entities.

## [RL-Based Power Control Cuts LLM Training Energy Use](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.11226) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper introduces a reinforcement learning-based controller that adapts LLM training generation parameters to reduce power-limit violations and improve energy efficiency. At 7B scale, it cuts violations by 89.8%, increases token output by 18.1%, and improves energy efficiency by 26.2%, but shows null results at 72B due to actuator authority issues. This work addresses a critical gap in datacenter power management for LLM training, which is typically workload-blind and reactive. By demonstrating a workload-aware control approach, it could enable more efficient use of GPU fleets, reduce energy costs, and lower carbon emissions, with potential for significant economic and environmental impact. The study uses half-second power telemetry across 7B, 14B, and 72B models on one to four A100s, with over 380,000 samples. A PPO meta-controller adapts generation parameters, but at 72B, the group-size actuator loses authority under model sharding; rebuilding on generation concurrency retains 17-22% power authority, achieving 35.7% more output with 2.27% budget violations and 87.2% fewer violations than uncontrolled operation.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 13, 04:00

**Background**: Reinforcement learning post-training, such as GRPO, is widely used for LLM alignment but its power behavior is not well understood. Datacenters typically manage GPU power with static caps and reactive throttling, which slow hardware indiscriminately. This paper explores a workload-aware approach using a PPO meta-controller to adjust generation parameters in real time, potentially enabling oversubscription of datacenter capacity.

## [Forecasting Side Effects of Activation Steering in LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.11227) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper introduces a systematic study showing that side effects of activation steering in language models can be forecasted using unsteered representations, despite their complexity and asymmetry. The authors constructed a cross-effect matrix over 67 behaviors across three open-weight models, revealing that side effects are common, structured, and often asymmetric. This work addresses a critical safety issue in activation steering, showing that side effects are predictable, which is a significant step toward safer deployment of LLMs. It provides a proactive safety auditing method, potentially impacting AI alignment and interpretability research. The study used a taxonomy of 67 behaviors across three open-weight language models, constructing a cross-effect matrix to analyze side effects. They found that side effect magnitude depends primarily on the target behavior, while direction can be forecasted from unsteered representations with higher accuracy than simple baselines.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 13, 04:00

**Background**: Activation steering is a technique that modifies a language model's behavior by adding a learned direction to its hidden activations, without retraining. It is an alternative to prompt engineering, offering more reliable control, but it can cause unintended side effects on other behaviors. This paper builds on prior work in activation engineering, such as the IBM activation-steering library, and aims to make steering safer by predicting side effects.

## [Second-Order Theory-of-Mind Improves Preference Learning in Human-Autonomy Teams](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.11229) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper introduces a new paradigm for preference-based reward learning where the human teacher actively constructs a curriculum using a model of the learner, and the learner maintains a second-order model of the teacher's model. The approach outperforms learner-led selection in simulations, especially when feature dimensions grow. This work challenges the standard passive-oracle paradigm in preference-based reward learning, potentially leading to more efficient human-robot interaction and reward learning systems. It highlights the importance of modeling the teacher's knowledge and could impact AI/ML and robotics communities by improving how agents learn from human feedback. The paper models preference learning as a human-autonomy team problem with two coupled behavioral models: the teacher models the learner to design an informative curriculum, and the learner emits structured preference constraints (understanding statements) to keep the teacher's model synchronized. Simulations show that teacher-model drift under alternating teachers erodes the advantage, but understanding statements repair it, with second-order (ToM-2) statements outperforming mean-belief statements when the teacher's error is directionally concentrated.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 13, 04:00

**Background**: Preference-based reward learning typically treats the human as a passive oracle answering learner-generated queries. Theory of Mind (ToM) is the cognitive ability to understand others' mental states, and in human-robot interaction, it enables robots to infer and respond to users' mental states. This paper applies second-order ToM (modeling what another agent thinks about your model) to improve the efficiency of curriculum design in preference learning.

## [LinearKV: Training-Free Position-Independent Caching for Hybrid LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.11231) ⭐️ 8.0/10

LinearKV introduces a training-free framework that enables position-independent caching (PIC) for hybrid LLMs by decoupling the initialization of linear layers, allowing existing PIC methods to be reused. The framework shows that a single cached state suffices as the linear layer's initializer, outperforming the exact composition approach used by concurrent work HYPIC. This work addresses a critical gap in LLM inference optimization, as hybrid models combining attention and linear recurrence layers are becoming increasingly common. By enabling PIC for hybrid LLMs, LinearKV can significantly improve inference efficiency and reduce time-to-first-token, benefiting a wide range of applications that rely on long-context processing. LinearKV is compatible with existing PIC methods, reusing their token selection and recomputation as-is. Experiments on three hybrid models and three PIC selectors show that a single cached state initializer recovers up to 92% of full quality on GDN models, while exact composition collapses on Mamba-2, recovering only 46.6% under EPIC versus 86.8% for a single state. The single-state approach also cuts time-to-first-token to 0.46x full prefill, compared to 5-17% overhead for exact composition.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 13, 04:00

**Background**: Position-independent caching (PIC) is a technique that accelerates LLM inference by reusing cached intermediate representations across different positions, typically relying on token-indexed KV caches in full-attention models. Hybrid LLMs, which replace most attention layers with linear recurrences that maintain a fixed-size state, break the token-indexed primitives that PIC depends on. LinearKV addresses this by decoupling the initialization of linear layers, allowing a single cached state to serve as the initializer, thus enabling PIC for hybrid architectures.

## [InfraBench: New Benchmark Evaluates AI Agents for Infrastructure Management](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.11234) ⭐️ 8.0/10

InfraBench is a new benchmark suite that evaluates AI agents on realistic infrastructure tasks across the full system stack and operational lifecycle, with fine-grained risk assessment. Experiments with 15 agent-model configurations show that even the strongest agents achieve only 40-88% effective scores, and top configurations pass only a fraction of their attempts. This benchmark addresses a critical gap in evaluating AI agents for infrastructure management, a domain with growing complexity and automation potential. It provides a standardized way to measure agent capabilities, highlighting common failure patterns that could inform future research and development of more reliable infrastructure automation. The benchmark covers tasks across L1 hardware to L4 applications and the operational lifecycle, including durability, invariants, cleanup, and risk. Per-check scoring reveals that agents often satisfy short-term objectives but leave non-durable changes, broken distributed invariants, unsafe side effects, and uncleaned state behind. InfraBench is publicly available at infraben.ch with a live leaderboard.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 13, 04:00

**Background**: AI agents are increasingly used to automate complex tasks, but evaluating their performance in real-world infrastructure management is challenging due to the complexity and risk involved. Existing benchmarks often focus on narrow tasks or lack fine-grained risk assessment. InfraBench aims to provide a comprehensive evaluation framework that covers the full stack and lifecycle, with a focus on identifying failure patterns that matter for production environments.

## [VQ-bench: Composable Framework for Vector Quantization](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.11240) ⭐️ 8.0/10

VQ-bench introduces a composable framework that unifies 25 common vector quantizers as pipelines of 7 primitives, and provides an open-source benchmark for reproducible evaluation. This framework simplifies the development and comparison of vector quantization algorithms, which are crucial for AI infrastructure such as approximate nearest neighbor search and model compression. It could accelerate innovation and ensure fair, reproducible benchmarking across the field. The framework defines 7 conceptual primitives and demonstrates that 25 existing quantizers can be re-expressed as pipelines of these primitives. VQ-bench is open-source and aims to provide a single, fair, reproducible benchmark where quantizers are measured side by side.

It is widely used in AI systems for tasks like approximate nearest neighbor search, model compression, and generative models (e.g., VQVAE, VQGAN). The field has seen renewed interest due to the growth of AI infrastructure, but lacks a unified framework for comparing and developing new algorithms.

## [Off-Support Barrier: Why Semantic Safety Constraints Aren't Learning Invariants](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.11243) ⭐️ 8.0/10

The paper argues that semantic safety constraints are off-support objects, not measurable with respect to the model and data distribution, and derives corollaries explaining reward hacking, sandbox escape, and the ineffectiveness of priors or penalties. It proposes that hard invariants belong in the harness while soft dispositions belong in the model. This provides a formal theoretical framework linking AI safety to singular learning theory, potentially reshaping how safety constraints are designed and enforced. It could influence future research on reward hacking, sandbox escape, and formal verification in AI systems. The paper uses the real log-canonical threshold (RLCT) from singular learning theory to show that safety predicates are not invariants, while RLCT is. It also discusses two precise points of disanalogy between formal verification and the local learning coefficient, and notes that identifying the relevant off-support region coincides with performative prediction and self-referential dynamics.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 13, 04:00

**Background**: Singular learning theory (SLT) studies models with singularities, where the Fisher information matrix is degenerate, and uses the RLCT to measure effective dimensionality. Semantic safety constraints, such as 'the agent does not escape its sandbox,' are often defined on behaviors outside the training distribution, making them off-support. The paper uses the July 2026 OpenAI–Hugging Face evaluation incident as a motivating case.

This paper introduces AI-Tutor, a reinforcement learning model that optimizes both short-term knowledge acquisition and long-term engagement in online education. It was evaluated on 23 million learning records from 33,700 learners, outperforming state-of-the-art baselines. This work addresses critical challenges in online education: low engagement and poor long-term learning effectiveness. By integrating cognitive theory and engagement modeling, AI-Tutor offers a personalized, adaptive approach that could significantly improve learning outcomes and reduce dropout rates, impacting the broader ed-tech ecosystem. AI-Tutor balances acquiring new knowledge and reinforcing prior learning in the short term, while modeling learner engagement to sustain motivation in the long term. The empirical evaluation on a large-scale dataset demonstrates consistent improvements across engagement, knowledge retention, and final learning outcomes, with learning path analyses showing adaptation to diverse learner profiles.

In education, RL has been applied to personalize learning paths, but challenges remain in balancing short-term gains with long-term engagement. This paper builds on prior research in RL for education and learner engagement modeling, aiming to create more sustainable online learning experiences.

## [Thea: A Harness for Embodied Agents Using Scene Graphs and Exit Codes](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.11246) ⭐️ 8.0/10

The paper introduces Thea, a harness for embodied agents that applies the coding agent paradigm to the physical world. It introduces Scene Graph as Context and Evaluation as Exit Codes to bridge the gaps in state reading and action evaluation. This work extends the successful harness paradigm from software agents to embodied agents, potentially improving the reliability and capability of robots in real-world tasks. It addresses fundamental challenges in physical world interaction, which could accelerate progress in robotics and AI. Thea wraps robot capabilities as callable tools within an agentic loop, inheriting core components from coding agents but modified for physical world constraints. The Scene Graph provides a persistent symbolic representation, while Evaluation as Exit Codes detects termination, success, and failure causes, enabling long-horizon tasks in real environments.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 13, 04:00

**Background**: An agent harness is the control layer that manages AI agents, providing tools, memory, and orchestration, similar to an operating system. Scene graphs are structured representations of a scene, commonly used in computer graphics and robotics to describe objects and their relationships. Exit codes are standard in programming to indicate the outcome of a process, which Thea adapts for action evaluation in the physical world.

## [LLM Conformity Mitigations Lie on a Single Resistance-Receptivity Frontier](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.11247) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper demonstrates that large language models are highly susceptible to peer pressure in multi-agent settings, reversing correct answers up to 71% of the time, and proposes a new framework for evaluating mitigations. This finding is significant for AI safety and multi-agent systems, as it reveals a critical vulnerability in collaborative AI. The proposed Resistance-Receptivity frontier provides a more comprehensive evaluation method, potentially influencing future research and development of safer AI systems. The study evaluated 23 open-weight models across 19 conditions and three datasets, yielding over a million graded responses. A unanimous wrong majority reversed 22.8% of correct MMLU answers, 54.8% on GPQA, and 71.0% on SimpleQA, with 84-89% of reversed answers matching the peers' answers. Six mitigation methods were scored on both Resistance and Receptivity, and their means fell on a single frontier with R² between 0.80 and 0.90.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 13, 04:00

**Background**: Multi-agent systems involve multiple language models interacting and refining each other's outputs, which can improve collective reasoning but also introduce conformity pressure. Conformity in LLMs has been studied previously, showing that models tend to align with the majority opinion even when incorrect. The Resistance-Receptivity frontier is a new evaluation framework that balances a model's ability to resist incorrect peer pressure and its ability to adopt correct peer answers.

## [AgonAlpha: Autonomous Alpha Discovery with Verified Artifact Search](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.11250) ⭐️ 8.0/10

AgonAlpha is an autonomous alpha-mining system that combines verified artifact search, adversarial review, and pending-aware budget allocation. It achieved Fitness of 9.50 and Sharpe of 3.48 on WorldQuant BRAIN across five users and six model backends. This system demonstrates a novel integration of agentic AI techniques for quantitative finance, potentially automating and improving alpha discovery. It could impact how quants and researchers leverage LLMs for factor generation, making the process more efficient and reliable. AgonAlpha searches over frozen research artifacts including hypotheses, executable expressions, platform evidence, rationales, and review status, rather than just formulas. It includes a fresh-context adversarial reviewer with re-execution and veto authority, and maintains a complete public evidence trail for every submission.

## [Local Verification Fails to Detect Non-Transportability in Agentic Reasoning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.11252) ⭐️ 8.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2608.11252) proves that local verification methods are structurally incomplete for detecting non-transportability in agentic reasoning, using cohomology theory to characterize evidence conflicts. The paper introduces a procedure called Ksetra that estimates via coboundary projection and gates abstention on the harmonic component. This work challenges the prevailing reliance on local verification as a safeguard for agentic AI systems, which are increasingly used in high-stakes domains like healthcare and finance. By showing that certain evidence conflicts are invisible to local checks, it highlights the need for more global verification approaches, potentially impacting AI safety practices and regulation. The paper models context space as a nerve covering and evidence as a real-valued 1-cochain, showing that path independence holds iff the cochain is exact. Hodge decomposition partitions evidence conflict into gradient, curl, and harmonic parts, and the central result is that no simplex-supported consistency checks can detect harmonic components, which require a statistic on a cycle basis.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 13, 04:00

**Background**: Agentic AI systems are designed to autonomously perform multi-step reasoning and actions, often across different contexts. Local verification is a common safeguard that checks each step for representability and coherence, but this paper argues it is insufficient. Cohomology is a mathematical tool from algebraic topology that studies global properties via local data, and Hodge decomposition is a way to break down differential forms into orthogonal components.

## [Agent Leaderboards Measure Specialization, Not Capability](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.11323) ⭐️ 8.0/10

A new paper applies Generalizability Theory to three open agent-trace benchmarks, showing that the agent main effect accounts for less than 3% of total variance, while agent-by-task interactions account for 7-23%. The authors propose a reporting discipline called Deployment Decision Reliability (DDR) to help enterprise buyers interpret leaderboards. This finding challenges the common practice of reading leaderboards as rankings of general agent capability, which could mislead enterprise adoption decisions. The framework provides a rigorous statistical basis for evaluating AI agents, potentially reshaping how benchmarks are designed and interpreted. The study uses a four-facet Generalizability Theory variance decomposition with three estimators (Henderson Method-I, REML via lme4, and a Bayesian binomial GLMM) that agree to three decimal places. It also finds that aggregate reliability collapses on the hardest task quartile (Eρ² on τ² action_checks drops from 0.752 to 0.000), and training-cell reliability negatively correlates with held-out reliability (r = -0.90 on τ²).

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 13, 04:00

**Background**: Generalizability Theory (G-theory) is a statistical framework that decomposes observed-score variance into components attributable to multiple facets (e.g., tasks, raters) and their interactions, unlike classical test theory which treats error as a single component. Henderson's Method-I is a classic variance component estimation technique for unbalanced data, while REML and Bayesian GLMMs are modern alternatives. Agent leaderboards are commonly used to compare AI agents, but this paper suggests they may primarily reflect task-specific specialization rather than general capability.

## [Apodex Discovery: New Framework and Benchmarks for Discoverative AI](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.11341) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Apodex Discovery introduces a framework and benchmarks for evaluating and building discoverative AI, featuring a heavy-duty solver system and a problem-scouting process that surveyed 561 industries and selected 20 problems for initial release. It also presents HDS6, a new evaluation metric that assesses six dimensions independently of final-task success. This framework addresses a critical gap in AI evaluation by moving beyond predefined benchmarks toward verifiable investigations of real-world problems, potentially enabling AI to contribute to genuine scientific discovery. It could reshape how AI systems are developed and assessed, impacting researchers, industry practitioners, and anyone relying on AI for complex problem-solving. The framework includes a common environment-task-episode abstraction that provides data, tools, constraints, feedback, trajectory recording, and verification of intermediate artifacts and final submissions. In tests, Apodex surpassed the published state of the art in AAV capsid design by 7%, and in drug repurposing, it improved the mean normalized prediction score of GPT-5.5 and GPT-5.6-sol by 2.5 and 7.6 points, respectively.

The heavy-duty solver is a system comprising a foundation model, harness, tools, and control policies that pursues extended, stateful, verifiable investigations. This framework aims to address the challenge that real-world problems often lack executable or verifiable forms, unlike typical AI benchmarks.

## [Self-Consistency Backfires on Hard Science Problems for Small LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.11403) ⭐️ 8.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2608.11403) demonstrates that majority-vote self-consistency reduces per-problem accuracy on a majority of GPQA Diamond problems for two small instruction-tuned models: 56.6% for Qwen2.5-7B and 65.7% for Llama-3-8B. The effect was pre-registered and confirmed across four hypotheses. This finding challenges the common assumption that self-consistency is a low-risk accuracy boost, potentially influencing how practitioners allocate inference compute, especially for small models on hard reasoning tasks. It highlights the need for caution when applying majority voting to difficult, knowledge-intensive benchmarks. A grid oracle routing each problem to the best N (among 1,2,4,8,16,32,64) shows a theoretical upper bound 14 accuracy points above N=1 for Qwen and 17 for Llama, but no verifier-free gate (plurality-agreement or token-entropy) reaches it. The mechanism is that confidence does not track correctness on these problems; in the highest-agreement bin, the plurality answer is correct only about half the time for Qwen.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 13, 04:00

**Background**: Self-consistency is a widely used inference-time technique where a model samples multiple chains of thought and returns the plurality answer, typically assumed to improve accuracy. GPQA Diamond is a benchmark of 198 graduate-level science questions where PhD experts achieve 65% accuracy but non-experts only 34%, making it a challenging test for reasoning. The paper focuses on small instruction-tuned models and flags reasoning-native models as an open question.

## [Social Chain of Thought: Multi-Agent LLM Framework for Medical Differential Diagnosis](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.11420) ⭐️ 8.0/10

The paper introduces Social Chain of Thought (SCoT), a multi-round, multi-agent pipeline for medical differential diagnosis that structures collaborative LLM reasoning as a deliberative framework. Evaluations show SCoT outperforms single-agent baselines, one-agent pipeline ablations, and best-of-n scaling, particularly on the hardest diagnostic cases. This work addresses a high-impact use case—medical diagnosis—where LLMs are increasingly used, with over 5% of ChatGPT messages being healthcare-related. By demonstrating when and why multi-agent systems help, it provides a pathway to more transparent and reliable AI-assisted diagnosis, potentially improving patient outcomes and trust in AI healthcare tools. SCoT is evaluated against single-agent baselines, one-agent pipeline ablations, and best-of-n scaling, showing that its recall advantage is not reproduced by monolithic inference alone. The method is most successful in the hardest diagnostic cases, where multiple rounds of specialist conversation help recover ground-truth diagnoses and converge on a higher-recall differential.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 13, 04:00

**Background**: Differential diagnosis is a systematic process clinicians use to identify the most likely condition among several possibilities by comparing symptoms and test results with known disease patterns. Multi-agent systems in LLM reasoning represent a shift from optimizing individual model performance to orchestrating collaborative intelligence among multiple model instances, which can improve reasoning on complex tasks. Chain-of-thought prompting, a technique that elicits step-by-step reasoning, has been a key advancement in LLM reasoning, and SCoT extends this by structuring multi-agent interaction as a deliberative framework.

## [MobileJudgeBench: Benchmarking LLM Judges for Mobile Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.11434) ⭐️ 8.0/10

The paper introduces MobileJudgeBench, a benchmark with 931 human-annotated trajectories across 6 mobile agent benchmarks, 4 agent models, and 68 apps, to systematically evaluate LLM-as-judge methods. It finds that a simple baseline judge with sampled screenshots often outperforms purpose-built methods, and the LLM backbone is the primary driver of judge quality. This work addresses a critical gap in evaluating LLM-based judges for mobile agent benchmarks, providing a standardized benchmark and systematic analysis. The findings that simple baselines can outperform complex methods and that the LLM backbone is key will guide future development and selection of judges for mobile agent evaluation. The benchmark evaluates 6 judge methods, including five adapted from SPA-Bench, A3 with two modes, AndroidArena, and AgentRewardBench, plus a simple baseline, across multiple LLM backends. Benchmark quality metrics correlate with real-world judge utility, and failure analysis reveals opposite failure profiles (conservative vs. permissive) linked to the backbone's precision-recall characteristics.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 13, 04:00

**Background**: LLM-as-a-judge is a framework where large language models evaluate the outputs of other systems, commonly used for assessing LLM applications at scale. Mobile agent benchmarks increasingly rely on such judges to determine task completion, but their reliability on mobile trajectories has been largely unexamined. This paper provides a systematic evaluation to address that gap.

The paper introduces SABLE (Synthetically-accessible Agentic Bayesian Ligand Exploration), an open-source framework that uses natural-language orchestration via an LLM to guide multi-objective hit-to-lead optimization in drug discovery. It integrates reaction-templated analog enumeration, property prediction, affinity scoring, and Bayesian optimization into a modular workflow. SABLE addresses the complex, multi-objective nature of hit-to-lead optimization, potentially accelerating early-stage drug discovery by providing an extensible decision-support tool. Its open-source, modular design allows researchers to adapt it to their specific workflows, which could lower barriers to AI-driven medicinal chemistry. SABLE's architecture allows tools and characterization backends to be replaced by editing a config file without modifying operational logic. It provides provenance for each numerical output, and in single- and multi-objective studies, it enriches candidate sets while evaluating only a subset of the enumerated search space.

Bayesian optimization is a sample-efficient method for optimizing expensive black-box functions, and reaction-templated analog enumeration generates virtual libraries of synthetically accessible compounds. SABLE combines these techniques with LLM orchestration to automate and streamline the design-make-test-analyze cycle.

## [Refusal Behavior in LLMs Concentrated in MLP Layers](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.11583) ⭐️ 8.0/10

A new study on arXiv (2608.11583) demonstrates that refusal behavior in safety-aligned large language models is primarily encoded in MLP layers, with a mid-network concentration, rather than being distributed across the entire network. The findings are based on weight transplantation experiments across two open-weight model pairs and four safety benchmarks. This research challenges the common assumption that safety alignment is a distributed property, suggesting it is localized and interaction-sensitive. The findings could inform targeted safety interventions and explain why fine-tuning can easily break safety alignment, impacting AI safety and interpretability research. Replacing MLP parameters recovered at least 2.7 times more malicious-prompt refusal than replacing attention parameters. The block spanning layers 8-11 was selected first in all six greedy searches, and the composition of safety-relevant components was non-additive, with selective block subsets sometimes outperforming full MLP transplantation.

Researchers introduced EnterpriseRAG, a benchmark of 983 expert-validated samples across six domains, simulating retrieval noise, knowledge gaps, and factual conflicts. Evaluation of 13 state-of-the-art LLMs revealed that while models satisfy 80% of individual constraints, only 26.8% of responses meet all requirements simultaneously, exposing a 57-point orchestration gap. The benchmark systematically simulates three failure modes absent from prior work: retrieval noise, knowledge gaps, and factual conflicts, coupled with complex instructions. Critical findings show deep barriers under knowledge gaps and factual conflicts, even with reasoning-enhanced inference, indicating production RAG requires explicit context-aware protocols and calibrated judgment.

## [ForeWAM: Efficient World Action Model Without Future Video Decoding](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.11605) ⭐️ 8.0/10

ForeWAM introduces a dynamics-conditioned direct-policy World Action Model that provides predictive context for action generation without decoding future videos, using a novel Future-KV mechanism. It achieves 96.7% and 96.9% average success rates on LIBERO with standard and accelerated variants, respectively, and 61.6% on LIBERO-Plus. This work bridges the gap between explicit-future and direct-policy World Action Models, offering a more efficient alternative that retains predictive dynamics without the computational cost of video decoding. It could significantly improve the practicality of world models in real-time robot learning and control. The Future-KV mechanism performs a single Video DiT prefill over the current visual latent and stochastic future slots, reusing layer-wise key-value states during action denoising. Dynamics registers are supervised by a frozen latent action teacher, and ground-truth future observations and the teacher are only used during training, not deployment.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 13, 04:00

**Background**: World Action Models (WAMs) couple future visual prediction with robot action generation, enabling policies to model physical world evolution. Explicit-future WAMs provide direct access to predicted scene evolution but incur high inference costs from iterative video denoising, while direct-policy WAMs are efficient but lack an explicit interface for predictive dynamics. ForeWAM leverages a Video DiT prefill and KV cache reuse to provide predictive context efficiently, similar to techniques used in large language models.

## [XBridge: Decode-Free Protocol for Heterogeneous LLM Communication](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.11676) ⭐️ 8.0/10

XBridge introduces a decode-free communication protocol for heterogeneous LLM agents, using Lexical Anchor Mapping (LAM) and a Latent Enrichment Bridge (LEB) to preserve entity identity across different model architectures. It outperforms text-based communication on all seven tasks across Llama, Qwen, and Mistral pairs, with 11x lower latency. This work addresses the entity grounding problem in cross-architecture communication, a critical issue for heterogeneous multi-agent LLM systems. By enabling efficient latent-level transfer without architectural homogeneity, XBridge could significantly improve the performance and scalability of multi-agent systems in real-world applications. LEB requires only 264M trainable parameters (3.8% of the receiver), is trained on a small balanced sample set, and adds negligible inference overhead. In a same-architecture setting, XBridge also exceeds a KV-sharing baseline on six of seven tasks.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 13, 04:00

**Background**: Heterogeneous multi-agent LLM systems use different model families to reduce redundant reasoning patterns, but existing communication protocols either rely on text, losing internal representations, or require architectural homogeneity for latent transfer. Cross-attention bridges that transfer continuous representations suffer from rare-token compression collapse, where entity identity is lost (bridge-only F1 ~30%). XBridge combines discrete lexical anchors with latent enrichment to ground contextual signals to specific entities.

FrontierFinance, a fully open benchmark of 220 expert-crafted queries and 11,543 source-attributed rubrics, was introduced to evaluate AI agents across six use cases spanning the full investor workflow. Evaluations show that the tool harness strongly shapes performance, with Samaya's in-house system leading at 56.0%, ahead of the strongest frontier model (Claude Fable 5, 49.2%) at roughly 2.2x lower cost. This benchmark addresses a critical gap in evaluating AI agents for professional investment research, which previously focused on narrow data extraction tasks that models have largely saturated. By covering the full investor workflow, it provides a more realistic and challenging evaluation, potentially influencing future research and development in finance AI agents. The benchmark includes six use cases: Screening & Discovery, Sector, Industry & Macro, and others, with the hardest being Screening & Discovery and Sector, Industry & Macro, where even the best systems reach only 33% and 39%. The best open-weight model (Kimi K3, 46.4%) nearly matches the best proprietary model at 4.5x lower cost, and the dataset and grading code are publicly available.

## [Trait-Invariant Safety Tuning Stabilizes LLM Safety Across System Prompt Traits](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.11705) ⭐️ 8.0/10

The paper identifies trait-induced safety variation, where the same user request yields different safety decisions under different system prompt traits, and introduces Trait-Invariant Safety Tuning (TIST) with its instantiation Trait-Subspace Neutralization (TraSN) to mitigate this issue. This work addresses a critical robustness gap in LLM safety, showing that safety behavior can be inconsistent depending on system prompt traits, which is vital for reliable deployment. The proposed method improves safety consistency without sacrificing general capability, benefiting developers and users of LLM-based systems. The paper introduces two refusal-based metrics: Trait-Induced Deviation and Trait-Induced Flip Rate, and provides a representation-level analysis showing traits perturb safety representations within a low-dimensional subspace. TraSN enforces invariance only within this identified subspace, improving safety on harmful requests while preserving general capability across multiple open-weight LLMs.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 13, 04:00

**Background**: Large language models (LLMs) are aligned to refuse unsafe requests and comply with safe ones, but this behavior can be influenced by the system prompt, which sets the model's persona or traits. Trait-induced safety variation is a failure mode where different traits lead to inconsistent safety decisions for the same request. The paper aims to measure, understand, and reduce this variation to make LLM safety more robust.

## [Harness-IF Benchmark Reveals Coding Agents Often Follow Rules Coincidentally](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.11727) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Harness-IF, a new benchmark and metric (AP-Acc) for evaluating instruction following in coding agents, was introduced. It evaluates 12 frontier models across 60 realistic multi-turn coding items, showing accuracy spans 72.1-85.9% and AP-Acc 66.1-78.6%, with every model performing worse on against-prior rules. This benchmark addresses a critical gap in existing instruction-following evaluations by distinguishing genuine compliance from coincidental behavior. It provides a more accurate measure of coding agents' true instruction-following capabilities, which is essential for improving AI agent reliability and safety. The benchmark uses a 642-rule library, with 256 rules receiving verdicts, placed across five configurable instruction surfaces. A counterbalanced conflict pilot on nine separate builds found that pooled precedence does not follow prompt depth, with system prompts, project files, and user instructions ahead of tool and skill descriptions.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 13, 04:00

**Background**: Coding agents are AI systems that assist with programming tasks by following instructions. Existing benchmarks often concentrate rules in the user turn or emphasize final task success, failing to distinguish when an agent obeys a rule because it was already going to do so. Harness-IF introduces Against-Prior Accuracy (AP-Acc), which scores only rules that oppose unprompted defaults, by re-running tasks with the rule withheld.

## [Agent Skills Can Cause Failures: Empirical Study Identifies 307 Skill-Induced Failures](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.11888) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper presents a comprehensive empirical study and a differential analysis framework showing that agent skills can induce failures in LLM agents, identifying 307 skill-induced failures, including 125 functional failures and 182 efficiency regressions. This research challenges the assumption that agent skills are always beneficial, highlighting that they can be harmful and cause failures. It provides a systematic framework for attributing failures to skills, which is valuable for improving the safety and cost-effectiveness of LLM agent systems. The study introduces SkillTriage, a taxonomy-guided attribution tool that normalizes paired cases, extracts differential evidence, and produces triage reports. Key findings include that functional failures are rarely caused by obviously irrelevant skills, and efficiency regressions are not explained by prompt length alone.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 13, 04:00

**Background**: Agent skills are a mechanism for extending LLM agents with reusable guidance, shaping task execution including planning, tool use, and validation. Prior work reported mixed results, with some skills improving success rates while others have no effect or even reduce success rates. This paper addresses the gap by systematically attributing failures to specific skills.

A new arXiv paper (2608.11905) proposes a hybrid symbolic approach that combines formal logic, answer set solvers, and LLM-based fact extraction for policy-based reasoning. This method outperforms policy-as-prompt and policy-as-code methods in most cases, achieving about a 10x reduction in token usage. This work addresses a critical limitation of generative AI in policy compliance scenarios, offering a more accurate, robust, and interpretable alternative. The significant token efficiency gain could lead to cost savings and broader adoption in real-world applications like tax rules and airline baggage policies. The approach separates fact extraction (using LLMs) from reasoning (using an answer set solver), grounding predicates in formal logic. The paper reports that this separation outperforms policy-as-prompt and policy-as-code methods in most cases, with approximately a 10x reduction in token usage.

## [OEIS Open Benchmark: LMs Resolve 30-44% of Open Math Conjectures](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.11941) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Researchers introduced OEIS Open, a benchmark of 492 open mathematical conjectures from the OEIS, formalized in Lean. They found that generic language models with minimal tools can resolve 147 conjectures (30%) with a $50 budget per attempt, and the best model scores 44% on a 100-conjecture subset with a $200 budget. This benchmark demonstrates that language models can autonomously resolve open research conjectures at modest cost, which could accelerate mathematical discovery and reduce reliance on human experts. The findings challenge assumptions about the necessity of complex agent loops or literature access, informing future AI for mathematics research. The benchmark includes OEIS Open Lite, a random subset of 100 conjectures for cheaper evaluation. Giving LMs access to 476,000 arXiv papers did not improve performance, nor did using more sophisticated agent loops, suggesting that simple tool use is sufficient. The conjectures are of uncertain significance and likely received little prior attention.

**Background**: OEIS (On-Line Encyclopedia of Integer Sequences) is a database of integer sequences with many unproven conjectures. Lean is a proof assistant and functional programming language used for formal verification of mathematical theorems. Formalizing conjectures in Lean allows automated systems to attempt proofs in a verifiable manner.

## [Claim-Level Reliability Assessment Boosts Test-Time Reasoning Efficiency](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.11994) ⭐️ 8.0/10

The paper introduces Claim-Level Reliability Assessment (CLR), a training-free framework that reallocates test-time compute from additional solution sampling to targeted verification of decision-critical claims. CLR exploits the asymmetry between solution construction and claim refutation, improving accuracy across four LLMs and four reasoning benchmarks under matched budgets. This work addresses a key inefficiency in current test-time scaling approaches, which often waste compute on redundant sampling. By focusing on claim-level falsification, CLR offers a more efficient and potentially more accurate path to improving reasoning model performance, which could influence future research and applications in AI reasoning. CLR condenses each reasoning trace into a compact set of decision-critical claims, isolating logical anchors and avoiding signal dilution from routine tokens. It uses nonlinear reliability scoring to suppress erroneous consensus; for example, on GPT-OSS-20B/CMIMC25, CLR improves pass@1 by 27.15 percentage points and raises self-consistency accuracy from 77.50% to 82.19% while using 37.0% fewer tokens.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 13, 04:00

**Background**: Test-time scaling in large language models (LLMs) typically involves generating multiple solutions and selecting the best one, but this can be computationally expensive and inefficient. Verification-based approaches, such as self-consistency, often evaluate entire traces, which can dilute the impact of critical errors. CLR introduces a principle of claim-level falsification, where instead of constructing a full correct solution, the model focuses on finding a single decisive flaw in an incorrect claim, which is inherently easier. This approach is part of a broader trend in AI verification that emphasizes semantic analysis and targeted checks over exhaustive generation.

Mechanist is an agentic system that autonomously discovers the mechanisms underlying AI intelligence, integrating a knowledge graph of ~13,000 interpretability papers with a 43-million-paper database and a library of 32 foundational methods. It outperforms Claude Code and existing AI-scientist systems in generating valuable hypotheses and executing experiments reliably. This addresses a critical gap in AI interpretability by automating mechanistic discovery, which is essential for AI safety and control as models become more complex. It could shift how researchers study AI mechanisms, enabling faster and more scalable understanding of model behavior and risks. Mechanist demonstrated a progression from discovering model behaviors to explaining and controlling AI models, including uncovering a counterintuitive safety risk where unsafe traits transfer across modalities through safe training data. It also developed a mechanism theory of belief and translated insights into practical interventions, such as steering DNA sequence generation.

## [Graph-Structured Rubrics Improve LLM Judge Scoring](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.12097) ⭐️ 8.0/10

The paper introduces Graph-Structured Rubrics (GSR), which compiles a rubric into a response-independent typed evaluation graph before observing responses. Under GPT-OSS-120B, GSR improves exact score agreement by 0.62–6.75 percentage points over Prometheus-style scoring on four pointwise datasets and achieves the highest end-to-end pairwise accuracy on two preference benchmarks. This work addresses a practical limitation in LLM-as-a-judge evaluation by making criterion composition explicit and typed, leading to more consistent and reliable scoring. It could influence future evaluation frameworks and improve the trustworthiness of LLM-based assessments across the AI industry. GSR uses criterion nodes for judgments and transformation, reduction, and gating operators to compose them through named ports, with a task-specific Readout mapping the unique sink to a score or preference. Compilation rejects malformed or type-incompatible graphs, and the method supports both pointwise and pairwise evaluation modes.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 13, 04:00

**Background**: Rubric-based LLM evaluators typically treat rubrics as prompt context or flat criteria, leaving criterion composition implicit. GSR compiles rubrics into typed graphs to make the evaluation structure explicit and robust. The experiments use GPT-OSS-120B, an open-weight reasoning model from OpenAI released under the Apache 2.0 license.

## [LLM Rankings Shift with Token Budgets](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.12150) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper shows that LLM evaluation rankings are not stable across token generation budgets, with 3-19% of items exhibiting non-monotone accuracy and ranking reversals on all benchmarks. It also proposes budget-aware routing that captures 14.1% of the oracle gap cross-domain. This challenges the fundamental assumption of stable model rankings in LLM evaluation, which could affect how models are benchmarked and selected in practice. The findings suggest that budget-aware evaluation and routing could lead to more efficient and accurate model deployment. The study evaluated four models on three reasoning benchmarks with 56,476 inferences across seven budget levels (64-4,096 tokens). Non-monotone behavior was model-specific with low cross-model overlap (6-14%), and oracle analysis showed model complementarity up to +27.8 percentage points, most pronounced at constrained budgets.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 13, 04:00

**Background**: LLM evaluation typically assumes that model rankings are consistent regardless of inference settings, such as the maximum number of tokens generated. However, this paper varies the token generation budget and finds that rankings can reverse, suggesting that evaluation protocols should consider budget constraints. Budget-aware routing is a technique that dynamically selects models based on the available token budget to optimize performance and cost.

This paper provides the first systematic comparison of oracle budget-aware guidance methods for protein structure prediction, including FK-steering, DPO, Best K-of-N, and a novel extension of O3 to this domain. The evaluation on calmodulin (1CLL) and E. coli aspartate transcarbamoylase (9EEH) reveals that no single method dominates across all budgets and oracles. This work addresses a critical practical constraint in protein structure prediction: the high cost of biological oracles. By providing actionable recommendations, it helps researchers and practitioners choose the most effective guidance method under real-world budget limitations, potentially improving the reliability of predictions in drug discovery and protein engineering. The study benchmarks four methods: FK-steering, DPO, Best K-of-N, and O3, which applies off-the-shelf optimizers within a generative model's latent subspace. Results show that O3 is most effective at low oracle budgets, while FK-steering and DPO improve as the budget increases, but the evaluation is limited to only two protein targets.

Guidance methods like FK-steering (Feynman-Kac steering) and DPO (Direct Preference Optimization) aim to use this budget efficiently, but until now, no systematic comparison existed to guide method selection.

## [Agentic Workflow Converts GAMESS Fortran to Modern Form](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.12249) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper introduces an agentic workflow that successfully converted GAMESS's two-electron-integral routines from fixed-form Fortran 77 to free-form Fortran 2008, covering 12 source files, 56,448 lines, and 225 subroutines. The agents, operating as three Claude Code roles, achieved bit-for-bit reproduction of energies across 612 test runs with zero chemistry-relevant differences. This demonstrates a scalable approach to modernizing legacy HPC code, which is often left undone due to the sheer volume of transformations. The use of an exact verification oracle and version-controlled specifications could significantly accelerate modernization efforts in computational science and beyond. The workflow uses three prompt-specialized agent roles operating under a version-controlled specification authored by the agents themselves, with humans holding a small number of gates. The merge criterion was bit-for-bit reproduction of energies from GAMESS's standard test suite, where a deviation in the twelfth decimal place counts as failure. All 12 files passed a 51-test validation battery, including 49 standard GAMESS tests and two additional calculations.

**Background**: Legacy Fortran codebases, especially in computational chemistry, are often massive and difficult to modernize manually. Fixed-form Fortran 77, with its column-based syntax, is outdated compared to free-form Fortran 2008, which offers better readability and modern features. GAMESS is a mature quantum-chemistry package with a 48-year development history, and its two-electron-integral routines are critical for computing electron repulsion integrals.

## [VAKRA Benchmark Reveals AI Agents' Weakness in Multi-Hop Reasoning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.12282) ⭐️ 8.0/10

IBM researchers introduced VAKRA, a benchmark with over 8,000 executable APIs across 62 domains, to evaluate multi-hop reasoning and retrieval in AI agents under tool-use policies. The benchmark reveals that even the best models achieve only 70.4% on single-hop tasks and drop to 50-51% on compositional tasks, with severe failures on policy-constrained questions. This benchmark addresses a critical gap in evaluating enterprise AI agents, which must reason across both structured APIs and document collections. The findings highlight significant limitations in current models' compositional reasoning and policy adherence, guiding future research and development in agent architectures. The benchmark includes three settings of increasing difficulty: diverse API interaction styles, multi-hop reasoning over structured APIs, and multi-source reasoning with natural-language tool-use policy constraints. Correctness is verified by re-executing predicted tool calls against live APIs, and a fixed ReAct harness is used to isolate model capabilities from agent architecture.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 13, 04:00

**Background**: Multi-hop reasoning in AI agents involves connecting disparate pieces of information to answer complex queries, often requiring multiple steps of tool calls or retrieval. Tool-use policies govern how agents can access and use APIs, ensuring security and compliance. The ReAct harness is a common framework that interleaves reasoning and acting, allowing agents to call tools and update context iteratively.

## [Retrofitting Recurrent Depth into Pretrained Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.11233) ⭐️ 8.0/10

The paper demonstrates that a pretrained language model, Qwen2.5-0.5B-Instruct, can be retrofitted with a weight-tied recurrent block, achieving comparable performance to full fine-tuning while using fewer trained parameters. The retrofit installs at two parameter budgets (6M and 180M) and enables step-wise computation with intermediate supervision. This work offers a practical, minimally invasive method to enhance reasoning flexibility and efficiency of existing LLMs without full fine-tuning. It could reduce computational costs and enable deeper reasoning in latent space, impacting model adaptation and deployment. The recurrent model matched the full block overall (83.8% vs 84.0%), led through depth 11, and trailed beyond. It extrapolated to ~1.5 times its supervised depth, holding 70% accuracy through depth 18, and retained 53% vs 2.5% beyond depth 10, answering 7.6 times faster than a scratchpad-trained model.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 13, 04:00

**Background**: Depth-recurrent language models loop a shared recurrent block to decouple effective depth from parameter count, enabling flexible compute scaling. Retrofitting involves converting existing pretrained non-recurrent models into depth-recurrent models through continued pretraining, leveraging pretrained weights and reusing architectural components.

## [Compaction Drops Session Constraints; COMPINT Suite and SC-Aware Extractor Proposed](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.11242) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper identifies that context compaction in LLMs systematically drops session constraints (SCs) such as 'do not delete any emails until I confirm.' It introduces COMPINT, an evaluation suite covering multi-turn chat, agentic trajectory, and long-horizon research, and proposes a plug-and-play SC-aware extractor that achieves over 90% SC retention across all scenarios. This research highlights a critical reliability issue in long-context LLM applications, where user instructions meant to persist across a session are silently lost during compaction. The proposed solution and evaluation suite provide a practical path to improve the trustworthiness of LLM systems in real-world deployments. Current compactors retain only 17% of injected SCs on average, and most perform worse than running the same task without compaction. Retention varies with compactor, prompt, context length, SC phrasing, and injection location, indicating the loss is systematic. The SC-aware extractor works alongside the compactor without modifying it or the LLM.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 13, 04:00

**Background**: Context compaction is a technique used in LLM systems to reduce the size of a long conversation or context by summarizing or discarding less important parts, allowing the model to continue tasks within its context window limits. Session constraints are user-issued instructions that should govern the model's behavior for the remainder of a session, but they are often not explicitly prioritized during compaction, leading to their loss.

## [Agent Safety Should Be a Runtime Contract](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.11274) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper argues that AI agent safety should be enforced as a runtime contract with preventive and evidential measures, backed by surveys and audits of real incidents. This is significant because it challenges the dominant training-time safety paradigm and proposes a complementary runtime approach, which could improve safety for autonomous agents in real-world deployments. The paper includes a survey of 52 documented AI-agent and LLM safety incidents, a false-completion audit with 31 core cases, a trajectory-schema audit of 12 public agent systems, and a title-level audit of 28,560 papers showing an 8-12x imbalance between training-time and deployment-time publications.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 13, 04:00

**Background**: AI safety is traditionally instilled during model training via methods like RLHF, DPO, or Constitutional AI. However, for autonomous agents that execute code and modify systems, runtime enforcement is necessary. The paper draws parallels with computer security and experimental sciences, which use runtime contracts with preventive and evidential elements.

## [LLMs as Explainable Validators for Metal-Organic Frameworks via Chemical Textualization](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.11283) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper demonstrates that large language models (LLMs) can serve as interpretable validators of metal-organic framework (MOF) structures when crystallographic information is converted into chemically meaningful text. By benchmarking nine descriptors, the authors found that fine-tuned LLMs using the specialized mof2text descriptor achieve performance comparable to graph-based models in identifying unreasonable MOFs. This work addresses a critical bottleneck in high-throughput screening of MOFs by providing an explainable validation method that can identify chemically unreasonable structures. It bridges AI and materials science, offering a practical tool that not only classifies but also generates diagnostic rationales, potentially accelerating the curation of reliable MOF databases. The study benchmarks nine descriptors and finds that successful LLM-based validation depends on organizing local coordination, framework connectivity, and chemical context into a linguistically learnable representation, not just the amount of structural information. Fine-tuned LLMs using mof2text also generate diagnostic rationales for likely error sources, including abnormal bonding, connectivity, and charge states, as well as error-category predictions for annotated datasets.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 13, 04:00

**Background**: Metal-organic frameworks (MOFs) are porous crystalline materials with high surface areas and tunable properties, making them promising for applications like gas storage and catalysis. Computation-ready MOF databases are essential for high-throughput screening, but many reported structures are chemically unreasonable or disordered, compromising simulation fidelity. Traditional validation methods often rely on heuristic rules or offer limited interpretability. This paper leverages LLMs, which have shown strong capabilities in understanding and generating text, by converting crystallographic data into text descriptors that LLMs can process.

## [Backdoor Decontamination Dynamics in LLM Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.11295) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper introduces a framework for studying backdoor decontamination in tool-calling LLM agents, showing that defensive poisoning followed by unlearning can effectively erase most unknown backdoors. Across 115 experiments, defensive poisoning alone erased around 56% of original backdoors, and subsequent decontamination drove almost all survivors to erasure. This work addresses a novel and critical security challenge in LLM agents, providing a systematic framework and empirical evidence for a practical defense strategy. The findings have significant implications for AI safety and robustness, especially as open-weight agents become more widely deployed. The study uses the AgentDyn benchmark and decouples trigger, response, teacher, and fine-tuning method across systematic experiments. Notably, malicious backdoors never persist when using different triggers of the same general type as the defensive backdoor followed by unlearning, and co-installing up to four backdoors increases resistance, yet decontaminating a single known co-resident backdoor clears 87% of co-residents. Visualization with J-lens shows that traces of original trigger awareness persist at intermediate layers despite restored benign responses.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 13, 04:00

**Background**: Open-weight LLM agents are vulnerable to backdoors installed during fine-tuning, which may remain undetectable if trigger conditions are never met during testing. Defenders who do not know the existing trigger cannot unlearn it directly, so one strategy is to install a known backdoor (defensive poisoning) and then unlearn it, hoping the original unknown backdoor is removed as a side effect. This paper systematically studies the outcomes of this decontamination strategy, revealing that trigger recognition and malicious execution are behaviorally dissociable.

## [Self-Evolving Network Verifiers Automate Model Maintenance](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.11340) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper introduces a counterexample-guided loop where a coding agent proposes extensions to a symbolic network verifier's encoding, and an oracle (e.g., emulated routers) provides ground-truth routing state, enabling the verifier to automatically evolve to match actual router behavior. A prototype taught a 3,000-line SMT-based verifier three new features—OSPF areas, BGP route reflection, and L3VPN over EVPN—converging on models that match the oracle and even noticing vendor-specific behavior. This approach addresses the significant manual maintenance burden of symbolic network verifiers, which currently require experts to hand-encode protocols and features. By automating model growth, it could make network verification more accessible and practical for a wider range of networks, potentially improving network reliability and security. The system uses a counterexample-guided loop, where the coding agent iteratively refines the network model based on disagreements with the oracle. The prototype successfully learned OSPF areas, BGP route reflection, and L3VPN over EVPN, demonstrating the feasibility of the approach, though it is a research preprint with limited community discussion yet.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 13, 04:00

**Background**: Symbolic network verifiers use symbolic execution to reason about network correctness across many routing inputs and failures, but they require experts to manually encode protocols and features. This manual encoding is difficult to maintain because vendor implementations deviate from RFCs and behavior changes with software releases. The paper proposes using the router software itself as the source of truth, with an oracle (e.g., emulated routers) providing ground-truth routing state, and a coding agent (likely an LLM-based agent) to propose model extensions. This is similar to counterexample-guided inductive synthesis (CEGIS), where a synthesizer proposes candidates and a verifier provides counterexamples.
