## [Structural Silence: AI Infrastructure Fails Underrepresented Languages](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.12278) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper identifies four interlocking structural barriers in AI infrastructure that disadvantage underrepresented languages, using Bengali as a case study. These include a severe web presence gap, a 67:1 training-token deficit, a tokenization penalty from Bengali's alphasyllabary script, and connectivity exclusion in rural areas. This research highlights that dataset scarcity is a structural barrier, not just a technical limitation, affecting billions of speakers of underrepresented languages. It calls for offline-first design as an equity-oriented infrastructure strategy, which could reshape how AI systems are built and evaluated for global inclusivity. The paper reports that Bengali accounts for less than 0.5% of global web content despite representing nearly 4% of the global population. It also notes a 67:1 training-token deficit between English and Bengali in major multilingual corpora, and a tokenization penalty due to Bengali's alphasyllabary script, which increases token fertility. Additionally, individual internet penetration is 36.5% in rural areas versus 71.4% in urban areas.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 13, 04:00

**Background**: AI tools for education and language support are often promoted as scalable solutions for under-resourced communities, but the underlying infrastructure—training corpora, tokenization schemes, evaluation benchmarks, and deployment architectures—can systematically disadvantage speakers of underrepresented languages before a model is even trained. Tokenization is a process that breaks text into smaller units (tokens) for processing by language models; languages with complex scripts like Bengali's alphasyllabary (abugida) may require more tokens per word, increasing computational cost and reducing efficiency. The web presence gap refers to the underrepresentation of certain languages in online content, which limits the availability of training data for AI models. Offline-first design is an approach that prioritizes functionality without continuous internet connectivity, which is crucial for low-connectivity environments.

This paper presents a systematic review of 57 CAM-based visual explanation methods published since 2016, organizing them into a taxonomy across CNN, transformer, and foundation-model paradigms. It highlights the shift from single-class, low-resolution explanations to comparative, multi-layer, token-aware, and foundation-model-aware approaches. This review provides a comprehensive, method-centered overview of CAM in explainable AI, which is crucial for researchers navigating the rapidly evolving field. By identifying gaps and trends, it can guide future research and help standardize evaluation protocols. The taxonomy categorizes methods by attribution mechanism, architectural dependence, and evaluation objective. The review notes that evaluation remains fragmented, with faithfulness, localization, robustness, computational cost, and human trust measured using different protocols.

## [Test-Time Strong-to-Weak Capability Transfer via Harnesses](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.12307) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper introduces strong-to-weak scaffolding, a method where a stronger builder model constructs inference-time harnesses to boost a weaker target model's performance without any parameter updates. On four Theory-of-Mind benchmarks, the approach nearly doubled average accuracy from 0.49 to 0.91. This work challenges the conventional reliance on training-time distillation, showing that capability transfer can happen at inference time. It offers a practical, cost-effective way to enhance weaker models, which could be especially valuable in enterprise settings where retraining is expensive or infeasible. The harnesses work by offloading unstable model reasoning into deterministic code, using benchmark-specific routing, and enforcing strict answer formats, rather than encouraging more extensive reasoning or broader sampling. The study also found that builder-model reasoning effort improves harness quality monotonically, and weaker target models benefit the most.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 13, 04:00

**Background**: Knowledge distillation traditionally transfers capabilities from large models to smaller ones by updating the smaller model's parameters during training. This paper explores an alternative: test-time capability transfer, where a stronger model builds inference-time scaffolds (harnesses) to guide a weaker model without retraining. Theory-of-Mind benchmarks assess a model's ability to infer beliefs, desires, and emotions, which is a challenging task for current LLMs.

The authors support their claims with a human evaluation study on nine existing benchmarks. This critique is significant because it challenges the validity of many AI benchmarks used to claim human-level performance, which could mislead research directions and public perception. The proposed recommendations could lead to more rigorous and meaningful evaluation of AI models, benefiting the AI/ML community and related fields. The paper identifies three key shortcomings: lack of human-validated labels, inadequate representation of human response variability and uncertainty, and reliance on simplified and ecologically invalid tasks. The human evaluation study on nine benchmarks revealed major limitations in task and label designs, supporting the need for the five proposed recommendations.

## [DREAMS: Hierarchical Multi-Agent Framework for Trustworthy DFT Simulations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2507.14267) ⭐️ 8.0/10

DREAMS introduces a hierarchical multi-agent framework for density functional theory (DFT) simulations, featuring a multi-tier safety guard that applies deterministic checks and scoped LLM judgment to verify every value and trace its provenance. It achieves average errors below 1% on the Sol27LC lattice-constant benchmark and reproduces expert-level adsorption-energy differences on the CO/Pt(111) puzzle. This work addresses a critical issue of LLM agent reliability in scientific workflows, where agents can produce plausible yet invalid results. By ensuring trustworthiness through verification, DREAMS paves the way for high-throughput autonomous materials simulation, potentially accelerating materials discovery and reducing human oversight. The safety guard operates at tool-call time, rejecting fabricated, laundered, or unsourced values, and at report time, where a judge audits the full provenance graph. The guarded system verifies every essential step at approximately 13 times the input tokens compared to its unguarded counterpart, and verification layers can be disabled individually to balance trustworthiness against cost.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 13, 04:00

**Background**: Density functional theory (DFT) is a quantum mechanical modeling method used to investigate the electronic structure of many-body systems, widely applied in materials science. Large language model (LLM) agents can automate long-horizon scientific workflows, but their outputs are often unreliable due to context loss and hallucination. Hierarchical multi-agent systems organize agents into layered structures where higher-level agents delegate subtasks to lower-level ones, improving coordination and error reduction. Provenance graphs record the lineage of data, showing how entities, activities, and agents influenced a piece of data, which is essential for auditing scientific results.

A new arXiv paper (2507.22423) proposes a formal, species-agnostic definition of intelligence based on entity fidelity, formalized as ε-concept intelligence, where an agent is ε-intelligent if no admissible distinguisher can separate generated entities from original exemplars beyond tolerance ε. This definition could provide a unified framework for evaluating and comparing diverse AI paradigms, from reinforcement learning to generative models, and has implications for AGI evaluation and safety. It offers a concrete, testable criterion that may help guide the development and assessment of increasingly capable AI systems. The paper formalizes the intuition as ε-concept intelligence, requiring a chosen admissible distinguisher and a tolerance ε. It outlines empirical protocols for testing this definition across different intelligent behaviors, and discusses implications for evaluation, safety, and generalization.

The concept of 'fidelity' in this context draws on the idea of faithfulness or exactness, as seen in dictionary definitions, but here it is applied to the ability to generate entities that match exemplars of a concept.

Researchers introduced SteeringSafety, a benchmark for evaluating representation steering methods across nine safety perspectives and 18 datasets, with modular implementations of DIM, ACE, CAA, PCA, and LAT. Results on Gemma-2-2B, Llama-3.1-8B, and Qwen-2.5-7B show that steering performance varies by method-model-perspective pairing and that all methods exhibit significant entanglement. This benchmark addresses a critical gap in evaluating safety interventions in LLMs, providing a systematic framework to measure entanglement across multiple safety perspectives. The findings highlight the need to evaluate steering methods through multiple safety angles, which is valuable for the AI safety community and future research on alignment and interpretability. The benchmark includes nine safety perspectives such as refusal, bias, hallucination, social behaviors, reasoning, epistemic integrity, and normative judgment. Key findings include that social behaviors are most vulnerable (degradation up to 76%), refusal steering can compromise normative judgment by up to 26%, and hallucination steering shifts political views unpredictably across models (from 25% right to 28% left).

## [Credo: Declarative Control of LLM Pipelines via Beliefs and Policies](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.14401) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Credo introduces a database-backed semantic control plane that represents LLM pipeline state as high-level beliefs and regulates behavior through declarative policies, enabling adaptive, auditable, and composable execution without modifying pipeline code. This addresses critical issues of opacity and brittleness in agentic AI systems, offering a more robust and verifiable approach to managing long-lived, stateful decision-making. It could influence how future LLM pipelines are designed, particularly in domains requiring dynamic adaptation and auditability. The framework is showcased in a decision-control scenario where beliefs and policies declaratively guide critical execution choices such as model selection, retrieval, and corrective re-execution. This design supports dynamic behavior without requiring changes to the underlying pipeline code.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 13, 04:00

**Background**: Agentic AI systems often rely on imperative control loops, ephemeral memory, and prompt-embedded logic, making them opaque and brittle. Credo proposes a declarative alternative by representing semantic state as beliefs and using policies to regulate behavior, backed by a database for persistence and auditability.

<a id="item-119"></a>
## [Moxia: Trust-First Neuro-Symbolic Architecture for Self-Explaining Math Reasoning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.00671) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Moxia (formerly AXIOM) is a new neuro-symbolic architecture that uses LLMs only as canonicalizers to rewrite natural-language math problems into a narrow schema, which is then processed by a deterministic Computer Algebra System (CAS) pipeline that derives and verifies answers or abstains. The system ships with 4,783 routes, answers 90.2% of the MATH test split with only one confident-wrong answer, and achieves 89.2% on held-out MATH-500 with zero confident-wrong answers. This work addresses the critical need for trustworthy AI in mathematical reasoning by ensuring answers are derived through deterministic computation rather than generated by a language model, thus providing verifiable and self-explaining outputs. The approach could influence future AI systems in domains where correctness and explainability are paramount, such as education, scientific research, and formal verification. The system uses a 1:1:1 alignment of problem-shape regex, schema-specific prompt, and closed-form CAS handler, with 71% of routes answering without invoking the LLM. Derivations can be exported to Lean 4, with 445 out of 479 task files accepted by the Lean kernel with Mathlib, though this gate covers a fixture corpus and live output is generated, not machine-checked. The rule-only path answers the 20,000-record lm-eval arithmetic benchmark at 100% with 1 ms per record.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 13, 04:00

**Background**: Neuro-symbolic AI combines neural networks with symbolic reasoning to leverage the strengths of both, aiming for more robust and explainable systems. A computer algebra system (CAS) is software that manipulates mathematical expressions symbolically, enabling exact computation and verification. Lean 4 is a proof assistant and functional programming language used for formal verification of mathematical theorems. Moxia's design aligns with the neuro-symbolic cycle, where neural components are used for perception and symbolic components for reasoning, ensuring trustworthiness.

## [LiteOdyssey: PIHF Boosts Rare Disease Diagnosis Accuracy](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.16149) ⭐️ 8.0/10

The paper introduces liteOdyssey, an agentic AI system that uses Policy Iteration with Human Feedback (PIHF) to improve rare disease diagnosis accuracy. It ranked the correct disease first in 59.3% of 1,243 benchmark cases, up from 26.5% without the policy, matching state-of-the-art systems with a lower deployment footprint. This work demonstrates that expert reasoning can be converted into a scalable AI capability through a governed learning process, addressing the scarcity of expert knowledge in rare disease diagnosis. It offers a path to deployable AI systems that remain under clinician control and generalize to unseen diseases, potentially improving diagnostic accuracy in clinical settings. The policy was developed via PIHF, an in-context policy-learning method adapted from generalized policy iteration in reinforcement learning, where model failures and expert corrections consolidate into an explicit, clinician-gated policy. The policy transferred without modification across closed- and open-weight models, and ablations showed gains exceeded automated prompting improvement or source access alone.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 13, 04:00

**Background**: Rare disease diagnosis often relies on scarce expert reasoning, and off-the-shelf large language models (LLMs) rank the correct disease first in only 35.4% of benchmark cases. Reinforcement learning from human feedback (RLHF) is a training technique that aligns model behavior with human preferences, but PIHF adapts generalized policy iteration to learn policies in-context without gradient updates, enabling expert knowledge to be captured and transferred.

This work shifts the focus from capability-driven development to deployment-driven requirements, addressing critical gaps such as contamination-resistant benchmarks and interactive training environments. The survey consolidates over 300 references, with emphasis on advances from 2025 to 2026, and formalizes medical agents as sequential decision-making systems under partial observability with six cognitive modules. Researchers introduced UNICON (UNified In-Context Operator Networks), a foundation model that learns predictive relations from graph-based examples and applies them across scientific and social disciplines without retraining. It approaches specialist performance and, when combined with language-model agents for contextual ensemble learning (CEL), surpasses state-of-the-art specialists in unseen disciplines. This work addresses a significant gap beyond language models by providing a foundation model for numerical intelligence, which is crucial for scientific and social modeling. Its cross-disciplinary generalization could accelerate discovery and enable AI systems to reason about numerical systems in a retrain-free manner, impacting fields like physics, economics, and epidemiology. UNICON uses graph-based examples as context to infer predictive relations, and training-corpus diversity improves generalization to unseen disciplines. The paper is an arXiv preprint (2607.28432v3) with a concise abstract, but lacks detailed evaluation metrics, so further validation is needed.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 13, 04:00

**Background**: Foundation models are large-scale machine learning models trained on broad data, capable of adapting to various tasks via techniques like in-context learning. In-context learning allows models to infer task-relevant knowledge from examples provided at inference time without weight updates. Operator learning is a paradigm in scientific machine learning that approximates operators (e.g., solution operators of PDEs) using neural networks. UNICON builds on these concepts to achieve numerical intelligence across disciplines.

The paper introduces a method to construct physically-grounded anomaly labels for the Volve field dataset, validated against engineering documents, and evaluates a dual-head model for anomaly detection and classification. The dataset, labels, reasoning, baseline scores, trained model, and code are released publicly under CC-BY-NC-SA 4.0. This work addresses a critical gap in anomaly detection for real-world industrial data, where fault logs are often absent. By releasing grounded labels and a baseline, it provides a valuable benchmark for the community and demonstrates that labels can be learnable, potentially improving reliability in industrial monitoring. The dual-head model marks both event occurrence and event type, and is tested on unseen wells, recovering event presence and type well but locating events only roughly. The unsupervised baseline, which never sees labels, lands on the same regions as the rule-based labels, indicating the labels are not arbitrary.

## [Flow-by-Flow: Bypassing Content Judgment for AI Output Governance](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.07474) ⭐️ 8.0/10

The paper introduces Flow-by-Flow, a governance paradigm that controls supervisory load without evaluating content, and proves that if V×L grows at any positive compound rate while supervisory capacity grows linearly, exceedance occurs in finite time. A Monte Carlo analysis across 1,000 parameter draws confirms the ordering survives the 30-year horizon in 90.8% of trials. This work challenges the assumption that capacity investment alone can solve AI oversight, offering a theoretical framework that could reshape AI safety strategies in high-loss domains. It provides a formal basis for flow control as a distinct and more effective intervention, potentially influencing governance design for autonomous systems. The paper identifies three components of cognitive load—triage, judgment, and response—that respond asymmetrically to capability improvement, with only judgment cost facing downward pressure. Four design invariants characterize admissible exceedance pathways: no content judgment, no scalable consumption of examiner capacity, identity-bound per-application friction, and no batch clearance; a reference implementation satisfying all four is presented.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 13, 04:00

**Background**: Human-in-the-loop oversight becomes structurally untenable when AI output velocity exceeds human cognitive capacity, but the operative constraint is V×L, where L is per-item cognitive load. Prior work focused on capacity limits, but this paper argues that flow control—pricing supervisory load without content evaluation—is a more effective intervention. The concept draws on cognitive load theory and parallels with patent system fees, which serve as precursors to flow control.

The paper introduces Combodied Agents, a human-centered paradigm that models and supports individual human-state trajectories over time by integrating digital and embodied actions. It proposes a closed-loop framework with event-based multimodal perception, longitudinal memory, Personal World Models, and an admissible intervention policy. This paradigm addresses a structural gap in agentic AI, where digital agents transform software states and embodied agents transform physical states, but neither focuses on the evolving human state. It could significantly impact personal assistants, health AI, and human-AI interaction by shifting the goal from task completion to sustained human benefit. The framework uses purpose-bounded, uncertainty-aware, user-correctable representations rather than requiring an exhaustive Human Digital Twin. It organizes the design space by human-state targets, relational contexts, and agent roles, and proposes scenario-centered evaluation, agency-preservation metrics, and edge-native personal models.

## [PAC-MAP: Recasting MAP Inference as Best Arm Identification](https://arxiv.org/abs/2601.16083) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper introduces a novel approach that recasts maximum a posteriori (MAP) inference as a best arm identification problem in multi-armed bandits, and presents PAC algorithms with provable optimality in both fixed-confidence and fixed-budget settings. The algorithms are implemented using probabilistic circuits and graphical models. This work bridges probabilistic inference and multi-armed bandits, offering a new theoretical framework that could lead to more efficient and guaranteed MAP solvers. It has potential implications for AI/ML and systems research, as MAP inference is fundamental to many applications. The PAC-MAP solvers provide provably optimal solutions in both fixed-confidence and fixed-budget regimes, and can be used standalone or to improve existing heuristics. They characterize tractability using information-theoretic measures estimated from finite samples, and experiments on benchmarks confirm their benefits.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 13, 04:00

**Background**: Maximum a posteriori (MAP) inference aims to find the most probable assignment of variables in a probabilistic model, but it is generally intractable. Multi-armed bandits is a framework for sequential decision-making under uncertainty, where the goal is to identify the best arm with high confidence. PAC (Probably Approximately Correct) learning provides guarantees on the accuracy and confidence of algorithms. This paper connects these areas by treating MAP as a best arm identification problem.

<ul>

<a id="item-132"></a>
## [Almost Orthogonal Features Enable Isolated Interventions in Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.04718) ⭐️ 8.0/10

The paper proposes constraining internal features in language models to be almost orthogonal, formalizing the problem of feature interference and providing theoretical upper bounds on its propagation. It introduces an explicit orthogonality regularization on the feature dictionary and demonstrates empirically that this enables more isolated interventions on mathematical reasoning concepts while preserving model performance. This work addresses a core challenge in mechanistic interpretability: feature entanglement causes unintended side-effects during interventions. By promoting modular representations, it could significantly improve the reliability of causal interventions in language models, benefiting interpretability research and downstream applications that rely on model editing. The method is grounded in the Independent Causal Mechanisms principle and relates the discrepancy between idealized and realized interventions to the self-coherence of the feature dictionary. The code is available on GitHub, and the empirical validation focuses on mathematical reasoning concepts.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 13, 04:00

**Background**: Mechanistic interpretability assumes that concepts in language models are represented as linear features in activation space. For reliable interventions, manipulating one feature should not affect others, but feature entanglement often causes interference. The Independent Causal Mechanisms principle suggests that modular representations are more amenable to causal intervention. Orthogonality regularization encourages features to be nearly orthogonal, reducing interference.

## [Post-Training with Policy Gradients: Optimality and Base Model Barrier](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.06957) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper provides a theoretical analysis of post-training linear autoregressive models with policy gradients, proving minimax optimality under margin conditions and revealing a barrier when extending beyond the base model's support. This work addresses a timely and important topic in AI alignment and RLHF, offering rigorous results that could influence future post-training methods and highlight fundamental limitations of current approaches. The paper proves that a variant of policy gradient can achieve likelihood 1-ε with an essentially minimax optimal number of reward queries O~( (α^{-1}+ε^{-1})/γ^2 ) on test samples where the base model has non-trivial likelihood α. However, it shows that going beyond the base model's support may require exponential reward queries in sequence length N, unless process rewards are used.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 13, 04:00

**Background**: Post-training of large language models often uses reinforcement learning, particularly policy gradient methods, to align models with human preferences. The margin condition is a concept from statistical learning theory that ensures separability of classes, and minimax optimality refers to achieving the best possible worst-case performance. This paper extends these concepts to sequence prediction and analyzes the sample complexity of policy gradient post-training.

## [CoRe: Representation Finetuning for Continual Learning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.11201) ⭐️ 8.0/10

The paper introduces CoRe (Continual Representation Learning), a novel framework that shifts continual learning finetuning from weight space to representation space by performing task-specific interventions in a low-rank linear subspace of hidden representations. This provides explicit control over representation drift, addressing catastrophic forgetting while maintaining parameter efficiency. CoRe introduces a new paradigm for continual learning, offering a more interpretable and effective alternative to existing weight-level PEFT methods. It could significantly impact how pre-trained models are adapted to dynamic data streams, benefiting applications in robotics, personalization, and online learning. CoRe constrains updates to a low-rank subspace, achieving exceptional parameter efficiency. Extensive experiments across multiple continual learning benchmarks show that CoRe outperforms existing state-of-the-art methods while preserving parameter efficiency.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 13, 04:00

**Background**: Continual learning aims to enable models to adapt to evolving data streams without forgetting previously learned tasks. Catastrophic forgetting is a major challenge, where neural networks abruptly lose old knowledge when learning new information. Parameter-Efficient Fine-Tuning (PEFT) methods, such as LoRA, adapt pre-trained models by updating a small number of parameters, but they often lack explicit control over representation drift. CoRe addresses this by operating in representation space, leveraging low-rank linear subspaces to intervene in hidden representations.

## [VLM2Rec Framework Tackles Modality Collapse in Multimodal Sequential Recommendation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.17450) ⭐️ 8.0/10

The paper introduces VLM2Rec, a framework that adapts Vision-Language Models (VLMs) as collaborative filtering-aware embedders for multimodal sequential recommendation, addressing the modality collapse issue that arises during standard contrastive supervised fine-tuning. It proposes Weak-modality Penalized Contrastive Learning and cross-modal Relational Topology Regularization to promote balanced modality utilization. This work addresses a critical yet underexplored problem in multimodal sequential recommendation: modality collapse in VLM embedders, which can degrade recommendation accuracy. By providing a solution that balances modality utilization, VLM2Rec could improve the robustness and accuracy of recommendation systems that leverage rich multimodal data, benefiting both researchers and practitioners in the field. VLM2Rec uses Weak-modality Penalized Contrastive Learning to mitigate gradient imbalance during optimization and cross-modal Relational Topology Regularization to preserve geometric consistency between modalities. Experiments show consistent improvements over strong baselines in both accuracy and robustness across diverse scenarios.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 13, 04:00

**Background**: Multimodal sequential recommendation (SR) integrates sequential user interactions with heterogeneous item features such as images and text. Traditional SR often relies on small frozen pretrained encoders, limiting semantic capacity and preventing collaborative filtering (CF) signals from being fully integrated. Vision-Language Models (VLMs) offer high-capacity embeddings, but adapting them via contrastive supervised fine-tuning can amplify inherent modality imbalance, leading to modality collapse. This paper builds on prior work on modality collapse in VLMs and multimodal SR to propose a solution.

## [Automated Framework Exposes and Mitigates LLM System Instruction Leakage via Encoding Attacks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.01039) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper introduces an automated evaluation framework that tests whether LLM system instructions remain confidential when extraction requests are re-framed as encoding or structured output tasks. Across seven common models and 46 verified system instructions, the framework observed high attack success rates (>0.7) for structured serialization, and demonstrated a mitigation strategy based on one-shot instruction reshaping using a Chain-of-Thought reasoning model. System instruction leakage is a critical security risk highlighted in the OWASP Top 10 for LLM Applications, as these instructions may contain sensitive information like API credentials and internal policies. This research provides a practical, automated method to evaluate and harden LLM systems against such attacks, which is especially relevant for agentic AI applications that rely on refusal-based instructions. The framework uses a judge model to evaluate whether the target model's response leaks the original system instruction when given an encoding-based attack prompt. The mitigation strategy involves reshaping the system instruction using a Chain-of-Thought reasoning model, which significantly reduces attack success rate without requiring model retraining.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 13, 04:00

**Background**: System instructions in LLMs are used to enforce safety policies, define agent behavior, and protect sensitive operational context. Many applications rely on refusal-based instructions that block direct requests for system instructions, but this research shows that re-framing requests as encoding or structured output tasks can bypass these refusals. The OWASP Top 10 for LLM Applications identifies system prompt leakage as a critical risk, and this work addresses that vulnerability.

The paper introduces Temper-5400, a dataset of 5,400 semantically verified emotion-neutral pairs, and shows that emotional framing degrades LLM quantitative reasoning accuracy by 2-10 percentage points, with neutralization recovering most performance. This finding is significant for real-world LLM deployment, where user queries often carry emotional tone, and highlights a robustness gap that could affect reliability in applications like tutoring or customer support. It also provides a lightweight mitigation (neutralization) and a new benchmark for studying stylistic robustness. The study evaluated 18 models ranging from 1B to frontier scale across GSM8K, MultiArith, and ARC-Challenge. ## [DORA Explorer: Training-Free Method Boosts LLM Agent Exploration](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.17244) ⭐️ 8.0/10

DORA Explorer introduces a training-free, inference-time algorithm that improves exploration in LLM agents by ranking candidate actions using sequence-level log-probability statistics, outperforming temperature-based sampling in Multi-Armed Bandit and Text Adventure environments. This addresses a critical limitation in LLM agents for sequential decision-making, where insufficient exploration leads to suboptimal solutions and repeated actions. The method is model-agnostic and delivers consistent gains across model families, potentially impacting LLM agent research and applications. DORA generates multiple candidate actions, scores them using sequence-level log-probability statistics, and samples an action via a tunable exploration parameter. In TextWorld, it improved Qwen-2.5 7B performance from 31.43% (ReAct) to 45.5% (DORA), and also prevents common failures like getting stuck in loops.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 13, 04:00

**Background**: LLM agents for sequential decision-making often struggle to produce diverse outputs, leading to insufficient exploration. Traditional sampling strategies like temperature scaling introduce diversity at the token level, not the sequence level, which is where actions are generated. DORA Explorer addresses this by ranking actions based on sequence-level log-probabilities, a training-free approach that can be applied at inference time.

## [Study Boosts Local LLM Linux Privilege Escalation Success from 8% to 67%](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.27143) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper empirically identifies six failure modes of small open-weight LLMs in Linux privilege escalation and evaluates five enhancement techniques, raising success rates of Llama3.1 8B and Qwen2.5 7B from 8% to 67% with guidance, matching guided GPT-4o, while Llama3.1 70B reaches 83%. This work addresses privacy and sovereignty concerns by improving local LLM performance in a security-critical task, potentially enabling more organizations to use on-premise AI for penetration testing without sending sensitive data to cloud APIs. The reproducible framework and transferable lessons benefit both offensive security researchers and defenders. The study uses the open-source hackingBuddyGPT framework and a full-factorial ablation, finding that reflection-based techniques contribute most and that vulnerability discovery, not exploitation, is the main constraint for local models. The techniques include chain-of-thought prompting, retrieval-augmented generation, structured prompting, history compression, and reflective analysis.

## [CAR: Training-Free Confidence-Aware Reranking for RAG](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.04495) ⭐️ 8.0/10

CAR introduces a training-free, query-guided confidence-aware reranking method for retrieval-augmented generation (RAG). It uses answer stability as a control to estimate each document's marginal contribution to generator behavior, then adjusts the ranking accordingly. This addresses the gap between relevance and generator-side usefulness in RAG, which is a common limitation of existing retrieval and reranking methods. By improving ranking quality without requiring training or model internals, CAR can be easily deployed in black-box LLM systems, potentially enhancing downstream generation performance across various applications. Experiments on NQ, HotpotQA, and FEVER across sparse and dense retrievers, seven ranking methods, and three generator families show robust improvements. In the BM25-centered main analysis, CAR achieves a +5.53% mean relative NDCG@5 gain, and on the fixed NQ-answerable downstream evaluation, it improves token-level F1 by +0.43 points, with ranking and generation gains strongly aligned across rankers (rho = 0.93).

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 13, 04:00

**Background**: Retrieval-augmented generation (RAG) combines a retriever with a generator to produce answers grounded in external knowledge. Traditional retrieval and reranking methods focus on query-document relevance, but relevance does not always translate to generator usefulness. CAR addresses this by using answer stability as a proxy for generator behavior, and it adjusts rankings based on the marginal contribution of each document to that stability, while preserving the original ranking as much as possible using Kendall distance.

## [FP4 LLM Training Divergence Traced to Weight Gradients; Hadamard Rotations Fix It](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.09825) ⭐️ 8.0/10

A new arXiv paper identifies weight gradient quantization as the primary cause of divergence in full-pipeline FP4 training of large language models, and demonstrates that deterministic Hadamard rotations can stabilize training on native MXFP4 hardware (AMD Instinct MI355X). This finding addresses a critical bottleneck in low-precision training, potentially enabling more efficient and cost-effective training of large models on FP4 hardware. It also provides a practical solution (deterministic Hadamard rotations) that could be widely adopted in the industry. The study used a controlled setup with Llama 3.1-8B on the C4 dataset, progressively enabling FP4 for forward propagation, activation gradients, and weight gradients. They found that stochastic rounding and randomized Hadamard rotations fail, while deterministic Hadamard rotations consistently restore stable optimization.

Hadamard rotations are orthogonal transformations that can help distribute quantization errors more evenly, and have been explored in quantization-aware training.

The paper introduces a novel cavity-enhanced optical architecture for collective quantum processing, where logical qubits are encoded in the polarization subspace of recirculating intracavity modes. It demonstrates that universal quantum gates can be achieved with relaxed experimental constraints, requiring only centimeter-scale cavities and accessible solid-state nonlinear media. This architecture could significantly lower the experimental barriers for building scalable quantum processors, making quantum computing more accessible to a wider research community. By decoupling the physical carrier from the computational degree of freedom, it offers a practical path toward cavity-based collective quantum architectures. The scheme uses harmonic cavity bundles as a stable resonant substrate and programmable polarization transformations for single-qubit operations. A polarization-selective nonlinear interaction in the entanglement region generates tunable controlled-phase gates, enabling a universal gate set without requiring extreme nonlinear coefficients, millisecond photon lifetimes, or sub-hertz laser stabilization.

The paper introduces a unified framework combining Context-Smoothed Pre-training (CSP) and Timestep-Modulated Reinforcement Learning (TMRL), which uses diffusion timestep modulation to control exploration during RL fine-tuning of pre-trained robot policies. This addresses a key challenge in robot learning: bridging behavioral cloning pretraining and RL finetuning to enable efficient exploration. It improves sample efficiency and enables real-world fine-tuning on complex manipulation tasks in under one hour, potentially accelerating deployment of robot policies. CSP injects forward-diffusion noise into policy inputs, creating a continuum from precise imitation to broad action coverage. TMRL trains a high-level policy to dynamically select the diffusion timestep (noise level) per action chunk, granting explicit control over exploration. The method integrates with arbitrary policy inputs, including states, 3D point clouds, and image-based VLA policies.

By modulating the timestep, TMRL can adjust the policy's action distribution between precise imitation and broad exploration.

memorywire introduces a JSON-Schema 2020-12 wire format standardizing five memory operations (remember, recall, forget, merge, expire) across four memory types, with a reference implementation and a human-in-the-loop governance channel. This addresses a critical interoperability gap among popular agent-memory frameworks like mem0, Letta, and Cognee, enabling seamless integration and migration. It also introduces governance features that allow human review of memory writes, which is crucial for trust and safety in AI agents. The reference implementation includes five backend adapters (sqlite-vec, mem0, Letta, Cognee, pgvector) and achieves recall@5 = 1.000 on a benchmark with ingest p50 = 37.8 ms and recall p50 = 40.6 ms. An adversarial-fusion experiment shows Reciprocal Rank Fusion maintains recall@5 = 1.000 across rank-0 injection sweeps, while max fusion collapses to 0.500 with 80% leak at K >= 5.

## [CHERRL: A Controllable Testbed for Reward Hacking in Rubric-Based RL](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.04923) ⭐️ 8.0/10

The paper introduces CHERRL, a controllable hacking environment for rubric-based reinforcement learning (RL), which injects known biases into LLM-as-a-Judge (LaaJ) to stably reproduce reward hacking, observe reward divergence, and identify hacking onset. The code and environment are publicly available on GitHub. This work addresses a critical issue in RLHF and AI safety by providing a systematic testbed to study and detect reward hacking, which can lead to ineffective or unsafe training outcomes. It enables researchers to analyze judge biases and develop mitigation strategies, advancing alignment research. CHERRL allows explicit observation of reward divergence and precise identification of hacking onset by injecting known biases into the judge. The paper analyzes different judge biases from the perspectives of discoverability and exploitability, and explores an agent for automatically detecting reward hacking onset from training logs.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 13, 04:00

**Background**: Rubric-based reinforcement learning (RL) uses an LLM-as-a-Judge (LaaJ) to score model outputs according to rubrics as rewards. However, policy models may exploit latent biases in the judge, leading to reward hacking, where the model optimizes for the reward signal rather than the intended objective. This can result in ineffective or unsafe training outcomes, making detection and mitigation crucial for AI alignment.

## [FACTR 2: Sensorless Force Estimation Boosts Robot Policy Learning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.12406) ⭐️ 8.0/10

The paper introduces NEXT, a data-driven method that estimates external joint torques without dedicated force sensors, training in 1 minute from 10 minutes of free-motion data. It also presents FIRST, a training strategy that up-samples contact segments during behavior cloning, achieving over 17% better task progress on long-horizon manipulation tasks. This work makes force-aware teleoperation and policy learning accessible to commodity robot arms without additional sensing hardware, reducing cost and complexity. The significant performance improvement across multiple tasks suggests broad applicability in contact-rich manipulation, potentially accelerating robotics research and deployment. NEXT uses a recurrent model trained on free-motion data to estimate external torques, achieving estimates comparable to dedicated joint-torque sensors. FIRST up-samples pre-contact and contact segments during behavior cloning, and the combined approach outperforms prior force-aware policies by over 17% in task progress across five long-horizon tasks.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 13, 04:00

**Background**: Contact-rich manipulation requires force sensitivity, but many robot arms lack dedicated force sensors due to high cost. Traditional force estimation often relies on system identification or additional hardware. NEXT offers a sensorless, data-driven alternative, and FIRST leverages estimated forces to improve imitation learning by focusing on contact-relevant phases.

## [ATMA Hybrid Model Boosts Long-Context Retrieval with Polar Attention and Gated-Delta Memory](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.25156) ⭐️ 8.0/10

ATMA, a 378M-parameter hybrid model combining Polar Attention with gated-delta recurrent memory, was introduced and evaluated, showing substantial gains in long-context retrieval. In a factorial sweep, memory improved Polar's 64K retrieval score in all 20 matched cells, with a mean increase of +47.8 points. This work addresses the critical challenge of length extrapolation in language models, offering a novel hybrid architecture that balances retrieval fidelity, long-document likelihood, short-context quality, and inference cost. The systematic factorial study and Pareto perspective provide valuable insights for the NLP community, potentially influencing future model designs for long-context applications. ATMA uses Polar Attention, which separates a normalized direction channel from a bounded participation-ratio magnitude channel, and combines it with gated-delta recurrent memory. The model was trained for 9.816B tokens at length 2K and evaluated up to 256K, achieving 34.4% teacher-forced target-token accuracy and 9.0% exact five-token accuracy at 256K, though exact retrieval on FinePDFs contexts was 0.0%.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 13, 04:00

**Background**: Length extrapolation in language models refers to the ability to handle sequences longer than those seen during training. Traditional attention mechanisms, such as RoPE, often struggle with this, and hybrid models that combine attention with recurrent memory have been explored to improve efficiency and performance. Polar Attention is a novel mechanism that separates direction and magnitude information, potentially offering better extrapolation properties. Gated-delta memory is a type of recurrent memory that updates states using a delta rule, which can help maintain long-term information.

## [Autonomous LLM Agent Outperforms Expert Models in Band-Gap Prediction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.29717) ⭐️ 8.0/10

A general-purpose coding agent, driven by an autonomous LLM research loop, achieved state-of-the-art band-gap prediction on the MatBench benchmark, surpassing all 17 expert-designed models without external pretraining. The agent implemented known methods, including element-pair features on message-passing edges and crystal space-group embeddings. This demonstrates that LLM-based autonomous research can effectively optimize machine learning models in materials science, potentially accelerating discovery and reducing reliance on human expertise. It also highlights the potential of AI agents to integrate and apply existing techniques in novel ways, which could transform research workflows across scientific domains. The agent's success came from implementing known techniques rather than inventing new ones, such as element-pair features on each message-passing edge and a crystal space-group embedding. The study also investigates the limitations of such autonomous research, providing insights into when and why these agents succeed or fail.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 13, 04:00

**Background**: Crystal graph networks are graph neural networks that represent crystal structures as graphs, with atoms as nodes and bonds as edges, to predict material properties like band gaps. MatBench is a benchmark suite for materials science machine learning, providing standardized tasks for fair comparison. Band-gap prediction is a regression task that estimates the energy gap between a material's valence and conduction bands, which is crucial for applications like semiconductors and photovoltaics.

## [Prompt-Driven Exploration: VLMs Refine Prompts for RL Policies](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.08837) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper introduces Prompt-Driven Exploration (PDE), a method that uses a vision-language model (VLM) to iteratively refine natural language prompts for RL policies based on rollout videos, enabling global exploration beyond action-space noise. PDE allows RL to learn successful policies even from zero-reward starts across manipulation and reasoning tasks. This approach addresses a fundamental limitation of action-space noise in RL exploration, which only produces rollouts close to the original policy. By leveraging VLMs to perform global perturbations via prompts, PDE has the potential to significantly improve sample efficiency and enable learning in sparse-reward settings, impacting RL research and applications. PDE resembles posterior sampling at the prompt level: the VLM maintains an implicit distribution over useful prompts and updates it from observed rollouts. The method is validated on manipulation and reasoning tasks, showing improved sample efficiency and success from zero-reward starts.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 13, 04:00

**Background**: Reinforcement learning (RL) relies on exploration to improve policies, but standard methods inject noise in the action space, which only yields rollouts close to the original behavior. Vision-language-action (VLA) models condition policies on natural language prompts, so modifying the prompt induces global changes in behavior. This paper proposes using a VLM to refine prompts based on rollout videos, drawing on the idea of posterior sampling from classical RL exploration.

## [Cryptographically Verifiable Authorization for Autonomous AI Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.21325) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper introduces a formal framework for cryptographically verifiable agent authorization (CVA), defining a relation R_CVA that binds agent identity, requests, execution context, and policy satisfaction, along with a zero-knowledge proof-of-concept using Groth16 zk-SNARKs. This addresses a critical gap in AI agent security by providing cryptographic evidence for authorization decisions, which is essential as autonomous agents increasingly operate with limited human oversight. It could influence future agentic security frameworks and standards. The framework defines candidate security properties including authorization soundness, principal binding, request binding, policy binding, and replay resistance. It also highlights the structural separation among identity binding, request binding, and execution binding as an open problem.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 13, 04:00

**Background**: Autonomous AI agents execute actions and invoke tools with limited human oversight, relying on existing authentication and authorization mechanisms. However, these mechanisms do not provide cryptographic evidence that a specific request satisfies the applicable policy in a given context. The paper proposes a formal abstraction and proof-of-concept to address this gap.

## [Continual Learning Shifts from Parameters to System-Level Adaptation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.06216) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper presents a comprehensive survey and a tri-axial framework (When, How, Where) to characterize the transition of continual learning from parameter-centric methods to system-level adaptation, covering emerging paradigms such as on-policy learning, test-time training, and external harness components. This conceptual shift broadens the scope of continual learning beyond traditional model updates, potentially enabling more flexible and capable AI systems that adapt across training and inference stages. It provides a structured lens for researchers to navigate and compare diverse approaches, fostering progress in lifelong learning and autonomous adaptation. The framework's How dimension includes off-policy, on-policy, and beyond-gradient optimization; the When dimension spans pre-training, post-training, and inference-time; the Where dimension distinguishes updates in internal parameters versus external structural constraints. The survey systematically reviews representative methods and discusses challenges and future directions.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 13, 04:00

**Background**: Classical continual learning focuses on enabling models to update and retain knowledge through parameter-centric mechanisms like training strategies, architectural designs, and weight adaptation. However, emerging paradigms such as on-policy learning (e.g., self-distillation fine-tuning) and test-time training extend learning beyond the traditional training phase, while external components like memory and skill libraries expand the boundaries of model capabilities. This paper introduces a tri-axial framework to analyze this evolution.

## [ED-CSP: AI Predicts Crystal Structures from Electron Diffraction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.06448) ⭐️ 8.0/10

ED-CSP is a new machine learning framework that predicts 3D crystal structures from chemical composition and electron diffraction data, using a periodic flow generator and trained on a large simulated dataset of 4.85 million structures. It achieves a structural match rate of 57.49% MR@5 on held-out CHILI-100K materials, outperforming the state-of-the-art PXRDGen model. This work addresses a challenging inverse problem in materials science, enabling structure prediction from sparse, unindexed electron diffraction data, which is more accessible than X-ray diffraction for many samples. It could accelerate materials discovery and characterization, and the large simulated dataset (ED-CS) provides a benchmark for future research. The model combines a relational set encoder, permutation-invariant multi-view aggregation, and a periodic flow generator to jointly predict lattice parameters and fractional atomic coordinates. Scaling training data improves performance: initializing from a one-million-structure precursor raises MR@5 to 66.27%, and on compositions absent from the training retrieval library, it still achieves 53.52% MR@5, demonstrating true generative capability.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 13, 04:00

**Background**: Crystal structure prediction (CSP) is the task of determining the 3D arrangement of atoms in a crystal from its chemical composition and other data. Electron diffraction (ED) is a technique that scatters electrons off a sample to reveal its structure, but interpreting sparse and unindexed ED patterns is difficult. Traditional methods rely on indexed reflections or structure libraries, while machine learning approaches like PXRDGen use powder X-ray diffraction data. ED-CSP extends this to electron diffraction, using a generative model to directly predict structures.

EvoX Genesis introduces persistent recursive worlds that allow finite-lived agents to collaboratively evolve complex software over long timescales, demonstrated by building a Rust-based C compiler in a 120-hour run with over 1,000 agent episodes. This architecture shifts the focus from persistent agents to persistent projects, potentially enabling more scalable and cost-effective autonomous software development. ## [EU Law Scope Not Determined by AI Inference Capability](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.10601) ⭐️ 8.0/10

The paper argues that the legal scope of EU digital law, specifically the AI Act and GDPR, is not determined by a system's inferential capability, and that agentic architectures make the non-coincidence of these legal perimeters operationally significant. This analysis is significant because it clarifies the distinct legal functions of inference under EU law, which is crucial for compliance as agentic AI systems become more prevalent. It provides a framework that helps both legal and technical audiences understand when the AI Act and GDPR apply, potentially reducing regulatory uncertainty. The paper introduces a two-level framework: inference performs constitutive and protective functions, with the protective function operating through identificatory, attributive, and decisional pathways. It also proposes a compositional-effects test for Article 22 GDPR and documentation duties calibrated to inference chains.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 13, 04:00

**Background**: The EU AI Act defines an AI system by its capability to infer, while the GDPR governs inference protectively without defining it. The two legal instruments have different scopes, and the paper argues that their non-coincidence becomes operationally significant with agentic architectures. The paper also references Regulation (EU) 2026/1744, which left the constitutive criterion untouched but added a provision about outputs influencing future operations.

## [Cost-Aware Cross-Tier Transfer Cuts LLM Prompt Optimization Cost](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.10694) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper introduces a cost-aware cross-tier transfer strategy for evolutionary LLM prompt optimization, which reduces search cost by 5.6-14x (and up to 25-54x with reasoning tiers) while maintaining or improving performance across four tasks and eleven models. This work addresses the dominant cost bottleneck in evolutionary LLM optimization—fitness evaluation—by decoupling LLM roles and leveraging cheaper tiers, making prompt optimization more accessible and scalable for practitioners. The method places over 96% of search tokens on the cheapest tier, reserving strong models for reflection/variation operators. It provides a cost-controlled characterization of when cheap-tier search substitutes for target-tier search and where it fails, tested on HotpotQA, IFBench, LiveBench-Math, and HoVer.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 13, 04:00

**Background**: Evolutionary optimization of LLM prompts (e.g., GEPA) iteratively mutates and evaluates candidate prompts, with fitness evaluation being the costliest step. By decoupling the answering, reflection, and variation roles of LLMs, the method exploits cross-tier transfer to evolve prompts cheaply and deploy them on stronger models.

## [Reference-Free Post-Training Boosts Multilingual Translation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.10812) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper introduces a reference-free post-training method for multilingual machine translation, applying Group Relative Policy Optimization (GRPO) with quality estimation rewards to the MiLMMT-46-v0.1 models, resulting in the MiLMMT-46-v1.0 models that outperform strong baselines and proprietary systems across 46 languages. This work demonstrates that reference-free reinforcement learning can effectively improve multilingual translation quality without relying on human references, potentially reducing the cost and complexity of training high-quality translation models. It also shows that open models can compete with or surpass proprietary systems, which is significant for the NLP community and industry. The method uses GRPO with a reward that averages two reference-free quality estimation models and is gated by language identification, followed by linear interpolation between SFT and RL checkpoints. On-policy distillation (OPD) was also investigated but did not surpass the RL with checkpoint interpolation approach.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 13, 04:00

**Background**: Multilingual machine translation aims to translate between many languages using a single model. Traditional supervised fine-tuning (SFT) requires parallel data with references, which is scarce for low-resource languages. Reinforcement learning, such as GRPO, can optimize models using reward signals, and reference-free quality estimation models provide rewards without needing references, enabling post-training on any source-target pair.

Deutsche Bank has been designated as the first foreign yuan clearing bank in Europe, a significant milestone in the internationalization of the Chinese currency. This move is expected to facilitate more efficient RMB transactions between Europe and China. This development could reduce reliance on the US dollar in international trade and finance, potentially shifting global financial power dynamics. It may also lower transaction costs for European businesses trading with China and strengthen the RMB's role as a global reserve currency. The designation allows Deutsche Bank to clear RMB transactions directly, bypassing the need for correspondent banks in the US. This is part of China's broader strategy to establish RMB clearing banks worldwide, with over 25 countries already having such arrangements.

## [Control-Theoretic Governance Prevents Collapse in Multi-LLM Agent Systems](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.11207) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces the Experience Orchestrator (EO), a governance layer combining contextual bandits, PID control, and POMDP belief tracking to manage multi-LLM agent interactions. In 60,000 simulations, EO achieved a +32 percentage point lift in high-intent advisor contact rate (78.1% vs. 46.1%) compared to a naive LLM control. This work addresses a critical problem in multi-agent LLM systems: goal misalignment leading to conversational collapse. By demonstrating that a control-theoretic governance layer can substitute for a shared goal function, it offers a promising path toward more robust and effective multi-agent AI systems in real-world applications. The EO uses a Contextual Bandit to select content arms calibrated from real-world web analytics, a PID controller to enforce behavioral consistency via dynamic schema constraints, and a POMDP belief tracker to maintain a probabilistic model of visitor intent. The CB variant selection accounted for 97% of between-factor outcome variance, and the findings are conditional on LLM-to-LLM simulation; the PID controller has not been calibrated against real human unpredictability.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 13, 04:00

**Background**: Multi-agent LLM systems involve multiple AI agents interacting to accomplish tasks, but without a shared goal function, they can drift toward locally coherent but globally incoherent states, leading to conversational collapse. Control theory provides mechanisms like PID control and POMDPs to manage dynamic systems under uncertainty, which can be applied to govern agent interactions. Contextual bandits are a class of reinforcement learning algorithms that balance exploration and exploitation to select actions based on context.

## [Distribird: Automating Informative Priors for Bayesian Calibration](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.11210) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Distribird is an agentic web application that automates the construction of informative prior distributions for Bayesian model calibration. It uses a multi-agent pipeline to search literature, extract and weight reported values, and fit distributions via AIC model selection, with a fallback to uninformative priors when no literature is found. This tool addresses a common bottleneck in Bayesian calibration—the tedious and error-prone manual construction of informative priors—potentially improving reproducibility and accessibility for researchers. By automating prior elicitation, it could encourage wider adoption of informative priors over default uniform priors, leading to more accurate and reliable model calibrations. The system was evaluated on 24 parameters across 10 scientific domains, comparing three open-weight models (Qwen3.6 27B, Gemma 4 31B, Mistral Small 4 119B) against a single-prompt LLM baseline, and matched baseline prior quality. Notably, all language-model calls run locally, ensuring privacy, and a built-in validity layer declines out-of-scope requests, whereas the baseline produced confident but unfounded priors in 11 of 30 model-parameter cases.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 13, 04:00

**Background**: Bayesian model calibration involves updating prior distributions with data to infer model parameters, but informative priors are often difficult to construct, requiring both domain and statistical expertise. AIC (Akaike Information Criterion) is a common method for model selection, balancing goodness of fit with model complexity. Agentic AI refers to AI systems that can autonomously pursue goals and use tools, often driven by large language models, enabling multi-step tasks like literature search and data extraction.

## [AI Agent Attacks Conway's 99-Graph with Partial Results](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.11211) ⭐️ 7.0/10

An autonomous AI research agent conducted a systematic, reproducible attack on Conway's 99-graph problem, proving that no circulant graph on Z/99 satisfies more than 68% of the constraints and establishing a forced-structure reduction that collapses the problem to a 12-regular graph on 84 vertices. The best verified artifact achieved 69.43% constraint satisfaction, with evidence suggesting this is a robust frontier. This work demonstrates the potential of autonomous AI agents in tackling long-standing open problems in mathematics, providing verifiable partial results and reductions that could guide future research. It also offers new insights into the structure of strongly regular graphs and the limitations of circulant graphs for this problem. The paper proves that no circulant graph on Z/99 satisfies more than 3366/4950 (68.0%) of the constraints, and the same ceiling holds for the other abelian group of order 99. The forced-structure reduction uses λ=1 to make each neighborhood a perfect matching and μ=2 to put outer vertices in bijection with non-matched neighbor-pairs, collapsing existence to a 12-regular graph on 84 vertices, validated by recovering the unique srg(9,4,1,2).

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 13, 04:00

**Background**: Conway's 99-graph problem asks whether there exists a strongly regular graph with parameters srg(99,14,1,2), meaning each pair of adjacent vertices has exactly one common neighbor and each non-adjacent pair has exactly two common neighbors. This is an unsolved problem in graph theory, with a $1000 prize offered by John Conway. Strongly regular graphs are a class of graphs with strong regularity properties, and circulant graphs are a special type where the adjacency matrix is circulant.

## [Detecting Route Flips in Quantized MoE Is Easier Than Judging Their Harm](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.11212) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper investigates how quantization-induced routing flips in Mixture-of-Experts (MoE) models cause damage, showing that detecting a flip is possible but determining whether it is harmful is not. It introduces a causal apparatus to price the route-mediated fraction (RMF) of quantization damage and reports that on OLMoE-1B-7B at 4-bit KV cache, about a third of the damage is routing-mediated (RMF ~ 0.31). This work addresses a novel and important issue in quantized MoE models, which are increasingly deployed in production. The finding that no local, inference-observable router statistic can predict a flip's loss sign above chance has implications for selective repair strategies, potentially limiting their effectiveness. The paper proposes no new mitigation but supplies a causal apparatus, empirical findings, and a detection-limit result. A deployable router margin detects flips with AUC 0.772 but cannot distinguish harmful from helpful flips (at chance). The signed-flip tax and sign-inseparability carry across models, while a real int4 KV kernel yields a fraction compatible with the fake-quant dose curve but underpowered (95% CI includes zero).

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 13, 04:00

**Background**: Mixture-of-Experts (MoE) layers replace a single feed-forward block with many experts and a router that selects a few per token, enabling large models to be computationally efficient. Top-k routing is discontinuous, so numerical disturbances like quantization can push tokens across decision boundaries, changing which experts fire. KV-cache quantization is a common technique to reduce memory usage in long-context LLMs, but it can introduce errors that propagate through the model.

## [SAPO: Segment-Level Prompt Optimization Outperforms Monolithic APO](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.11219) ⭐️ 7.0/10

SAPO introduces a segment-level automatic prompt optimization method that decomposes prompts into role, context, tasks, and output format, then applies targeted improvements based on top-5 and bottom-5 examples. It achieves the best average score across SQuADv2, TweetEval, XSUM, CommonGen, and GSM8K on GPT-3.5-Turbo and GPT-4o-mini, outperforming strong baselines like APE, OPRO, EvoPrompt, GEPA, and StraGO. This work addresses a key limitation of monolithic prompt optimization, where improving one behavior can degrade others. By optimizing segments independently, SAPO offers a more modular and controllable approach, potentially leading to more robust and efficient prompt engineering for LLMs. SAPO uses a single LLM with static meta-prompts and structured outputs for segmentation, weakness analysis, and candidate generation. The optimization loop follows a two-stage process: segment-level diagnosis and recommendation extraction, followed by candidate synthesis constrained by weak/strong segment signals.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 13, 04:00

**Background**: Automatic Prompt Optimization (APO) aims to automatically refine prompts for large language models (LLMs) to improve performance on specific tasks. Traditional APO methods often rewrite the entire prompt monolithically, which can lead to unintended trade-offs. SAPO addresses this by breaking prompts into semantic segments and optimizing each individually, allowing for more targeted improvements.

This paper introduces P&ID Pilot, an end-to-end AI pipeline that automates PFD synthesis and P&ID generation. It combines genetic algorithms (GA) and large language models (LLM) to produce optimal valid PFD topologies and transform them into source-grounded P&IDs with 100% execution success. This work addresses a critical bottleneck in industrial process engineering, where PFD and P&ID creation is largely manual. By automating these stages, it could significantly reduce engineering effort and costs, and enable exploration of more design alternatives, potentially transforming how process plants are designed. The hybrid GA-LLM approach achieved the lowest loss value among four compared methods while satisfying outlet flow parameters without engineering-rule violations. The LLM-based transformation agent uses a restricted engineering SDK to generate validated, executable modifications, achieving 100% execution success while maintaining compliance with domain-specific rules and reference graph structures.

## [CORA-Diff: Training-Free Acceleration for Diffusion Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.11235) ⭐️ 7.0/10

CORA-Diff is a training-free method that accelerates diffusion language model decoding by using confidence and persistence signals to skip unnecessary denoising steps, without modifying the backbone or learned acceptance models. It achieves up to 3.32x speedup on GSM8K and HumanEval, and 13.14x under a fixed-horizon protocol, while maintaining task quality. This work addresses a practical bottleneck in diffusion language model inference, offering a plug-and-play acceleration that preserves model quality. It is significant for deploying DLMs in latency-sensitive applications and complements existing inference-time optimization research. CORA-Diff applies confidence-and-persistence gating only to positions left unresolved by the original transfer rule, and accepted tokens remain visible as context. It selects one operating point on a GSM8K calibration subset and freezes it for all evaluations, showing robustness across tasks and protocols.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 13, 04:00

**Background**: Diffusion language models (DLMs) generate text through iterative denoising, updating many tokens in parallel, unlike autoregressive models. Practical decoders often use a fixed denoising horizon, leading to repeated dense forward passes even when many predictions stabilize early. Existing acceleration methods rely on learned filters or modified scores, whereas CORA-Diff leverages native trajectory signals to skip unnecessary steps.

## [GeoIncNO: Geometry-Aware Incremental Neural Operator for Stable Long-Horizon PDE Prediction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.11237) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces GeoIncNO, a geometry-aware incremental neural operator that predicts latent increments for residual advancement and uses low-rank projectors to regulate channel coupling within active frequency bands derived from spectral energy distribution. It also introduces a mean-fluctuation decoupled reconstruction mechanism with phase correction applied only to the zero-mean fluctuation component. This work addresses a critical challenge in neural operators for PDEs: long-horizon autoregressive prediction stability. By improving spectral fidelity and rollout stability, GeoIncNO could enhance the reliability of neural PDE solvers in scientific machine learning applications, potentially impacting fields like climate modeling and engineering simulation. GeoIncNO was evaluated on six PDE benchmarks covering 1D, 2D, and 3D dynamical systems, demonstrating consistently strong prediction accuracy, improved rollout stability, and better spectral fidelity compared to competitive neural-operator baselines. The method uses lightweight low-rank projectors and a mean-fluctuation decoupled reconstruction mechanism.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 13, 04:00

**Background**: Neural operators are deep learning models that learn mappings between function spaces to solve partial differential equations (PDEs) efficiently. However, long-horizon autoregressive prediction suffers from error accumulation, leading to spectral inconsistency, phase misalignment, or mean drift. Existing methods focus on improving state representations and operator backbones but leave the latent transition increment weakly structured. GeoIncNO addresses this by predicting latent increments and regulating channel couplings via low-rank projectors based on spectral energy.

## [Q-CARE: Query-Agnostic, Reference-Free RAG Evaluation Framework](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.11238) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers introduced Q-CARE, a query-agnostic and reference-free framework for evaluating Retrieval-Augmented Generation (RAG) systems. It decomposes queries into sub-queries and answers into atomic claims, using query coverage and claim verifiability to produce fine-grained metrics that outperform existing methods like RAGEval and RAGChecker on human-annotated benchmarks. This framework addresses a significant gap in RAG evaluation by providing consistent, fine-grained diagnostics across diverse query types without requiring reference answers. It offers a more reliable automated evaluation method, which could improve the development and benchmarking of RAG systems in the NLP community. Q-CARE introduces coverage-aware retriever metrics (C-Prec@k, C-nDCG@k) and claim-level generator metrics (Completeness, Conciseness, Verifiableness). It was validated on a human-annotated benchmark spanning eight datasets, achieving higher correlation with human judgments than four existing RAG evaluation metrics, including RAGEval and RAGChecker. Code and data are publicly available on GitHub.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 13, 04:00

**Background**: Retrieval-Augmented Generation (RAG) combines retrieval of relevant documents with large language models to improve factual accuracy. Existing evaluation frameworks often rely on reference answers or struggle to handle the wide range of user queries, from fact-seeking to open-ended questions. Q-CARE aims to overcome these limitations by being query-agnostic and reference-free, decomposing queries and answers into finer components for more nuanced assessment.

## [RecSys Factory: Bounding LLM Agent Autonomy to Decision Points](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.11241) ⭐️ 7.0/10

RecSys Factory, an LLM-agent platform deployed for 78 days across three Tencent recommender business lines, introduces the autonomy-determinism-efficiency trilemma and a design principle of bounding agent autonomy to decision points. The platform records 1,624 CLI-tool dispatches at a 78.6% aggregate success rate, with zero CPU consumption during 94% of wall-clock time spent waiting on Spark or GPU jobs. This work addresses a critical tension in deploying LLM agents in industrial settings, offering a concrete framework and deployment evidence that could guide future systems engineering. It demonstrates a practical balance between autonomy, determinism, and efficiency, potentially influencing how LLM agents are integrated into large-scale recommender systems and other industrial operations. The runtime is deconstructed into three host-emitted event sources (Claude Code Stop hooks, corporate-IM webhooks, workflow scheduler APIs), eliminating the need for a long-running daemon. The capability layer comprises a 29-file skill ecosystem (8,971 lines of SKILL.md) with per-skill pitfall tables compiling into a 400-entry PitfallStore, and a human-in-the-loop card protocol serves as an audit-trail primitive.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 13, 04:00

**Background**: LLM agents are increasingly used to automate complex workflows, but in industrial recommender systems, they must balance general autonomy (interpreting operator intent) with industrial determinism (schema-conforming outputs, non-crashing A/B tests) and end-to-end efficiency. The 'impossible trinity' concept from economics, which states that certain policy goals cannot be simultaneously achieved, is analogous to the trilemma proposed here. Claude Code Stop hooks are a mechanism to enforce constraints on LLM-generated code, running after response generation to validate or force revisions.

## [BEST-KAG: Multimodal Knowledge Graph and LLM Framework for Building Standards QA](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.11244) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces BEST-KAG, a multimodal knowledge-driven framework that combines a large-scale knowledge graph with large language models to enhance question answering on building engineering standards. It achieves up to a 74.01% improvement over baseline LLMs in expert evaluation and metrics like BLEU and ROUGE. This work addresses the limitations of traditional keyword-based retrieval in construction standards, enabling multi-clause reasoning and traceable evidence linkage. It demonstrates the potential of combining knowledge graphs with LLMs for domain-specific applications, which could improve safety and efficiency in building engineering. The framework includes a multimodal knowledge graph (MKG) with 251 building engineering standards, 171,652 nodes, and 310,914 edges, built via a rule-LLM hybrid extraction pipeline. It also employs a graph-retrieval-based knowledge-augmented generation architecture for clause-grounded and traceable answers.

Traditional RAG methods often suffer from ambiguity and noise, which KAG aims to overcome by leveraging structured knowledge. Multimodal knowledge graphs extend this by incorporating diverse data types like text and images, supporting complex reasoning in specialized fields such as construction standards.
