## [EvoGraph-Mem: Failure-Aware Editable Graph Memory for Long-Term Language Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.11248) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces EvoGraph-Mem, a novel framework for long-term language agents that maintains insight-level memories using an editable graph. Each insight node tracks positive and negative evidence and an activation state, enabling failure-aware memory maintenance that updates the graph after task execution. This work addresses the underexplored problem of memory pollution in long-term language agents, where outdated or over-generalized insights degrade performance. By enabling evidence-aware retrieval and graph-level editing, it improves memory reliability and downstream task performance, potentially enhancing agent robustness in evolving environments. The framework includes a utility-aware retrieval mechanism and a graph controller that keeps reliable insights, archives invalid ones, revises outdated ones, and adds new reusable insights. Experiments show consistent outperformance over representative memory-based baselines across different backbone models, and ablation studies confirm that append-only memory is insufficient for long-horizon tasks.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 13, 04:00

**Background**: Long-term memory in language agents stores past experiences to aid future interactions, but existing methods often focus on storage and retrieval without addressing memory quality degradation. Memory pollution occurs when outdated or over-generalized insights are repeatedly reused, harming performance. EvoGraph-Mem models insights as an editable graph with evidence tracking to mitigate this issue.

## [Symbolic ML Improves Vapor-Liquid Equilibrium Predictions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.11255) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces a symbolic machine learning approach that derives interpretable corrections to the Peng-Robinson equation of state, significantly improving vapor-liquid equilibrium predictions for hydrocarbon-nitrogen mixtures across various chain lengths. This work addresses a practical challenge in chemical engineering by offering an interpretable alternative to black-box deep learning models, potentially improving process design and simulation accuracy for hydrocarbon systems. The approach uses a two-level strategy: first identifying symbolic expressions for individual hydrocarbon systems, then representing coefficients as functions of carbon number to generalize across systems. Results show significantly improved accuracy over the original PR-EOS for all tested hydrocarbon-nitrogen systems.

Symbolic regression is a machine learning technique that searches for mathematical expressions fitting data, offering interpretability and explicit analytical forms, unlike deep learning models.

## [Adaptive Hybrid PSO Modulates Gradient Influence via Swarm Diversity](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.11258) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces Adaptive Hybrid PSO (AHPSO), which uses a sigmoid function on swarm diversity to automatically modulate gradient influence, eliminating manual phase-switching. Under budget-normalized comparison, AHPSO wins on smooth-basin problems (F8, F24-F27) but loses overall to standard PSO (20% vs 52.5% win rate, p=7.0e-5). This work provides a principled characterization of when gradient injection benefits PSO, which is valuable for the optimization community. It suggests that hybrid methods should adaptively control gradient influence rather than applying it uniformly, potentially improving performance on specific problem classes without sacrificing robustness. Under iteration-matched comparison across 29 functions (42 configurations, 14,700 runs), AHPSO-Adadelta ranks first among 9 methods including CMA-ES (p=9.75e-4). The authors emphasize that the contribution is a characterization of when gradient injection helps, not a claim of universal superiority.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 13, 04:00

**Background**: Particle Swarm Optimization (PSO) is a population-based metaheuristic inspired by social behavior, often used for continuous optimization. Gradient descent is a classic local search method that uses derivative information. Hybridizing them can be beneficial, but gradient information may mislead the swarm during exploration. AHPSO uses swarm diversity as a proxy for exploration/exploitation phase, modulating gradient influence via a sigmoid function.

## [Glance, Scrutinize, Think: New Paradigm for Video Anomaly Detection](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.11260) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces Glance then Scrutinize (GtS), a training-free framework for coarse-to-fine video anomaly grounding, and a tool-augmented agentic VAD method that uses a multimodal LLM to invoke tools and self-correct via reinforcement learning. It also extends the VAGU benchmark to VAGU-T with 7,567 videos and introduces the JeAUG metric. This work addresses the 'when-what' dissociation in video anomaly detection by unifying temporal grounding and semantic reasoning, potentially improving both accuracy and interpretability. It offers a training-free baseline and an agentic approach, which could influence future VAD systems and benchmarks. GtS uses static and dynamic textual guidance for coarse-to-fine anomaly grounding. The agentic model is trained with cold-start supervised fine-tuning followed by reinforcement learning with a joint answer-grounding reward, and it invokes a video cropping tool to inspect densely resampled frames. Experiments show the agentic model achieves higher accuracy and faster inference than training-free baselines.

Traditional DNN-based methods detect anomalies but lack semantic understanding, while LLM-based methods explain anomalies but neglect precise temporal grounding. This paper proposes a human-inspired global-to-local paradigm to unify these capabilities.

## [Frontier LLMs Match Multimodal Embeddings in Text-to-Image Retrieval](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.11343) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv preprint (2608.11343) compares Google's natively multimodal Gemini Embedding 2 with frontier LLMs (GPT-4.1, Claude Sonnet 4.6) on hard-negative text-to-image retrieval using the Flickr30k dataset. The study finds that these LLMs perform on par with the specialized embedding model. This is the first direct comparison between natively multimodal embeddings and LLM-based zero-shot ranking for text-to-image retrieval. The finding that LLMs can match specialized embeddings suggests that practitioners may have flexible options for multimodal retrieval, potentially reducing the need for dedicated embedding models in some applications. The evaluation is limited to a single dataset (Flickr30k), and the paper is a preprint, so the impact is not yet fully established. Additionally, the study notes that once embeddings are precomputed, multimodal embeddings are better suited for low-latency applications, highlighting a trade-off between LLM flexibility and embedding efficiency.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 13, 04:00

**Background**: Multimodal retrieval has traditionally relied on dual-encoder models that align visual and textual representations via contrastive learning. Gemini Embedding 2, released in March 2026, is Google's first natively multimodal embedding model that maps text, images, video, audio, and documents into a single shared space. Hard-negative text-to-image retrieval involves finding images that match a text query while distinguishing them from very similar but incorrect images, which is a challenging task for retrieval systems.

## [Inverse Theory of Mind for Recommender Systems](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.11354) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces an Inverse Theory of Mind (IToM) pipeline that infers user beliefs and preferences from observed interactions, using LLM-driven counterfactual reasoning and multi-hypothesis abductive inference to build structured user personas. It evaluates this approach on the OPeRA dataset, showing that inferred personas match or exceed ground-truth personas in tasks like next action prediction and Big Five personality inference. This work addresses a key limitation of current recommender systems that treat observed actions as reliable proxies for preferences, which can be misleading. As interfaces evolve toward generative UIs and XR, this deeper, modality-agnostic user understanding could enable more adaptive and personalized experiences, impacting both AI/ML and HCI fields. The IToM pipeline reconstructs decision contexts, applies LLM-driven counterfactual reasoning to generate evidence-grounded belief statements, and synthesizes them into personas via multi-hypothesis abductive inference. It demonstrates cross-modal transferability with a persona-driven spatial banking application on VisionOS, and shows that multi-hypothesis reasoning is essential for accurate personality prediction.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 13, 04:00

**Background**: Theory of Mind (ToM) is the ability to attribute mental states to others, and inverse ToM involves inferring these states from behavior. Recommender systems traditionally rely on explicit or implicit feedback, but such signals can be noisy or reflect exploration rather than stable preferences. The paper proposes using LLMs for counterfactual reasoning to better infer user beliefs, which is a novel application in this context.

## [Specialist LLM Agents and GRPO Boost Real Estate Analysis](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.11381) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces Larix, a framework of eight specialist LLM agents for European listed real estate analysis, and shows that decomposition improves numerical tasks by 15.8 percentage points but not judgment tasks, while GRPO post-training on Qwen3.5-9B raises overall scores by 12.0 points and judgment aggregate by 14.2 points. This research provides empirical evidence on how LLM specialization and reinforcement learning can be applied to financial analysis, potentially guiding the development of more effective domain-specific AI tools for investment professionals and analysts. The study held model, source evidence, task instructions, output schema, and scoring fixed while comparing monolithic versus specialist-decomposed prompting across 19 firms and seven regulatory wrappers. GRPO post-training with task-aligned structured rewards improved performance on unseen firms (+15.2 points overall) and unseen regulatory wrappers (+4.3 points), with positive transfer on all anti-memorization splits.

The 16-lens framework is a structured approach to analyzing European listed real estate, covering various financial and operational aspects.

## [LOFA: Learning Shopping Agents from Online User Feedback](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.11604) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces LOFA, a framework that enables large language model-based shopping agents to learn directly from real online user interaction logs without human annotation. It combines reinforcement learning over verifiable purchase outcomes with feedback-aware on-policy distillation to convert noisy user feedback into dense token-level supervision. This approach addresses a practical challenge in e-commerce: leveraging the rich but noisy conversational feedback from real users to improve shopping agents. By learning from online feedback without human annotation, LOFA can enhance recommendation quality and user satisfaction, potentially reducing the need for costly data labeling and improving agent adaptability in real-world deployments. LOFA combines two complementary objectives: reinforcement learning over verifiable purchase outcomes and feedback-aware on-policy distillation that identifies in-dialogue directives. Experiments on real-world e-commerce logs show consistent improvements over strong baselines in recommendation quality, response helpfulness, and user-satisfaction alignment.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 13, 04:00

**Background**: Large language model-based shopping agents are increasingly deployed in e-commerce platforms, generating massive interaction logs. Traditional training relies on offline signals like user-item interactions or synthetic preferences, overlooking the rich supervision in natural conversational feedback. On-policy distillation trains a student on samples from its own evolving policy while a teacher provides dense token-level guidance, reducing the train-inference distribution gap. LOFA leverages this technique to handle heterogeneous, sparse, and noisy online feedback.

This paper introduces MBA-Bench, the first multimodal benchmark for training and evaluating business ideation agents, comprising 30K samples across six domains. It also presents two novel agents, MBA-b and MBA-k, trained with LoRA-based supervised fine-tuning and group relative policy optimization, which outperform baselines by up to 77.1%. It provides a standardized evaluation framework that could accelerate research and development of more capable AI agents for business innovation. The benchmark uses GPT-4o to generate reference ideas via retrieval query generation, market evidence retrieval, and evidence-augmented synthesis, and evaluates agents using MLLM-as-a-Judge across six business-oriented criteria. MBA-b and MBA-k are trained with two novel reward objectives (creativity and feasibility), with MBA-k additionally optimizing six disclosed criteria, totaling eight objectives.

## [Structured Workflows Boost Student Uptake of AI Feedback](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.11625) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A large-scale quasi-experimental study involving 13,037 students and 51,296 resources compared three AI-mediated feedback workflows, finding that the 'Enacted Feedback' workflow, which prompts students to select and evaluate feedback suggestions, significantly increased feedback uptake to 26.2%, compared to 14.1% for Directed Feedback and 0.1% for Self-Directed Feedback. This study highlights that simply providing AI-generated feedback is insufficient; the design of workflows that actively engage students in feedback enactment is crucial for improving learning outcomes. It has significant implications for the development of AI feedback systems in education, emphasizing the need to position learners as active participants rather than passive recipients. The study used a quasi-experimental sequential cohort design across three conditions: Directed Feedback (n=3,723), Self-Directed Feedback (n=3,951), and Enacted Feedback (n=5,363). Enacted Feedback also led to significantly higher self-assessment confidence and submitted-work quality compared to the other conditions.

**Background**: Feedback is known to strongly influence student learning, but its effectiveness depends on both the quality of feedback and students' ability to interpret and act on it. This study addresses the gap by comparing different workflow designs that aim to structure students' enactment of feedback literacy processes.

## [CLAIM: Uncertainty-Driven Active Clarification for LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.11631) ⭐️ 7.0/10

CLAIM introduces an uncertainty-driven framework that uses entropy from answer disagreements across multiple models to determine when and what to clarify in open-domain LLM interactions, eliminating the need for manual annotations. The framework combines supervised fine-tuning (SFT) with group-relative policy optimization (GRPO) to train a unified clarification decision model. This approach reduces the high annotation costs and improves generalization in active clarification, which is crucial for enhancing interaction quality in open-domain human-computer dialogue. It offers a low-cost, robust solution for proactive understanding in real-world LLM applications. The entropy-driven synthetic data generation pipeline integrates entropy-based uncertainty estimation with semantic clustering and reasoning-based judgments for automatic annotation. Experiments show that CLAIM learns stable and generalizable clarification strategies without relying on manually labeled data.

Entropy, a concept from information theory, quantifies uncertainty and is used here to measure answer disagreements across models.

It formulates the problem as Joint Multi-Scene Understanding (JMSU) and proposes two components: Endogenous Data Augmentation and Global Context Ranking, along with a new benchmark dataset called SortingBench. HUGIN addresses these by recombining verified atomic facts for data augmentation and aligning instruction representations with complete visual context.

## [Bayesian Updating Defines Proportional Analogies for Probability Distributions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.11724) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces a novel definition of proportional analogies for probability distributions based on Bayesian updating, extending the axiomatic framework to the probabilistic domain. It investigates this framework for several standard members of the exponential family and discusses extensions to arbitrary distributions via Gaussian mixture approximations. This work bridges analogical reasoning and probabilistic modeling, potentially enabling more flexible reasoning in AI/ML systems that handle uncertainty. It extends a well-established axiomatic framework to a new domain, which could impact areas like machine learning, cognitive science, and automated reasoning. The definition relies on the idea that two distributions are related if one can be transformed into the other via Bayesian updating induced by a suitable set of observations. The paper examines this for exponential family members and uses Gaussian mixture approximations to handle arbitrary distributions.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 13, 04:00

**Background**: Proportional analogies are quaternary relations of the form 'A is to B as C is to D' and have been formalized axiomatically for Boolean, symbolic, and real-valued domains. Bayesian updating is a core concept in Bayesian statistics where a prior distribution is updated to a posterior using observed data. The exponential family is a parametric set of probability distributions that includes many common distributions like normal, Poisson, and binomial.

## [HyperANFIS: Boosting Neuro-Fuzzy Interpretability via Hyperbolic Geometry](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.11768) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces HyperANFIS, a hyperbolic extension of the adaptive neuro-fuzzy inference system (ANFIS), which performs rule learning, activation, and aggregation in hyperbolic space instead of Euclidean space. This modification aims to enhance rule representation and interpretability while improving predictive accuracy. This work addresses a known limitation of ANFIS—its reliance on Euclidean space—potentially improving performance on hierarchical or scale-free data. It could advance interpretable AI by offering a more expressive yet transparent rule-based framework, benefiting applications that require both accuracy and explainability. HyperANFIS retains the fuzzy semantics and core architecture of conventional ANFIS, including the ability to generate IF-THEN rules. Experimental results show it consistently outperforms standard ANFIS and its variants across all datasets, while also producing higher-quality fuzzy rules.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 13, 04:00

**Background**: ANFIS is a neuro-fuzzy system that combines neural networks with fuzzy logic to create interpretable IF-THEN rules. Hyperbolic geometry, which has negative curvature, is better suited for representing hierarchical or tree-like data than Euclidean space, and has shown performance gains in machine learning models for such data.

## [Gist Compression Helps Multi-Hop but Hurts Temporal Recall in LLM Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.11775) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces Salience-Weighted Consolidation (SWC), a biologically-inspired compression framework, as a diagnostic probe to study gist-based context compression in long-horizon language model agents. Evaluating on the LoCoMo benchmark, it finds a task-type interaction: compression outperforms truncation on multi-hop reasoning and single-hop factual questions but significantly underperforms on temporal questions, with a one-sentence prompt modification recovering +0.314 judge accuracy on temporal questions. This work provides novel insights into when gist-based context compression helps or hurts in long-horizon agents, a critical issue for building effective memory systems. The findings could influence future agent design by highlighting the need to preserve temporal information during compression, potentially improving performance on time-sensitive tasks. The study uses four conditions across all ten LoCoMo conversations, with 1,935 matched text-only questions (1,501 used in the primary aggregate after excluding adversarial Category 5 questions) at temperature 0. A preservation analysis shows an approximately 20-fold increase in temporal expression preservation (3.05% to 62.39%) with a one-sentence prompt modification, while named entity and event preservation rates barely change (x1.02 and x1.11).

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 13, 04:00

**Background**: Gist-based context compression summarizes older conversation history into compact representations, a common technique in long-horizon language model agents to manage limited context windows. Salience-Weighted Consolidation (SWC) is inspired by sleep-based memory consolidation in neuroscience, where memories are replayed and strengthened based on salience. The LoCoMo benchmark is a dataset for evaluating long-term conversational memory, consisting of ten conversations with rich context. This paper uses SWC as a diagnostic tool to understand the effects of compression on different memory retrieval types.

## [ExRole: Learning Executable Roles from Team Trajectories for Multi-Agent QA](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.11949) ⭐️ 7.0/10

ExRole is a novel framework that learns future-aware role prototypes from prefix-local team traces, converting them into readable instructions and token-aligned role markers. It improves multi-hop QA performance on MuSiQue and 2WikiMultiHopQA by 15.0/14.4 and 13.5/16.1 EM/F1 points over single-agent search, respectively. This work addresses a key limitation in multi-agent systems where roles are typically hand-written and static, by making them executable and learned from data. It demonstrates significant gains on multi-hop QA benchmarks, suggesting that trajectory-induced role conditioning can lead to more effective and adaptable agent coordination. ExRole optionally routes shared LoRA rank slots with turn-aligned credit, and its controlled results consistently favor trajectory-induced role conditioning over role-free, manual, random, and shuffled alternatives. Role-Agent-Turn interventions show that induced roles capture transferable behavioral specialization beyond fixed agent identities or turn positions.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 13, 04:00

**Background**: In multi-agent language model systems, roles are typically defined as static prompt labels that guide agent behavior but are not tied to learned parameters. ExRole treats roles as executable control variables that summarize behavior predictive of future utility, guide subsequent interaction, and identify trainable capacity. The framework learns prototypes from team trajectories and resolves them into instructions and markers, optionally using LoRA for parameter-efficient adaptation.

## [BENCH2ROBUST: Teaching LLM Agents to Handle Tool Failures](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.11977) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces BENCH2ROBUST, a framework that converts failure-free tool-use benchmarks into controlled stochastic environments with scenario-controlled solvability, enabling the study of LLM agents' recovery strategies. It also proposes Bayesian Tool Memory (BTM) and curriculum-controlled reinforcement learning as complementary interventions, showing robustness improvements across multiple models. This work addresses a critical gap in LLM agent robustness by simulating tool failures and studying recovery strategies, which is essential for real-world deployment where tools can fail. The findings suggest that combining environment-specific recovery knowledge with learned recovery behavior can significantly improve agent performance under tool failures, benefiting the broader AI agent ecosystem. Across 7 models from 4 families and two multi-turn benchmark families, tool failures produce a near-universal robustness gap. On held-out Retail tasks, BTM improves robustness by up to 16.8 percentage points without retraining, while RL learns complementary recovery behavior; combining both reaches 40.8-45.5% under injection while preserving failure-free performance.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 13, 04:00

**Background**: Tool-using LLM agents are typically trained and evaluated in environments where tool calls succeed reliably, but deployed tools can fail transiently, persistently, or silently. Robust recovery requires more than repeated retries: an agent may need to retry the same path, switch to an alternative, or recognize that no viable path remains. BENCH2ROBUST creates controlled environments to study these scenarios, and BTM provides structured runtime recovery context, while curriculum-controlled RL learns recovery behavior.

## [CTBench: New Benchmark for AI Agents in Telecom Troubleshooting](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.12002) ⭐️ 7.0/10

CTBench, a public benchmark introduced by researchers from Huawei Technologies and Queen Mary University of London, evaluates AI agents' troubleshooting capabilities in realistic, partially observable telecom network operations, focusing on root cause analysis and path restoration. It uses expert-grounded metrics that assess both final answers and diagnostic evidence. This benchmark addresses a critical gap in evaluating AI agents for telecom network operations, where existing evaluations fail to model real network characteristics. It provides a standardized way to measure agent performance in realistic scenarios, which could accelerate the adoption of AI in network maintenance and improve operational efficiency. CTBench tasks are constructed by experts and annotated with rich metadata, including golden evidence steps. Experiments show that state-of-the-art agents perform well at identifying endpoints in path-restoration tasks but underperform in root cause analysis, especially for interface state, link-layer, and service-management faults, and often fail to provide evidence-grounded diagnoses.

## [GUIDE: Governed Multi-Agent Framework for Enterprise Document Processing](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.12133) ⭐️ 7.0/10

GUIDE is a new governed multi-agent framework that automates extraction and artifact generation from enterprise guideline documents. It achieves 96% document success on 120 real-world documents, reducing turnaround time from 2-3 days to 40-125 minutes. This framework addresses critical challenges in enterprise AI by combining governance, provenance, and human-in-the-loop oversight, which are essential for regulated industries. It demonstrates a practical path to automating complex document workflows, potentially saving enterprises significant time and resources. GUIDE uses six specialized agents for parsing, VLM-driven extraction, consistency checking, evaluation, human-in-the-loop escalation, and artifact synthesis, all coordinated via a shared versioned rule store and schema-validated inter-agent contracts. It extracted 3,896 rules with 71.4% auto-approved and produced 812 deployment-ready artifacts.

**Background**: Enterprise guideline documents are heterogeneous and multimodal, containing narrative text, complex tables, and embedded images. Traditional LLM and VLM systems often produce hallucinated content and degrade table structures, lacking governed workflows that extend beyond extraction to validation and artifact generation. Multi-agent frameworks coordinate multiple AI agents to solve complex problems, and schema-validated contracts ensure reliable inter-agent communication.

The framework was validated on the Low-Pressure Coolant Injection system of a decommissioned Boiling Water Reactor, demonstrating consistent reconstruction across repeated runs. It enables faster and more reliable generation of executable functional models for diagnostic reasoning, safety assessment, and failure propagation analysis in high-reliability domains like nuclear power plants. The framework constructs KG-DML across the DML hierarchy using targeted retrieval, preserving functional dependencies and explicit logical relationships. A multi-level validation methodology evaluates layer-specific precision and recall, logical gate consistency, and overall structural integrity, ensuring the model's reliability.

Backtrader-Bench introduces a framework with deterministic MCQ and generator-solver pipelines to benchmark LLM coding agents on algorithmic trading, achieving 90% accuracy with tool-augmented agents (GPT-5.5 and Opus 4.7). This benchmark addresses data contamination and verification issues in evaluating LLM agents for algorithmic trading, providing a more reliable and scalable evaluation method. TRACE Bench introduces a task-driven agentic checklist evaluation framework for roleplay models, decomposing role profiles into fixed checklists and using a User Agent to converse naturally while privately updating checklist states. It achieves 99.91% coverage of role-profile points compared to 73.74% for existing free-dialogue benchmarks. This framework addresses a critical gap in roleplay evaluation by providing traceable, checklist-based scores instead of black-box holistic impressions. It enables more reliable model comparisons and supports closed-loop benchmark evolution, benefiting researchers and developers in the LLM roleplay ecosystem. The framework was tested across 26 models, showing stable rankings under repeated runs and User Agent replacement. It also supports Closed-Loop Benchmark Evolution, distilling verification methods from failed traces to improve future evaluations.

This paper introduces diffusion language models (DLMs) as a new inference paradigm for lossless text compression, aiming to overcome the throughput limitations of autoregressive LLM-based compressors. The proposed DLM-based framework achieves state-of-the-art compression ratios on the enwik8 benchmark. This work addresses a critical practical barrier—low throughput—that has prevented LLM-based lossless compression from being widely adopted. By leveraging DLMs, it could enable faster and more efficient compression of large-scale text data, benefiting data storage and transmission applications. The framework requires solving algorithmic challenges because DLMs allow the number and positions of encoded symbols per forward pass to be decided independently. Experimental results on enwik8 show that the DLM-based approach advances the state of the art, and further improvements are expected as DLM technology matures.

**Background**: Lossless text compression aims to reduce data size without losing information. Diffusion language models generate text by iteratively denoising a corrupted sequence, allowing parallel generation of multiple tokens, which could alleviate the throughput bottleneck.

## [Probability Combination Rules: Matching Formulas to Situations and Stakes](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.11275) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper systematically analyzes when different probability combination rules (averaging, multiplying odds, etc.) are appropriate, deriving the correct formulas for each situation and validating them with Monte Carlo experiments. It also shows that mismatched rules can lead to substantially different probabilities even when binary decisions at threshold 1/2 are the same. This work addresses a fundamental issue in AI/ML and decision-making, where combining probability estimates from multiple sources is common. By clarifying assumptions and providing correct formulas, it can improve the reliability of systems that rely on such combinations, such as ensemble methods, sensor fusion, and proof-based reasoning. The paper states assumptions behind several common combination rules and derives corresponding combined probabilities. It also gives probabilistic interpretations of conflicting-evidence rules and shows that for overlapping derivations, retaining identities of shared uncertain premises allows direct calculation of the probability that at least one derivation is available, while pairwise combination loses information for three or more derivations.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 13, 04:00

**Background**: Probability combination rules are used to merge multiple numerical assessments of the same yes/no question, such as when aggregating expert opinions or combining evidence in AI systems. Common rules include averaging and multiplying odds, but their validity depends on the underlying assumptions about the sources, such as conditional independence and common priors. This paper provides a rigorous framework to choose the appropriate rule based on the situation and the stakes involved.

## [Federated Learning Enables Privacy-Preserving CNC Tool Wear Prediction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.11281) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper demonstrates that federated learning can effectively predict CNC tool wear in distributed settings, achieving performance close to centralized models while preserving data privacy. The federated models significantly outperform local client baselines. This work addresses a practical industrial challenge by enabling collaborative model training across machines or organizations without sharing raw data, which is crucial for privacy-sensitive manufacturing environments. It shows that federated learning can be a viable solution for distributed industrial AI applications. The study simulates a federated learning scenario by distributing tool trajectories across simulated clients. The federated models are compared against centralized references and local client baselines, showing near-centralized performance and significant improvement over local models.

## [PINN with Implicit Neural Representations Improves Myocardial Perfusion MRI Quantification](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.11282) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper extends physics-informed neural networks (PINNs) with spatiotemporal implicit neural representations (INRs) to represent the MR signal as a continuous spatiotemporal function, improving accuracy, smoothness, and physical consistency for myocardial perfusion MRI quantification. The proposed method demonstrates improved robustness and parameter estimation accuracy over established methods on realistic simulated CMR datasets. This work addresses a challenging inverse problem in medical imaging, offering a more accurate and physically consistent approach to quantify myocardial perfusion from CMR data. Improved quantification can enhance clinical diagnosis and treatment planning for cardiac diseases, potentially impacting patient outcomes. The method uses spatiotemporal INRs to parameterize the MR signal as a continuous function of space and time, which helps overcome limitations of traditional PINNs. The code is available at https://github.com/q-cardIA/pinn-inr, and validation was performed on realistic simulated datasets, not real patient data.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 13, 04:00

**Background**: Myocardial perfusion MRI quantifies blood flow to the heart muscle by fitting tracer-kinetic models to dynamic contrast-enhanced data. Multi-compartment exchange models describe the contrast agent's evolution in tissue, but fitting them is sensitive to noise and variability. Physics-informed neural networks (PINNs) have been proposed as an alternative to conventional non-linear least squares fitting, and implicit neural representations (INRs) parameterize signals as continuous functions of coordinates, which can improve smoothness and accuracy.

## [SegPAR: Class-Centric Sparse Attack Boosts Segmentation Robustness](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.11285) ⭐️ 7.0/10

SegPAR introduces a novel decision-based black-box sparse attack framework for semantic segmentation, shifting from image-centric to class-centric exploration and introducing a discrepancy reward to improve query efficiency and sparsity. It significantly outperforms existing black-box baselines and remains competitive with white-box sparse attacks. This work addresses an underexplored area in adversarial robustness, providing a rigorous benchmark and a more efficient attack method for semantic segmentation. It highlights the vulnerability of segmentation models to sparse perturbations, which is critical for real-world applications like autonomous driving and medical imaging. The paper adapts representative decision-based black-box sparse attacks from classification to segmentation as baselines, revealing that image-centric pixel accumulation causes severe query inefficiency. SegPAR's class-centric paradigm and discrepancy reward achieve better sparsity and MIoU reduction, with code available on GitHub.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 13, 04:00

**Background**: Decision-based black-box attacks rely only on the model's output labels, making them practical for real-world scenarios where model internals are unknown. Sparse attacks modify only a small number of pixels, which is more realistic but challenging. Semantic segmentation, which classifies every pixel, is a critical task in computer vision, and its robustness against such attacks is under-researched.

## [CLEAR: Class-wise Expert Aggregation for Long-Tailed Classification](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.11287) ⭐️ 7.0/10

CLEAR is a modular ensemble framework that generates diverse experts via threshold-based structured sampling and estimates class-wise trust scores using smoothed class-wise precision. It aggregates expert predictions through class-wise generalized product-of-experts, achieving competitive accuracy and strong few-shot performance on CIFAR-100-LT, ImageNet-LT, and Places-LT. This work addresses a critical reliability issue in long-tailed classification by explicitly estimating which expert to trust for each class, a dimension often overlooked by existing methods. It offers a design principle that could improve model robustness in real-world applications with imbalanced data, such as medical imaging or rare species detection. CLEAR preserves the full label space while sampling diverse experts, and uses smoothed class-wise precision to compute trust scores. The class-wise generalized product-of-experts aggregation allows different experts to be emphasized for different classes, and experiments span multiple backbones across three benchmark datasets.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 13, 04:00

**Background**: Long-tailed classification deals with datasets where a few classes have many samples while most have few, causing models to be biased toward frequent classes. Traditional approaches include re-balancing, adjustment, representation learning, and multi-expert modeling, but they rarely consider per-class reliability. Ensemble methods combine multiple models to improve performance, and generalized product-of-experts is a technique for aggregating probabilistic predictions.

## [CQD-ERL: Contextual Quality-Diversity Evolutionary RL for HVAC Control](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.11324) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces CQD-ERL, a novel controller that combines contextual quality-diversity with evolutionary reinforcement learning for supervisory control of tropical water-cooled chiller plants. It maintains a product archive of specialized policies and uses a safety shield, demonstrating improved performance over an ASHRAE Guideline 36 baseline in annual backtests. HVAC systems account for a significant portion of building energy consumption, and improving their control efficiency can lead to substantial energy savings and reduced carbon emissions. CQD-ERL offers a promising approach that leverages quality-diversity to discover diverse, specialized policies, potentially outperforming traditional single-policy RL methods in complex, dynamic environments. The controller uses a two-tier reduced-order environment representing latent load, cooling-tower approach, and humidity constraints of a Singapore commercial building. It combines a gradient-free evolutionary operator and a soft-actor-critic policy-gradient operator sharing one replay buffer, with every action filtered through a deterministic safety shield.

## [DD-CMD: Dual-Domain Cross-Modal Decoding for Text-Guided Medical Segmentation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.11335) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces Dual-Domain Cross-Modal Decoding (DD-CMD), a method that integrates spatial and frequency domain text guidance for clinical text-guided pulmonary infection segmentation. It achieves state-of-the-art results on QaTa-COV19 and MosMedData+ datasets, with Dice scores of 91.46% and 81.95%, respectively. This work addresses a gap in text-guided medical image segmentation by incorporating frequency domain information, which is crucial for texture and boundary details. The method's consistent improvements over prior baselines suggest it could enhance clinical workflows where precise segmentation is critical. DD-CMD uses Text-Guided Spatial Cross-Attention (TGSA) for spatial alignment and Spectral-Text Adaptive Modulation (STAM) with 2D DCT and FiLM parameters for frequency-aware decoding. It embeds these into a coarse-to-fine decoder (7x7 to 56x56) and uses a lightweight two-stage refinement module, achieving average gains of +1.96 Dice and +2.67 mIoU over the strongest baselines.

**Background**: Text-guided medical image segmentation uses clinical text to narrow down target regions, but prior methods often focus on spatial alignment, neglecting frequency content that affects texture and boundaries. The method is validated on public datasets for pulmonary infection segmentation, demonstrating its practical applicability.

This paper presents the first real-world application of calibration models for offline hyperparameter selection in reinforcement learning, using a municipal water treatment plant as a case study. The authors evaluate several calibration model approaches, including a k-nearest neighbors model with a Laplacian distance metric, on high-dimensional, non-stationary sensor data. This work demonstrates that offline dynamics models can be used for hyperparameter selection in real-world industrial settings, moving beyond simple simulations. It addresses a critical practical challenge in deploying reinforcement learning, potentially enabling broader adoption in domains where online experimentation is costly or infeasible. The study uses a municipal water treatment plant as a case study, evaluating calibration models on high-dimensional, non-stationary sensor data for nexting prediction tasks. The results show that these models can generate realistic long-horizon rollouts and recover meaningful hyperparameter sensitivity trends, while also examining scalability to year-long datasets, support for fine-tuning learning rates, and robustness under distribution shift.

## [GazeAnywhere: Promptable Gaze Target Estimation with Concepts](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.11367) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces the Promptable Gaze Target Estimation (PGE) task and GazeAnywhere, the first model for this task, which uses natural language or visual prompts to specify the subject of gaze analysis in an end-to-end manner. It also presents Gaze-Co, a dataset of 120K prompt-annotated image pairs, and achieves state-of-the-art performance on multiple benchmarks. This work addresses the brittleness of multi-stage gaze estimation pipelines by unifying subject localization and gaze estimation into a single concept-driven model, enhancing flexibility and robustness. It opens new possibilities for human-AI interaction, allowing users to query gaze targets using natural language, which could benefit applications in assistive technology, human-robot interaction, and behavioral analysis. GazeAnywhere uses a transformer-based detector to fuse features from frozen encoders, simultaneously solving subject localization, in/out-of-frame presence, and gaze target heatmap estimation. The model is open-sourced on GitHub, and the Gaze-Co dataset includes prompts like 'the boy in the red shirt' or 'person in point [0.52, 0.48]'.

## [FJR+ Axiom: Efficient Verification and Computation for Committee Elections](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.11500) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces FJR+, a new proportionality axiom that strengthens both FJR and EJR+, and can be verified and satisfied in polynomial time. It also analyzes the Residual-Budget Greedy (RBG) algorithm, proving that every size-k completion of its partial committee satisfies FJR+, and extends the axiom to participatory budgeting. This work addresses a key open problem in computational social choice by providing a proportionality axiom that is both strong and computationally tractable, potentially leading to more practical and fair committee election algorithms. It also extends to participatory budgeting, broadening its impact on real-world resource allocation. The paper proves that RBG selects a partial committee such that every size-k completion satisfies FJR+, and using sequential Phragmén yields a priceable completion that always satisfies FJR+ and the sub-core. It also introduces a Droop-quota version of FJR+ and a project-specific RBG for participatory budgeting that computes the property in polynomial time.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 13, 04:00

**Background**: Approval-based committee elections aim to select a committee that proportionally represents voters. Full justified representation (FJR) is a strong proportionality axiom, but verifying it is coNP-complete. The new FJR+ axiom strengthens FJR and EJR+ while enabling polynomial-time verification and satisfaction, building on prior work on greedy algorithms and sequential Phragmén.

## [Learning to Wait: A New Paradigm for Efficient Sequential Decision Making](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.11511) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces a 'waiting policy' that allows agents to deliberately pause sensing and decision-making for set durations, trained via lexicographic reinforcement learning to minimize resource use without sacrificing task performance. Experiments across 4 discrete and 3 continuous environments show agents can wait for over 50% of task duration in some cases. This work challenges the assumption that agents must act at every timestep, offering a way to significantly reduce sensing and computational costs in real-world applications like robotics and autonomous systems. It could enable more energy-efficient and multi-tasking agents, impacting fields from household robotics to industrial automation. The approach uses lexicographic multi-objective reinforcement learning, prioritizing task performance (e.g., completion time) as the primary objective and waiting frequency as the secondary objective. The waiting policy is trained to decide both where and how long to wait, and can adapt pre-trained policies to incorporate waiting behaviors.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 13, 04:00

**Background**: Sequential decision making typically assumes agents are active closed-loop systems that observe and act at every timestep. However, many tasks, like brewing coffee, have periods where constant monitoring is unnecessary. Lexicographic reinforcement learning is a multi-objective RL variant where objectives are optimized in order of priority, allowing strict satisfaction of primary goals before secondary ones.

## [Influence Tactics in Prompts Affect LLM Code Quality](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.11513) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This study empirically tests whether psychology-based influence tactics in prompt framing affect LLM code generation quality, using eight tactics from Yukl & Falbe's taxonomy across five open-weight LLMs and two benchmarks (LiveCodeBench and SWE-bench Verified). Results show that urgency-framed prompts reduce correctness and security. This is the first large-scale empirical study of influence-induced prompt framing in software engineering tasks, highlighting how linguistic cues can shape LLM outputs. It provides practical insights for designing transparent and interpretable human-AI interactions in code generation, which is increasingly important as LLMs are integrated into developer workflows. The study operationalized eight influence tactics (e.g., rational persuasion, ingratiation, exchange) into reproducible prompt templates and evaluated them on four software quality dimensions: functional correctness, quality, maintainability, and security. Notably, urgency-framed prompts were associated with reduced correctness and security, suggesting that certain persuasive framings may have unintended negative effects.

**Background**: Prompt engineering is known to influence LLM performance, but the impact of psychologically inspired framings was unexplored. Yukl & Falbe's taxonomy is a well-known framework for influence tactics in human communication, which the authors adapted to prompt design. LiveCodeBench and SWE-bench Verified are widely used benchmarks for evaluating code generation and real-world software engineering tasks, respectively.

## [Semantic Prism: Generative Segmentation with Hierarchical Alignment](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.11537) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Semantic Prism introduces a conditional semantic-image generation-and-refinement framework with deterministic inference, using a diffusion-distilled one-step generator and hierarchical feature alignment to improve segmentation accuracy. This work addresses key limitations in generative semantic segmentation, such as color drift and boundary mixing, by introducing an explicit probabilistic interface and hierarchical alignment. It achieves significant mIoU improvements on Cityscapes and demonstrates robust transfer to other datasets, potentially advancing the field of generative models for structured prediction. On the 500-image Cityscapes validation set, Semantic Prism achieves 72.07% mIoU, 11.39 points above direct-interface decoding, with 0.41% expected calibration error. It also introduces Contextual Interface-Hierarchy Disagreement (C-IHD), a fixed readout for ranking pixel errors without auxiliary predictors, improving AUPR on ACDC from 0.6580 to 0.7557.

**Background**: Generative semantic segmentation treats structured prediction as image generation, but direct color decoding suffers from color drift and boundary mixing. Semantic Prism uses a diffusion-distilled one-step generator to render a semantic RGB image, then defines an explicit probabilistic interface via per-pixel distances to a class-color codebook. Hierarchical Generator Evidence Alignment (HGEA) aligns multi-level generator features and predicts additive residuals to the interface logits, retaining the image-defined interface as reference.

This paper introduces a closed-loop framework for video reflection removal, comprising S2R-Synthesis for physics-grounded reflection simulation, S2R-Removal as the first diffusion-based video dereflection model, and S2R-Bench, the first dedicated benchmark for video reflection removal. Video reflection removal is largely underexplored compared to single-image methods, and this work addresses key gaps by providing paired training data, a temporally coherent removal model, and a benchmark. S2R-Removal adapts a pretrained video diffusion prior with reflection-aware latent adaptation and one-step pixel-geometric refinement, enabling single-step denoising for fast inference.

## [FM-LLM: Frequency-Enhanced MoE for Time-Series Forecasting](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.11623) ⭐️ 7.0/10

FM-LLM introduces a prompt-free, frequency-aware framework that adapts frozen LLMs to time-series forecasting using a Fourier Analysis Network (FAN)-based spectral token aligner and an asymmetric Mixture-of-Experts (MoE) decoder. It achieves state-of-the-art performance on 59 out of 78 metrics across eleven benchmarks, with average improvements of 5.3% in MSE and 5.6% in MAE over the strongest autoregressive LLM baseline. This work addresses key limitations of existing LLM-based time-series forecasting methods, which rely on textual prompts and overlook spectral dynamics, by enabling prompt-free and frequency-aware adaptation. It could significantly improve forecasting accuracy and efficiency, benefiting applications in finance, energy, and other domains that rely on time-series data. The framework uses constrained asymmetric coupling: shared experts with lightweight FAN layers reconstruct the global periodic backbone, while routed experts (standard FFNs) specialize in non-periodic residual dynamics. A time-frequency hybrid loss function jointly optimizes temporal accuracy and spectral consistency, mitigating error accumulation in long-horizon autoregressive rollouts. FM-LLM also demonstrates robust transferability in 10% few-shot and zero-shot scenarios.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 13, 04:00

**Background**: Time-series forecasting is crucial in many fields, and large language models (LLMs) have recently been adapted for this task. However, existing methods often require textual prompts to align modalities, which adds computational overhead and fails to exploit the spectral (frequency) information inherent in time-series data. Fourier analysis decomposes signals into frequency components, and Fourier Analysis Networks (FAN) incorporate this idea into neural networks. Mixture-of-Experts (MoE) is a technique that uses multiple specialized sub-networks to improve model capacity and efficiency.

## [HPSE: Hybrid-Policy Self-Editing for Composable Knowledge Editing](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.11660) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces HPSE, a hybrid-policy self-editing approach that enhances the composability of unstructured knowledge editing in large language models. It casts editing as proactive self-distillation from a privileged in-context state, and uses a hybrid rollout to fill coverage gaps in on-policy distillation. This work addresses a critical limitation in knowledge editing: edited models often fail to compose new facts into multi-hop reasoning. By introducing composability as a key property and providing a plug-and-play method, it could significantly improve the practical utility of knowledge editing for dynamic LLM updates. HPSE builds a hybrid rollout that places missing facts onto the student's own trajectory precisely where coverage fails, while staying on-policy elsewhere. The method is theoretically analyzed and empirically validated across four LLM backbones and two KE editors under various scenarios.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 13, 04:00

**Background**: Knowledge editing (KE) updates specific knowledge in LLMs without altering unrelated knowledge. Unstructured KE (UKE) uses free-form passages that may state multiple facts, but existing editors often fail to make the model answer atomic questions or compose facts for multi-hop reasoning. Self-distillation is a technique where a model learns from its own outputs, and composability refers to the ability to combine building blocks into different systems.

## [Multimodal Pseudo-Labels Boost Open-Vocabulary Segmentation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.11681) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper proposes a multimodal framework that leverages pre-trained vision-language models to generate pseudo-labels, improving open-vocabulary instance and panoptic segmentation. It introduces CLIP-guided synonym filtering and GPT-based caption reconstruction to enhance visual-textual alignment. This work addresses key challenges in open-vocabulary segmentation, such as noisy pseudo-masks and handling out-of-vocabulary words, which are critical for real-world applications. The proposed method outperforms state-of-the-art approaches on COCO benchmarks, potentially advancing the field and enabling more robust segmentation systems. The framework uses Grounded SAM, LLaVA, and CLIP to construct pseudo segmentation masks, descriptive captions, and semantically aligned synonym sets. It employs three training objectives: an extended grounding loss, a semantic consistency loss, and a generative caption reconstruction loss, achieving substantial improvements on both OVIS and OSPS benchmarks.

## [APEX: Adaptive Expert Prefetching Boosts Edge MoE Inference Efficiency](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.11688) ⭐️ 7.0/10

APEX introduces an adaptive expert prefetching framework that predicts candidate experts before the attention block and dynamically fetches them using a learned confidence model, achieving over 99% overlap accuracy. It supports a correctness-preserving mode that reduces per-token latency by up to 26% and improves energy-delay product by up to 41% over state-of-the-art baselines. This work addresses a critical memory bottleneck in edge MoE inference, where expert parameters often reside in off-chip memory, causing loading delays. By overlapping expert loading with computation, APEX can significantly improve the efficiency of deploying large MoE models on resource-constrained edge devices, potentially enabling more powerful AI applications at the edge. APEX uses a lightweight prefetch router with a learned confidence model to predict experts, and offers two execution modes: a correctness-preserving mode that guarantees exact routing semantics, and a stall-free mode that eliminates residual stalls with negligible accuracy impact. The framework was evaluated across multiple MoE models, showing consistent improvements in latency and energy-delay product.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 13, 04:00

**Background**: Mixture-of-Experts (MoE) models are a class of neural networks that activate only a small subset of parameters per token, enabling high model capacity with lower compute cost. In edge deployment, expert parameters are often too large to fit in on-chip memory, so they reside in off-chip memory, and loading them becomes a bottleneck. Prefetching is a technique that anticipates data needs and loads them in advance to hide latency. APEX applies this idea to expert loading in MoE inference, using a learned model to predict which experts will be needed.

## [REOPD: Reliability-Adaptive Reward Extrapolation for On-Policy Distillation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.11698) ⭐️ 7.0/10

REOPD introduces a reliability-adaptive reward extrapolation framework for on-policy distillation, using token-level adaptive coefficients to prevent reward hacking and improve training stability. This method addresses the critical issue of reward hacking in on-policy distillation, which can destabilize training and degrade model performance. By providing a practical solution without requiring extra models, it could improve the efficiency and reliability of LLM training across various domains. REOPD combines a token-level compatibility weight with a batch-level adaptive budget, yielding a token-wise coefficient λ_b,t = 1 + γ_b q_t. It requires no verifier, reward model, value model, or extra rollout beyond standard OPD, and outperforms G-OPD on single-teacher mathematics and multi-teacher settings while matching it on single-teacher code.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 13, 04:00

**Background**: On-policy distillation (OPD) trains a student model on its own trajectories under dense token-level supervision from a teacher. Reward-extrapolation methods like ExOPD amplify the teacher-reference log-likelihood ratio to move beyond direct imitation, but they apply a single global coefficient to all tokens, which can cause reward hacking. Reward hacking occurs when an AI optimizes a proxy reward function in unintended ways, leading to poor performance relative to the true objective.

## [ViaMOBO: High-Dimensional Multi-Objective Bayesian Optimization via Variable Interaction Analysis](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.11713) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces ViaMOBO, a novel framework for high-dimensional multi-objective Bayesian optimization (MOBO) that uses variable interaction analysis to decompose the decision space into subspaces, enabling local Bayesian optimization. It outperforms state-of-the-art MOBO methods on synthetic and real-world benchmarks. This work addresses a key limitation of MOBO—its poor scalability to high-dimensional decision spaces—by providing a generic framework that can handle separable, partially separable, and non-separable problems. It has the potential to expand the applicability of MOBO to a wider range of expensive black-box optimization problems in engineering, science, and design. ViaMOBO uses a variable interaction analysis model to determine whether the decision space can be completely or partially divided, without strong assumptions about the objective function. It then performs local Bayesian optimization in the divided subspaces, which reduces sampling complexity and improves efficiency.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 13, 04:00

**Background**: Multi-objective Bayesian optimization (MOBO) is a sample-efficient approach for finding Pareto-optimal solutions to expensive black-box problems, but it struggles with high-dimensional decision spaces due to exponential sampling complexity. Variable interaction analysis examines how decision variables interact to affect the objective, which can reveal separability and guide decomposition. This paper builds on prior work like MORBO, which also addresses high-dimensional MOBO but through local modeling rather than variable interaction.

## [Multilingual API Calls: SFT Beats RL for Language Consistency](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.11715) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper identifies a new failure mode in multilingual API calling, termed Argument Language Mismatch (ALM), and introduces a benchmark to evaluate it. The authors show that supervised fine-tuning (SFT) can match or exceed more complex reinforcement learning (RL) approaches like GRPO in mitigating ALM. This work addresses a practical and under-explored problem in multilingual tool use for LLMs, which is critical for real-world API integration. The finding that SFT can be as effective as RL offers a simpler and more accessible solution for practitioners, potentially reducing the need for complex RL pipelines. The benchmark evaluates argument language consistency and end-to-end function call accuracy. While GRPO can improve language consistency and preserve general reasoning ability, these gains are incremental and most pronounced in generalization and multi-objective trade-offs.

## [Non-Invasive Fingerprinting of Text-to-Image Diffusion Models via Collapsed Generation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.11732) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces a novel framework to fingerprint text-to-image diffusion models by exploiting collapsed generation, a phenomenon where certain input conditions yield highly consistent images across stochastic seeds. The method works in both white-box and black-box access settings, enabling ownership verification without invasive watermarks. This work addresses the growing concern of intellectual property protection for proprietary diffusion models, which are often distributed as hosted services or downloadable checkpoints. By providing a non-invasive fingerprinting method, it offers a practical solution for ownership verification in cases of model leakage, copying, or unauthorized fine-tuning, potentially influencing future IP protection strategies in the AI industry. The framework prepares collapse-prone conditions on the source model and verifies suspects under two access settings: white-box pipeline access with optimized continuous embeddings, and black-box API-only access with natural language prompts. Experiments across UNet- and transformer-based diffusion models show low confusion between different source models, and fingerprints remain verifiable in fine-tuned derivatives and under common or adaptive obfuscations, with a modest verification query budget.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 13, 04:00

**Background**: Diffusion models are a class of generative models that create images by gradually denoising random noise, and they power popular tools like DALL-E, Stable Diffusion, and Midjourney. Model fingerprinting is a technique to identify the origin of a model or its outputs, often used for IP protection; existing methods typically rely on watermarks embedded in the model or its outputs, which can be invasive or vulnerable to removal. Collapsed generation refers to a phenomenon where certain inputs cause the model to produce nearly identical outputs regardless of random seeds, which this paper exploits as an intrinsic, model-specific signature.

## [First 12-CNOT Double Qubit Excitation Gate Decomposition](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.11733) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper presents the first 12-CNOT decomposition of the double qubit excitation operator, reducing the CNOT count from the previous state-of-the-art (SOTA) of 13 to 12. This new circuit also achieves the lowest CNOT depth (10) and total circuit depth (16) among all previous SOTA implementations. This improvement is significant for quantum computing efficiency because CNOT gates are typically the primary source of errors and execution time in quantum circuits. Reducing the CNOT count and depth can enhance the performance of near-term quantum algorithms, such as those used in variational quantum eigensolvers (VQE), and contribute to more efficient fault-tolerant quantum computation. The new circuit adds only 2 extra one-qubit gates compared to the lowest one-qubit gate count (11) among previous SOTA circuits, resulting in a total of 13 one-qubit gates. The decomposition is specifically for the double qubit excitation operator, which is a building block in quantum algorithms like unitary coupled cluster (UCCSD) in VQE.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 13, 04:00

**Background**: In quantum computing, CNOT (controlled-NOT) gates are essential for creating entanglement, but they are more error-prone and slower than single-qubit gates. Optimizing circuits to minimize CNOT count and depth is crucial for practical quantum computation. The double qubit excitation operator is used in variational quantum algorithms to approximate the fermionic double excitation operator, which is important for simulating molecular systems.

This paper proposes a model-based architecture that integrates software-level and hardware-level monitoring to enhance control-flow anomaly detection and attack identification. ## [LookBack: Training-Free LVLM Scoring via Visual Lookback](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.11847) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces LookBack, a training-free method for scoring LVLM responses by augmenting token likelihood with a visual lookback score, which measures how strongly each response token refers to image tokens. Across four benchmarks and three models, LookBack consistently improves Best-of-N selection over existing baselines with negligible additional overhead. This addresses a critical gap in LVLM evaluation, as existing confidence-based metrics from LLMs are insufficient for detecting image-grounded hallucinations. By providing a lightweight, training-free scoring method, LookBack can improve the reliability of LVLM outputs in practical applications, benefiting developers and users who rely on these models for image understanding tasks. LookBack augments token likelihood with a visual lookback score, which is a lightweight measure of how strongly each response token refers to image tokens. The method is training-free and can be obtained during generation, making it an efficient alternative to existing scoring approaches.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 13, 04:00

**Background**: Large Vision-Language Models (LVLMs) integrate visual perception with language generation, but they can hallucinate against the image, producing fluent responses ungrounded in what they see. Existing confidence-based metrics adopted from LLMs are insufficient for LVLMs, as removing the input image barely changes confidence-based selection, indicating that output-space confidence primarily captures textual plausibility rather than agreement with the image. LookBack addresses this by incorporating a visual lookback score to better align scoring with image content.

The paper introduces CoQui, a coordinate-conditioned quantum implicit generative adversarial network that reformulates quantum image generation as coordinate-conditioned implicit function learning, using expectation values of a color qubit to determine pixel intensities. This approach decouples image resolution from address-qubit requirements and avoids shared probability-normalization constraints. CoQui addresses key limitations of existing QGANs, such as quantum resource growth with image resolution and probability competition among pixels, potentially enabling more efficient and higher-quality quantum image generation. It also demonstrates better performance than classical baselines, highlighting the potential of quantum machine learning in generative tasks. The method uses a classical embedding network to generate input-dependent circuit parameters and evaluates a variational quantum circuit at each coordinate. Simulated experiments on two benchmark datasets show that CoQui outperforms FRQI-based generation and PQWGAN in visual and quantitative quality while using fewer qubits, and also achieves better generation quality than the corresponding classical baseline.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 13, 04:00

**Background**: Quantum generative adversarial networks (QGANs) use parameterized quantum circuits for image generation, but existing amplitude-based approaches encode pixel locations with computational-basis indices or address qubits, causing quantum resources to scale with image resolution. Implicit neural representations (INRs) parameterize signals as continuous functions of coordinates, decoupling memory from spatial resolution, and have been successful in classical computer vision. CoQui applies this concept to quantum computing, using coordinate-conditioned implicit functions to generate images.

## [DexterSQL: Deep Schema Exploration and Rule-based Correction for Text-to-SQL](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.11889) ⭐️ 7.0/10

DexterSQL introduces three novel components for prompting-based Text-to-SQL generation: a deep schema explorator, a database-agnostic rule creator, and multi-path SQL generation. It achieves state-of-the-art accuracy on BIRD-Dev, with 67.6% using GPT-OSS-120B and 71.6% and 72.2% using GPT-4o and GPT-5.2, respectively. This work addresses key limitations in prompting-based Text-to-SQL, such as coarse schema understanding and recurring failure patterns, which are common in real-world applications. By improving accuracy without fine-tuning, it offers a cost-effective solution for leveraging LLMs in database querying, benefiting both NLP and database communities. The deep schema explorator analyzes individual and joint data distributions of ambiguous columns to uncover their relationships. The rule creator mines mismatches between generated and gold SQL on training data to produce database-agnostic corrective rules, and multi-path generation uses a dependency-tree-based intermediate representation to guide SQL skeleton creation.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 13, 04:00

**Background**: Text-to-SQL aims to translate natural language questions into SQL queries. Prompting-based methods use large language models without fine-tuning, but often struggle with ambiguous schema and complex conditions. DexterSQL improves this by exploring schema details and learning from past errors, making it more robust across different databases.

## [Benchmark Framework Assesses Indian Foundation Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.11891) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces a benchmark-based framework to assess Indian foundation models across eight capability domains, comparing them to global models. It also proposes a four-dimension Benchmark Maturity Index (BMI) to score evaluation maturity. This framework helps policymakers and researchers understand India's AI capabilities and identify gaps in evaluation practices. It highlights that many apparent capability gaps may actually be due to missing benchmark participation, which is crucial for national AI funding and monitoring decisions. The study uses only publicly reported benchmark results, finding that Indian models perform well on saturated benchmarks like MMLU and MATH-500 but participate less in newer, agentic, and domain-specific evaluations. Sarvam AI reports the broadest benchmark coverage among Indian organizations.

## [Label-Free Strategies Fail to Improve LLM Multiple-Choice Accuracy](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.11947) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2608.11947) systematically tests label-free strategies for mitigating option-order bias in multiple-choice LLM benchmarks, finding that withholding option labels does not reliably improve accuracy and that eliminating positional influence alone does not yield gains. This challenges common assumptions in LLM evaluation, suggesting that simply hiding option labels is not an effective debiasing method. It provides a rigorous decomposition that could guide future benchmark design and evaluation practices. The paper evaluates two label-free strategies: generation-then-matching and scoring options in isolation (positionally unbiased by construction). Neither reliably improves accuracy; the only configuration matching baseline accuracy uses all options with an LLM matcher, yet eliminating positional influence still does not yield gains, while cyclic permutation often improves accuracy.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 13, 04:00

**Background**: Multiple-choice benchmarks are widely used to evaluate large language models (LLMs), but they conflate knowledge with sensitivity to option order, making scores unreliable. Positional bias, where models favor certain answer positions, is a known issue. Label-free strategies aim to remove this bias by preventing the model from seeing option labels while committing to an answer, but this paper shows they are ineffective.

## [TailBooster: Dual-Layer Generative Framework for Extreme Value Augmentation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.11951) ⭐️ 7.0/10

TailBooster introduces a dual-layer generative framework that augments extreme value data in air transport, combining a Tabular Variational Autoencoder with two anomaly detection layers to enforce operational validity. Evaluated on US flight records, it reduced Mean Absolute Error by 47-49% for extreme air time and 29-57% for extreme arrival delay prediction compared to conventional synthetic data. This framework addresses a critical gap in synthetic data augmentation for extreme events, which are rare but have significant operational and safety impacts in air transport. By ensuring operational validity, it enables safer and more reliable machine learning applications in safety-critical domains, potentially extending to other fields where extreme-event prediction is crucial. The framework uses an interquartile range-based statistical layer to extract extremes and an autoencoder-based cleaning layer to discard synthetic records violating the operational envelope. It is fully data-driven and model-agnostic, evaluated across six regression algorithms, and improves operational validity and utility for extreme-event prediction.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 13, 04:00

**Background**: Extreme events in air transport, such as severe delays, are rare but cause cascading disruptions, leaving insufficient training data for machine learning models. Synthetic data augmentation can help, but conventional generative models often underrepresent distributional tails and may generate operationally infeasible instances. TailBooster combines generative modeling with anomaly detection to address both issues, leveraging techniques like Tabular Variational Autoencoders and interquartile range-based extreme extraction.

## [Causal Inference for Group-Contaminated Structured Outcomes](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.11954) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces a framework for causal inference when structured outcomes like images are observed after unknown, unit-specific transformations, characterizing when quotient reduction is statistically lossless and providing exact randomization inference. It constructs a maximal invariant under integer translations and quarter turns for finite-support multichannel lattice images and demonstrates a quotient test with high power. This work addresses a novel and theoretically deep problem in causal inference with structured outcomes, providing formal characterizations of observability and losslessness. It is likely to be of high interest to researchers in causal inference and statistics, potentially improving analysis of biological imaging data where acquisition geometry can confound effects. The paper distinguishes observability from statistical losslessness, showing quotient reduction is sufficient exactly when the conditional law of raw observations given treatment, covariates, and quotient has a parameter-free version. Under the sharp null, the quotient test rejected in 0.052 of simulation replicates; at unit effect strength its power was 0.992, and the primary RxRx1 contrast had an enumerated paired-swap p-value of 0.0078.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 13, 04:00

**Background**: Structured potential outcomes such as microscopy images may be recorded after an unknown, unit-specific transformation, which can depend on treatment, covariates, or the intrinsic outcome, mixing biological effects with acquisition geometry. The paper studies the unrestricted observation model X = Γ · Y(A) and characterizes observable information: a target is uniformly recoverable exactly when it is constant on group orbits, while a Borel maximal invariant retains every measurable invariant target. Concepts like Borel maximal invariants and Blackwell equivalence are central to the theoretical framework.

This paper introduces uncertainty-aware probabilistic constrained clustering (UPCC), formalizing real-valued, entangled pairwise supervision with a canonical aleatoric target. It proposes ProbPair, an angular pairwise objective, and ECI-PP, an estimator-corrector-integrator framework that outperforms state-of-the-art deep constrained clustering methods. This work addresses a critical gap in constrained clustering by moving beyond hard must-link/cannot-link constraints to handle realistic, uncertain supervision. It provides a principled framework with theoretical identifiability analysis, potentially improving clustering performance in applications where expert labels are noisy or ambiguous. The ECI-PP framework consists of belief estimation, correction, and reliability-aware integration stages, refining imperfect supervision. Experiments across diverse benchmarks show robustness with a shared default configuration, and the paper includes conditional identifiability analysis of the aleatoric target.
