## [Object-Centric World Models: Better Slots Improve Planning Robustness](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.12078) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper presents a controlled study of object-centric world models (OCWMs) for visual model-predictive control, finding that planning success correlates positively with unsupervised slot-quality metrics (FG-ARI, mBO), though gains saturate at high slot quality. It also shows that with well-bound slots, auxiliary proprioception inputs and masking inductive bias become unnecessary, and that OCWMs with well-bound slots are more robust than scene-centric LeWM under distribution shifts. This research addresses a gap in understanding how object-centric representation quality affects planning and generalization in world models, providing empirical insights that can guide the design of more robust model-based reinforcement learning agents. The findings suggest that focusing on improving slot quality and leveraging pretrained features can yield significant robustness gains, which is valuable for real-world applications where distribution shifts are common. The study compares OCWMs with scene-centric LeWM and DINO-WM, which uses frozen pretrained features, under unseen distribution shifts. Key findings include that planning success saturates at high slot quality, and that well-bound slots eliminate the need for auxiliary inputs and masking biases, while pretrained features are a key contributor to robustness.

**Background**: Object-centric representations decompose a scene into a set of slots that bind to objects, serving as an inductive bias for world models to improve sample efficiency and generalization. Metrics like FG-ARI and mBO measure the quality of slot-based segmentation, indicating how well slots correspond to actual objects.

## [QV-PIC: Query-Aware Visual Caching Boosts RAG Efficiency](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.12121) ⭐️ 7.0/10

QV-PIC introduces a query-aware dual-resolution caching framework that reuses visual token caches in RAG serving, improving average F1 by 21.6 points over vanilla rendered-image PIC and reducing TTFT by 83.8% relative to full prefill. This addresses a critical performance bottleneck in RAG serving by reducing redundant computations while maintaining quality, potentially enabling faster and more cost-effective deployment of RAG systems in production. The framework uses model-native templates to compile visual caches offline, and online it preserves global context with low resolution while restoring fine-grained textual evidence within a high-resolution budget using cumulative query relevance scores. It surpasses optimized text PIC by 2.58 F1 while reducing TTFT by 17.2%.

**Background**: Retrieval-Augmented Generation (RAG) repeatedly prefills identical text chunks across queries, causing redundant computations. Position-Independent Caching (PIC) mitigates this by reusing precomputed Key-Value (KV) caches across positions, but its efficiency is limited by the large volume of text tokens. Rendering text as images compresses tokens into fewer visual tokens, but this can degrade quality due to contextual mismatches and loss of fine-grained textual details.

## [Ready Cohorts: Formalizing GPU Opportunity in LLM-Agent Control](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.12123) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces the 'ready-cohort' boundary to formalize when GPU execution is feasible in LLM-agent control paths, and proposes exact algorithms to compute the offline share P*. It also demonstrates that keeping GPU-computed route decisions on-device avoids host round trips and is faster across all tested configurations. This work provides a measurable framework for optimizing GPU utilization in LLM-agent services, which are increasingly common but often suffer from inefficient control-path scheduling. By quantifying the opportunity and demonstrating the benefits of device-resident decisions, it offers practical guidance for system designers and could improve latency and throughput in real-world agent deployments. The paper defines metrics F, P*, U, and A to characterize the ready-cohort boundary, and uses a dynamic program to compute P* exactly under idealized assumptions. In a simulation with 100,000 active sessions, exact packing recovers 81.83% of lost opportunity, and device-resident routing is 1.19x to 2.39x faster than host round trips across 36 configurations.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 13, 04:00

**Background**: LLM-agent services repeatedly execute small deterministic transitions between model and tool calls, such as routing outcomes and updating state. This control path often involves CPU-GPU communication overhead, and scheduling these small tasks on GPUs can be inefficient. The paper formalizes when enough concurrent work exists to justify GPU execution, and explores the impact of keeping decisions on-device.

## [M-Net: Adding Mathematical Inductive Biases to U-Net for Medical Image Segmentation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.12196) ⭐️ 7.0/10

M-Net integrates spectral features (condition number of local pixel matrices) and physical field operators (divergence and curl-like operators) into U-Net, along with a Math-Attention Gate (MAG) to fuse these mathematical features with CNN features. On LiTS, KiTS, and BraTS benchmarks, it achieves Dice scores of 78.42%, 76.15%, and 83.67%, outperforming baseline U-Net by 12.37%, 3.52%, and 5.55% respectively. This work demonstrates that explicit mathematical inductive biases can significantly improve medical image segmentation, offering a complementary approach to purely data-driven deep learning. It opens new avenues for incorporating linear algebra and vector calculus into deep architectures, potentially benefiting other imaging tasks and improving performance where annotated data is limited. The condition-number feature provides a 2.14% gain over binary invertibility features, and MAG adds 1.45% over simple concatenation. The method uses a differentiable measure of texture ill-conditioning and captures focal intensity extrema and edge non-smoothness via physical field operators.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 13, 04:00

**Background**: Medical image segmentation often relies on deep learning models like U-Net, which learn features purely from data. However, medical images have inherent mathematical structures, such as texture and edge properties, that can be captured by concepts like the condition number (a measure of matrix ill-conditioning) and vector calculus operators like divergence and curl. By explicitly integrating these mathematical priors, M-Net aims to enhance the model's ability to segment anatomical structures.

## [NetlistBench: New Benchmark Tests LLM Reliability on SPICE Netlists](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.12197) ⭐️ 7.0/10

NetlistBench, a structure-verified benchmark with 2,342 cases across 24 task families, has been introduced to evaluate LLM reliability in SPICE netlist recognition and manipulation. It reveals significant performance variation with structural complexity, from 96-100% accuracy on simple local edits to 41-83% on device addition and 49-90% on equivalence judgment. This benchmark addresses a critical gap in evaluating LLMs for circuit design, separating netlist reliability from high-level design reasoning. It highlights a distinct bottleneck for trustworthy LLM-based circuit design automation, impacting researchers and practitioners in EDA and AI-assisted design. The benchmark uses a deterministic structure-aware oracle for evaluation, covering parameter and connectivity recognition, edits, hierarchical operations, equivalence judgment, and long-horizon compound editing. Enabling reasoning improves weaker models but does not eliminate structure-preservation failures, with performance degrading sharply as edit horizon increases.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 13, 04:00

**Background**: SPICE netlists are textual descriptions of circuit topology and parameters, used by simulators to model electronic circuits. LLMs are increasingly applied in circuit design workflows, but their reliability on these simulator-facing netlists is poorly understood. NetlistBench provides a structured way to assess this reliability, using a deterministic oracle to verify outputs.

## [Hybrid Learning-Optimization Planning for Automated Driving](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.12198) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces a hybrid planning architecture for automated driving that combines a deep neural network for behavior proposal with an optimization-based supervision layer to enforce safety and drivability constraints. The approach was evaluated in open-loop studies on real-world urban data and deployed on the research vehicle 'karl'. This work addresses the critical challenge of balancing the flexibility of learning-based methods with the safety guarantees of classical approaches in automated driving. By demonstrating real-world deployment, it provides a practical pathway toward trustworthy and explainable autonomous driving systems. The architecture uses a deep neural network to interpret complex traffic scenes and propose driving behavior, while an optimization-based layer validates the proposal and enforces explicit constraints. The evaluation includes open-loop studies on real-world urban data and discusses system integration for stable closed-loop operation.

**Background**: Automated driving motion planning traditionally relies on optimization-based or rule-based methods, which are verifiable but may struggle in complex environments. Learning-based approaches offer flexibility but lack transparency and safety guarantees. Hybrid planning architectures aim to combine the strengths of both paradigms, as surveyed in recent literature. Open-loop evaluation tests planners on pre-recorded data, while closed-loop evaluation involves real-time interaction with the environment.

## [SCOUT Framework Enhances Spatial Reasoning in Vision-Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.12220) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces SCOUT, a framework combining structured chain-of-thought (CoT) with multi-objective process reward reinforcement learning to improve spatial reasoning in vision-language models (VLMs). It also presents SCOUT-24k, a synthesized dataset for training, and reports that SCOUT-7B outperforms GPT-4o by 4.28% on spatial benchmarks. Spatial reasoning is a critical bottleneck for VLMs, and SCOUT addresses it with a novel approach that improves credit assignment and 3D understanding. This could advance applications in robotics, autonomous driving, and embodied AI, where accurate spatial perception is essential. SCOUT-3B improves baseline models by 16.85% on general spatial benchmarks and 6.3% on complex spatial reasoning tasks. Despite training only on single images, SCOUT-7B generalizes to multi-image and video scenarios, indicating robust out-of-domain performance.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 13, 04:00

**Background**: Vision-language models (VLMs) often struggle with spatial reasoning, which involves understanding object positions, orientations, and 3D relationships. Reinforcement learning (RL) methods use verifiable outcomes to improve reasoning, but they face the credit assignment problem—determining which intermediate steps contributed to the final reward. Structured chain-of-thought (CoT) approaches decompose reasoning into explicit steps, but often lack depth perception for 3D understanding. SCOUT combines these ideas by introducing a structured CoT that models 3D perception and a multi-objective process reward to assign credit more precisely.

## [Few-Shot Ordinal Learning for Hyperspectral Fish Freshness Estimation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.12230) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces the first few-shot learning framework for day-wise fish freshness estimation from hyperspectral images, using ordinal regression and biological constraints to handle scarce labeled data. On a 16-day salmon HSI dataset, the method achieves a mean absolute error of 1.58 days and 72.3% 2-day accuracy with only three labeled days per fillet. This work addresses a practical challenge in food quality assessment where labeled data is scarce and expensive to obtain. By enabling few-shot learning for hyperspectral imaging, it could reduce the cost and effort required for deploying such systems in real-world food supply chains, potentially improving food safety and reducing waste. The framework treats each fillet as a distinct episodic task and uses a CORAL-style ordinal prediction head with cumulative threshold modeling. Biologically grounded monotonicity and embedding smoothness constraints guide predictions toward plausible trajectories, and the method outperforms scalar regression and label-distribution baselines under a strict unseen-fillet protocol.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 13, 04:00

**Background**: Hyperspectral imaging (HSI) captures spectral signatures linked to biochemical changes during storage, enabling non-destructive food quality assessment. However, existing deep learning approaches require densely annotated training sets, which are costly to obtain. Few-shot learning, particularly episodic training, allows models to learn from very few examples per task, making it suitable for scenarios with scarce labels. Ordinal regression, such as CORAL, models ranked outcomes by predicting cumulative probabilities, which is appropriate for freshness levels that progress monotonically over time.

## [HAMP-LIC: Hessian-Aware Mixed-Precision PTQ for Learned Image Compression](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.12239) ⭐️ 7.0/10

HAMP-LIC introduces a Hessian-aware mixed-precision post-training quantization (PTQ) framework with a four-stage optimization strategy for learned image compression (LIC) models. It achieves up to 4.85x model compression with as little as 0.59% BD-rate loss on models like Minnen2018 and Cheng2020. This work addresses a critical deployment challenge for LIC models—high computational complexity and cross-platform encoding-decoding mismatches—by enabling efficient low-bit deployment. It could significantly reduce the memory and computational footprint of LIC models, making them more practical for real-world applications on heterogeneous hardware. The framework estimates block-wise sensitivity from the Hessian trace, refines it with a task-aware module, allocates bit widths under a global model-size constraint, and performs block-wise reconstruction with a small calibration set. It completely eliminates cross-platform encoding-decoding errors and consistently outperforms existing fixed- and mixed-precision PTQ methods across multiple datasets.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 13, 04:00

**Background**: Learned image compression (LIC) uses deep neural networks to achieve better rate-distortion performance than traditional codecs, but suffers from high computational cost and hardware heterogeneity issues. Post-training quantization (PTQ) reduces model size and speeds up inference without retraining, but uniform fixed-precision quantization often degrades quality at low bit widths. Hessian-based methods, like HAWQ, use second-order information to guide mixed-precision quantization, assigning higher bits to sensitive layers.

## [NA-UNETR: Neighborhood Attention Transformer for LAD Artery Segmentation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.12274) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces NA-UNETR, a 3D transformer-based segmentation model that integrates Neighborhood Attention (NA) and Dilated NA (DiNA) blocks to improve delineation of the left anterior descending (LAD) artery in low-contrast CT images. It achieves 45.64% Dice, 38.16 mm HD95, and 10.01 mm ASD on institutional data, outperforming nnU-Net and Swin UNETR in key metrics. Accurate LAD segmentation is critical for cardiac dose sparing in thoracic radiotherapy, but the vessel's small size and poor contrast make it challenging. This work demonstrates that transformer-based architectures with local-global attention can significantly improve boundary accuracy, potentially enhancing treatment planning and reducing cardiac complications. The model is pretrained on 1,000 CTA volumes and fine-tuned with LoRA-based parameter-efficient adaptation on 20 free-breathing CT scans. A composite Dice-Focal and Hausdorff loss, dynamically balanced via homoscedastic uncertainty, improves overlap and boundary accuracy. On ImageCAS, it achieved 79.49% Dice, 8.89 mm HD95, and 1.02 mm ASD.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 13, 04:00

**Background**: Neighborhood Attention (NA) is a sliding-window attention mechanism that localizes each query's receptive field to its nearest neighboring tokens, offering efficiency and translational equivariance. Dilated NA (DiNA) extends this by dilating neighborhoods to capture longer-range dependencies. LoRA (Low-Rank Adaptation) is a parameter-efficient fine-tuning technique that updates only small low-rank matrices, reducing memory and computational overhead. Uncertainty-guided optimization uses homoscedastic uncertainty to dynamically balance multiple loss terms, improving robustness in medical image segmentation.

The paper introduces the 'Agentic Self-Improvement' framework, which uses a multimodal LLM and Bayesian optimization to iteratively refine prompts and co-optimize stochastic seeds and CFG scales for black-box image-to-video generation. In human preference studies, videos generated with this approach achieved win rates up to 69% over baseline outputs. This framework addresses the significant pain point of trial-and-error in professional image-to-video workflows, enhancing predictability and control. It moves the field toward reliable, production-ready tools, potentially benefiting content creators and automated video production pipelines. The framework uses Davidsonian Scene Graph (DSG) queries for semantic adherence and Common Mistake Questions (CMQ) for artifact detection, with a novel Video-Text Adherence (VTA) score guiding the search. It outperforms unguided search methods in human preference studies, achieving win rates up to 69%.

## [RCI Framework Converts Sparse Feedback to Dense Costs for Safe Offline RL](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.12306) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces the Redistribution-based Cost Inference (RCI) framework, which converts sparse trajectory-level stop-feedback into dense per-step cost estimates via return decomposition, enabling constrained offline RL on augmented datasets. Experiments on highway driving and robotic manipulation show lower violation rates than sparse and classifier-based baselines. This work addresses a practical gap in safe offline RL, where supervisors often provide only sparse trajectory-level feedback rather than dense per-step costs. By enabling lossless transformation and better-conditioned cost critic learning, it could improve the applicability of safe RL in real-world scenarios like autonomous driving and robotics. The RCI framework is theoretically shown to preserve the feasible policy set and optimal Lagrangian in a CMDP, making the transformation lossless. It also demonstrates robustness to heterogeneous dataset compositions and label noise, with empirical improvements over baselines.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 13, 04:00

**Background**: Safe offline reinforcement learning (RL) typically assumes access to dense per-step cost annotations, but in practice supervisors may only provide trajectory-level stop-feedback—a binary signal at the first unsafe transition. This is a temporal credit assignment problem, where the challenge is to attribute long-term outcomes to specific actions. Return decomposition is a technique used to break down trajectory returns into per-step contributions, which RCI leverages to infer dense costs.

## [DreamFly: Diffusion-Based Aerial VLN with Causal Memory and Receding-Horizon Planning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.12308) ⭐️ 7.0/10

DreamFly introduces a diffusion-based aerial vision-language navigation (VLN) framework that integrates a causally aligned historical memory and receding-horizon diffusion planning. It achieves state-of-the-art performance on the OpenFly benchmark, with 32.04%/29.46% SR and 28.22%/23.54% SPL on test-seen/test-unseen splits. This work addresses key challenges in aerial VLN—limited historical context, short planning horizons, and unreliable termination—by jointly modeling temporal reasoning, future action structure, and explicit stop prediction. It demonstrates a promising direction for embodied AI and aerial robotics, potentially improving autonomous navigation in complex, partially observable environments. DreamFly builds on Dream-VLA and uses a plan-K, execute-one strategy, where the policy predicts a K-step action chunk but executes only the first action before replanning. LiteStop estimates stop probability directly from action logits at the initial all-mask state, decoupling termination from action generation.

**Background**: Aerial vision-language navigation (VLN) requires an agent to integrate visual evidence over time and plan actions under partial observability. Recent VLA (vision-language-action) models offer a perception-to-action paradigm, but adapting them to aerial navigation is challenging. Diffusion models have been used for robot planning due to their ability to capture rich action distributions, and receding-horizon planning is a common technique in robotics for balancing adaptability and safety.

## [Causal Agent Framework Enhances LLM Causal Reasoning on Tabular Data](https://arxiv.org/abs/2408.06849) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces the Causal Agent, a novel agent framework that equips large language models (LLMs) with causal tools, memory, and reasoning modules to perform causal reasoning on tabular data. It also presents the CausalTQA benchmark with about 1.4K questions across four levels of causal problems, achieving accuracy above 80% and outperforming the previous SOTA on the QRData dataset by 6%. This work bridges the gap between LLMs' natural language strengths and the structural mismatch of tabular causal data, enabling more accurate causal reasoning. It provides a practical framework that could influence future research in both LLM applications and causal inference, potentially improving automated decision-making in fields like healthcare and economics. The Causal Agent consists of three modules: tools (calling Python code with encapsulated causal functions), memory (maintaining a dictionary of causal graphs), and reasoning (iterative interaction with tools). The CausalTQA benchmark includes variable-level, edge-level, causal graph-level, and causal effect-level questions, and the code is available on GitHub.

**Background**: Large language models (LLMs) have shown remarkable success in natural language tasks, but causal reasoning on tabular data remains challenging due to the difficulty of describing causal methods in natural language and the structural mismatch between tabular data and LLMs' text-centric processing. Causal inference involves discovering causal relationships and estimating causal effects, which are critical for decision-making. Agent frameworks, which combine LLMs with external tools and memory, have emerged as a promising approach to extend LLMs' capabilities to structured data and complex reasoning tasks.

OpenAg is a new comprehensive AI framework that integrates domain-specific foundation models, neural knowledge graphs, multi-agent reasoning, causal explainability, and adaptive transfer learning to provide context-aware agricultural intelligence. The system includes a unified agricultural knowledge base integrating scientific literature, sensor data, and farmer knowledge; a neural agricultural knowledge graph for structured reasoning; an adaptive multi-agent reasoning system; and a causal transparency mechanism. ## [Deep Fictitious Play-Based Potential Differential Games for Human-Like Driving at Unsignalized Intersections](https://arxiv.org/abs/2506.12283) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces a Deep Fictitious Play-based Potential Differential Game (DFP-PDG) framework for learning human-like interactive driving policies at unsignalized intersections, and it is the first to train such policies using Deep Fictitious Play. The framework provides theoretical convergence guarantees to a Nash equilibrium and is validated on the INTERACTION dataset. This work bridges game theory and deep learning to model complex vehicle interactions, which is crucial for autonomous driving in real-world scenarios. By learning from naturalistic data and providing convergence guarantees, it offers a more human-like and theoretically sound approach to interaction modeling, potentially improving safety and efficiency at unsignalized intersections. The framework models vehicle interactions as a differential game, reformulated as a potential differential game, with cost function weights learned from the dataset to capture diverse driving styles. The ablation study highlights the importance of each component, and the code is publicly available at https://github.com/zeonchen/DFP-PDG.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 13, 04:00

**Background**: Game theory provides a mathematical framework for modeling strategic interactions, where Nash equilibrium is a key concept representing a stable state where no player can benefit by unilaterally changing their strategy. Differential games extend this to dynamic systems, and potential differential games simplify the analysis by associating the game with an optimal control problem. Deep Fictitious Play is a scalable algorithm that combines deep neural networks with the fictitious play method to find Nash equilibria in complex games.

## [Open-Weight LLMs Show Limited Explicit Feature Recovery but Strong Surrogate Power for Optimization](https://arxiv.org/abs/2512.13374) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This preprint investigates whether frozen, open-weight LLMs can capture combinatorial optimization problem structure for feature recovery and algorithm selection. Experiments across five models (3B-120B parameters) show limited explicit feature extraction but strong predictive power of hidden-layer representations, comparable to traditional feature extraction. This work explores a novel intersection of LLMs and combinatorial optimization, suggesting that LLM representations can serve as effective surrogate descriptors for downstream optimization tasks. It could enable automation in optimization without specialized feature engineering, though explicit feature recovery remains a challenge. The study spans four benchmark problems, three instance representations, and five open-weight models from Llama instruct and GPT reasoning families, including a chain-of-thought study on Llama models. Results show a consistent gap between implicit encoding and explicit retrieval, with model scale attenuating the gap but not closing it.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 13, 04:00

**Background**: Combinatorial optimization involves finding the best solution from a finite set of discrete options, often requiring algorithm selection based on problem features. Open-weight LLMs are models with publicly released parameters, allowing local use and fine-tuning. Probing analysis is a technique to inspect what information is encoded in neural network hidden layers.

## [FlowAgent: Continuous Flow for Agentic Reasoning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.07339) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces FlowAgent, a novel method that reconceptualizes tool chaining as continuous trajectory generation in semantic space using conditional flow matching, and proposes the first plan-level closed-loop benchmark for evaluating agentic reasoning in dynamic environments. This work addresses limitations of step-wise tool chaining methods, such as error accumulation and poor generalization, by providing a global planning perspective. It could improve the robustness and adaptability of LLM-based agents in long-horizon tasks, impacting the broader agentic AI ecosystem. FlowAgent leverages conditional flow matching to generate continuous latent trajectories, with theoretical bounds on utility convergence and guarantees of robust generalization and error attenuation. The new benchmark is plan-level and closed-loop, designed for dynamic real-world environments.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 13, 04:00

**Background**: Large Language Models (LLMs) have shown remarkable ability in orchestrating tools for reasoning tasks, but existing methods rely on step-wise paradigms that lack a global perspective, causing error accumulation over long horizons and restricting generalization to unseen tools. Conditional flow matching is a training method for continuous normalizing flows that learns deterministic vector fields to map between distributions, offering a simulation-free and robust training procedure. This paper applies this concept to tool chaining, treating it as continuous trajectory generation in semantic space.

## [RedditPersona: Modular Framework for Community-Conditioned LLM Adaptation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.06027) ⭐️ 7.0/10

RedditPersona is a new modular framework that standardizes data collection, community definition, and evaluation for community-conditioned LLM adaptation. It applies five grouping strategies to 112 subreddits and trains QLoRA adapters, revealing a consistent trade-off between identifiability and distributional similarity. This framework addresses the lack of standardization in community-conditioned LLM adaptation, enabling fair comparisons across studies. It provides a systematic evaluation of grouping strategies, which is valuable for researchers in social media NLP and LLM adaptation. The study uses 301,429 user profiles and over 16 million comments from 112 subreddits in the urban well-being domain. The framework includes five grouping strategies: subreddit-based, graph-structural, semantic, hybrid, and interaction-based, and evaluates adapters on fluency, fidelity, distributional alignment, and community identifiability.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 13, 04:00

**Background**: Community-conditioned language model adaptation involves tailoring LLMs to specific online communities, but previous studies made independent choices about data, community definition, and evaluation, hindering comparability. QLoRA is a parameter-efficient fine-tuning method that quantizes the base model to 4-bit precision and uses low-rank adapters, reducing memory usage while preserving performance. RedditPersona standardizes these choices to enable systematic comparison.

The paper introduces SPIRE, a framework that formulates page-level slide personalization as an inverse planning problem, using structural denoising and multi-agent reinforcement learning to infer latent design intents without tool-specific knowledge. This addresses a practical gap in AI-generated presentations, as current methods struggle with fine-grained page-level design. SPIRE's principled approach could improve the quality and personalization of automated slide generation, benefiting users who rely on AI for presentation creation. SPIRE corrupts visual structures of clean slides to create a denoising task, where two agents collaboratively refine executable designs via reinforcement learning. The paper provides a proof that structural denoising is a consistent surrogate for PSP and that the multi-agent formulation reduces policy gradient variance.

## [SportD: Benchmarking Strategic Decision-Making in Vision-Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.14616) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces SportD, a dataset of 1,415 soccer decision scenarios, and shows that vision-language models (VLMs) select optimal actions only about 30% of the time, exhibiting risk aversion and conflating success probability with value. This work provides a rigorous benchmark for evaluating physical strategic decision-making in VLMs, revealing systematic biases that could impact real-world applications such as autonomous driving or robotics. It highlights a critical gap between perception and decision-making in current models. Frontier VLMs place the highest-success-probability action among their top choices in 83-92% of cases, yet they assign higher value to more likely actions (Spearman correlation 0.30 to +0.52), while ground truth shows no such relationship (-0.08). The conservatism stems from a mis-calibration of value, not a failure to identify successful actions.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 13, 04:00

**Background**: Vision-language models (VLMs) combine computer vision and natural language processing to interpret images and text. While they excel at describing scenes, their ability to make strategic decisions in dynamic environments is less studied. Soccer provides an objective testbed with quantifiable action values, making it ideal for evaluating decision-making. This benchmark addresses the need for more holistic VLM evaluation beyond perception tasks.

## [Novel Geometric Belief Interface for Secure EHR Interoperability](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.10300) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces a Geometric Belief Interface (GBI) that uses logit boundaries and sheaf-theoretic diagnostics to create a secure, self-contained substrate for EHR interoperability, along with a Decentralized Cryptographic Sheaf-Enclave (DCSE) protocol sketch. It also presents GBI BoundaryBench v0.1, a synthetic benchmark where Qwen3-4B-Instruct-2507 achieved zero coverage due to strict admission checks. This work addresses a critical challenge in healthcare IT: secure and reliable EHR interoperability. By introducing a mathematically rigorous framework that separates model proposals from deterministic admission, it could improve trust and safety in AI-assisted clinical workflows, potentially influencing future standards and system designs. The framework combines finite boundary semantics, local Dirichlet evidence, cellular-sheaf and mapping-cone diagnostics, and advisory geometric audit charts. The benchmark results show that all 768 executions were rejected (369 during parsing, 399 during schema validation), highlighting the strictness of the admission boundary.

**Background**: EHR interoperability is a complex problem involving legacy systems, generative models, and human reviewers. The mapping cone is a topological construction used to study maps and their properties.

## [HexEval: Evidence-Driven Hexagonal Framework for Scholar Assessment](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.10584) ⭐️ 7.0/10

HexEval introduces an evidence-driven hexagonal framework for multidimensional scholar assessment using large language models (LLMs), evaluating both intrinsic research quality and external scholarly behavior. It preserves intermediate evidence and dimension-specific rationales to enable interpretable and auditable scholar profiles. This framework addresses a gap in current scholar assessment methods, which rely heavily on bibliometric indicators and reputation proxies. By enabling evidence-driven reasoning over heterogeneous scholarly evidence, it has the potential to improve hiring, funding, and promotion decisions with more transparent and auditable AI assistance. HexEval organizes assessment into two layers: an intrinsic layer evaluating research rigor, methodological innovation, and scientific contribution on anonymized works, and an external layer using evidence from GitHub, Lens, OpenAlex, and other sources to assess knowledge translation, research coherence, and academic impact. Experiments show dimension-dependent agreement with human or external criteria, with structured calibration improving absolute agreement for intrinsic quality.

## [Generative Transformer Synthesizes Human Mobility Patterns](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.17468) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers propose a generative Transformer model that synthesizes daily activity chains and trajectories using socio-demographic and household attributes, trained on open-source household travel survey data and transferable to new regions. The model achieves high accuracy in traffic simulations, with a cosine similarity of 0.997 for activity locations and a MAPE of 4.97% for vehicle-miles-traveled compared to the SCAG Activity-Based Model. This approach addresses limitations of existing deep learning and activity-based models by using open-source data and transfer learning, making it more adaptable and cost-effective for regions with limited travel data. It provides urban planners and policymakers with improved tools for simulating transportation systems, supporting decisions in urban development and public health. The model was trained on national household travel survey data and fine-tuned with local data, transferring effectively to California, Washington, and Mexico City. Its practical utility was demonstrated through large-scale traffic simulations in Los Angeles County, achieving a MAPE of 5.85% for traffic volume and 4.36% for speed on California's I-405 corridor compared to real-world Caltrans PeMS observations.

## [Semantic Inequivalence Game Trains LLMs for Code Reasoning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.03818) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces SInQ, a semantic inequivalence game where a generator LLM creates semantically distinct code variants and an evaluator LLM identifies inputs that differentiate them, training each other semi-adversarially. The method improves vulnerability detection in C/C++ despite training only on Python, and shows substantial gains on the Python builtin identifier swap benchmark. This work addresses the challenge of generating high-quality code reasoning training data, which is often scarce and expensive. By enabling LLMs to improve their code understanding and vulnerability detection across languages, it could enhance AI-assisted software engineering and security analysis. The game has no theoretical performance cap, as it can generate arbitrarily complex code variants. The authors release code and synthetic data for fine-tuning LLMs, and the method shows cross-language transfer from Python to C/C++.

## [CoLoRA: Efficient CNN Fine-Tuning via Convolutional Low-Rank Adaptation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.18315) ⭐️ 7.0/10

CoLoRA introduces a parameter-efficient fine-tuning method for convolutional neural networks by extending LoRA to convolutional layers, decomposing kernel updates into depthwise and pointwise components. This reduces trainable parameters by over 80% while preserving performance on OCT image classification tasks. This work provides a deployment-efficient alternative to full fine-tuning for CNNs, which is significant for resource-constrained environments such as medical imaging. It extends the popular LoRA technique beyond transformers, potentially influencing future research on parameter-efficient adaptation for convolutional architectures. Experiments on MedMNIST datasets (especially OCTMNISTv2) with VGG16 and ResNet50 show competitive performance with substantial parameter reduction. The method allows learned updates to be merged into pretrained kernels, preserving original model size and inference complexity, but peak GPU-memory measurements show parameter efficiency does not directly translate to proportional memory savings.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 13, 04:00

**Background**: LoRA (Low-Rank Adaptation) is a parameter-efficient fine-tuning technique that introduces low-rank matrices to adapt large models without updating all parameters. CoLoRA extends this concept to convolutional layers by decomposing kernel updates into depthwise and pointwise components, enabling efficient fine-tuning of CNNs. The method is evaluated on medical imaging datasets like OCTMNISTv2, which are part of the MedMNIST collection.

## [Uncertainty-Aware LiDAR Change Detection for City-Scale 3D Mapping](https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.21112) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces an uncertainty-aware, object-centric method for city-scale LiDAR-based 3D change detection, aligning multi-temporal point clouds with multi-resolution NDT and point-to-plane ICP, and refining associations via semantic segmentation and class-constrained bipartite matching. On the Subiaco, Western Australia dataset (2023 vs 2025), it achieves 95.3% accuracy, 90.8% macro F1, and 82.9% macro IoU, outperforming the Triplet KPConv baseline by 0.3, 0.6, and 1.1 percentage points respectively. This work addresses key limitations in existing change detection methods, such as sensitivity to vertical bias and viewpoint mismatch, and the assumption of perfect alignment. By providing a more robust and accurate approach, it can significantly improve urban planning, municipal compliance monitoring, and asset management, including the tracking of built structures and urban greenery. The method uses multi-resolution NDT and point-to-plane ICP for alignment, normalizes elevation, and computes per-point detection levels from registration covariance and surface roughness to calibrate change decisions. It employs tiled processing to bound memory and preserve narrow ground changes, and instance-level decisions integrate overlap, displacement, and volumetric differences under local detection gating.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 13, 04:00

REVERE is a new self-adapting agent framework that learns from a Global Training Context, distills cross-repository failure modes into reusable heuristics, and applies targeted code-based edits to agent prompts. It improves over expert-crafted instructions by 4.50% on SUPER, 1.3% on ResearchCodeBench, and 4.89% on ScienceAgentBench, while being nearly 10x lower cost and 2.7x faster adaptation than existing baselines. This work addresses key limitations in prompt optimization—poor generalization and unstable adaptation—which are especially problematic in research-coding workflows. By enabling agents to learn from global context and evolve their prompts efficiently, REVERE could significantly improve the performance and cost-effectiveness of AI agents in complex software engineering and scientific research tasks. REVERE is a lightweight framework that uses a Global Training Context to distill recurring failure modes into heuristics, and applies targeted, code-based edits to prompts rather than full rewrites. It is evaluated across long-horizon and single-shot benchmarks, showing consistent improvements while being nearly 10x lower cost and 2.7x faster adaptation than existing prompt-optimization baselines.

## [LLMs Capture Social Meaning Structure but Distort Magnitude; Pragmatic Prompting Helps](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.02512) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces two calibration metrics, the Effect Size Ratio (ESR) and Calibration Deviation Score (CDS), to evaluate how well LLMs approximate human social meaning quantitatively, and tests pragmatically motivated prompting strategies across three frontier LLMs. This work provides a framework for distinguishing structural fidelity from magnitude calibration in LLM social reasoning, which is crucial for applications where precise inference strength matters, such as persuasion or negotiation. It also demonstrates that pragmatic theory can inform prompt design, offering a practical lever to improve LLM alignment with human social cognition. The study focuses on numerical (im)precision and finds that all models reproduce qualitative structure but differ in magnitude calibration. Prompting models to reason about speaker knowledge and motives consistently reduces magnitude deviation, while alternative-awareness prompting amplifies exaggeration; combining both components improves all calibration-sensitive metrics across all models, though fine-grained calibration remains only partially resolved.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 13, 04:00

**Background**: Large language models (LLMs) are increasingly used in tasks that require pragmatic and social reasoning, such as interpreting implicatures or inferring speaker intent. Calibration in this context refers to how well the model's quantitative judgments (e.g., effect sizes) match human judgments, beyond just getting the qualitative direction right. Pragmatic prompting involves designing prompts based on theories of language use, such as reasoning over linguistic alternatives or considering speaker knowledge states, to elicit more human-like responses.

## [Stable Scale Interval for Gaussian KANs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.21174) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper systematically analyzes the sensitivity of Gaussian Kolmogorov-Arnold Networks (KANs) to the scale parameter ε and proposes a stable operating interval ε ∈ [1/(G-1), 2/(G-1)], where G is the number of Gaussian centers. The authors validate this design rule through extensive experiments on function approximation and physics-informed problems. This work establishes scale selection as a central design principle for Gaussian KANs, addressing a critical gap in the literature. It provides practical guidance for researchers and practitioners, potentially improving the reliability and accuracy of KAN-based models across various applications. The analysis is grounded in the geometry and conditioning of the first-layer feature matrix, showing that loss of feature distinguishability in the first layer propagates through the network. The proposed interval is a stable design rule, not a universal optimum, and supports fixed-scale selection, variable-scale models, constrained optimization, and efficient scale search using early-stage training error.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 13, 04:00

**Background**: Kolmogorov-Arnold Networks (KANs) are a neural network architecture inspired by the Kolmogorov-Arnold representation theorem, replacing fixed activations with learnable univariate functions. Gaussian radial basis functions are a common choice for these functions, but their performance is sensitive to the scale parameter, which controls the width of the basis functions. This paper provides a systematic study of this parameter, offering a practical rule for selecting it to ensure stability and accuracy.

This paper introduces LQ-GM-PID, an analytically solvable class of bridge diffusions where scores and marginals are explicit for Gaussian-mixture targets, eliminating inner stochastic simulation loops. It demonstrates the approach on 2D corridor and multi-entrance tasks, plus high-dimensional scaling up to d=32 and M=16 modes. This work provides a reference model for testing score approximations and path-shaping objectives without relying on neural network training, potentially simplifying current generative modeling and stochastic control pipelines. This paper introduces Concept Fields, a black-box method that measures hallucination and novelty in text corpora using sentence-embedding deltas, and supports it with a new Vector Sequence Database (VSDB). This work offers a fast, lightweight, and interpretable alternative to LLM-as-judge and white-box detectors for hallucination and novelty detection, which is crucial for improving trustworthiness in AI-generated content. Its cross-domain stability suggests broad applicability across different text domains. The method scores candidate sentence transitions using zeta, the mean absolute z-distance between the observed delta and the field's local Gaussian estimate. It also sketches how divergence and curl of the Concept Field can surface semantic patterns like logic sources, sinks, and implicit topics, though these are hypothesis-generating rather than quantitative results.

## [Ranking vs. Assignment: Metric Mismatch in Multi-View Object Association](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.02022) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper reveals a fundamental mismatch between ranking metrics (AP, FPR-95) and the actual assignment objective in multi-view object association, and shows that Sinkhorn-based normalization can improve ranking metrics without improving assignment accuracy. This challenges common evaluation practices in the computer vision community, as models may appear better under ranking metrics while their actual assignment performance remains unchanged. It highlights the need for assignment-level metrics in multi-view association tasks, potentially influencing future research and evaluation standards. The paper provides theoretical proof that AP and FPR-95 can be imperfect even when assignments are correct, and that Sinkhorn normalization can make them perfect. Conversely, optimal pairwise ranking can still lead to incorrect assignments. They validate this with a controlled post-processing stress test, showing that optimizing a few parameters boosts AP and FPR-95 without improving assignment-level metrics like ACC and IPAA.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 13, 04:00

**Background**: Multi-view object association aims to match detections of the same physical object across synchronized camera views, typically formulated as a constrained one-to-one matching problem. Recent works often use pairwise ranking metrics like Average Precision (AP) and False Positive Rate at 95% True Positive Rate (FPR-95) for evaluation, but these may not align with the actual assignment objective. Sinkhorn normalization is an algorithm that alternates row/column scaling to produce doubly stochastic matrices, commonly used in optimal transport and machine learning applications.

## [LakeQuest: New Benchmark for Grounded QA over Data Lakes](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.12310) ⭐️ 7.0/10

LakeQuest is a new human-validated benchmark with 9,846 QA pairs across three domains (AI/ML metadata, retail banking, and multimodal biomedical drug information), designed to evaluate end-to-end retrieve-and-synthesize pipelines over realistic data lakes. It pairs every question with exact, modality-aware evidence pointers and includes baseline evaluations of standard RAG and agentic tool-use methods. This benchmark addresses a significant gap in QA evaluation by focusing on the noisy discovery process over heterogeneous data lakes, which current benchmarks often ignore. It exposes critical failure modes in modern QA systems, highlighting the need for robust discovery and faithful cross-file composition in future agentic QA systems. The benchmark spans three diverse domains and pairs each question with exact, modality-aware evidence pointers, isolating source discovery from cross-modal synthesis. Baseline evaluations show that high-quality retrieval does not guarantee correct reasoning, with systems struggling on relation chaining in metadata graphs, policy grounding in bank ledgers, and joint tabular QA in biomedical contexts.

Current benchmarks abstract away the noisy discovery process, failing to evaluate end-to-end performance. LakeQuest aims to bridge this gap by providing a benchmark that evaluates the full retrieve-and-synthesize pipeline, including source discovery and cross-modal synthesis.

## [Reducing Per-Sample Interference in Stochastic Optimization](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.16261) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces a method to reduce per-sample interference in stochastic optimizers by solving a surrogate optimization problem on the last linear layer, which can be integrated into standard optimizers like SGD and AdamW. This approach could improve generalization in deep learning models by addressing a previously overlooked source of optimization inefficiency. It offers a practical, scalable solution that can be easily adopted in existing training pipelines. The method reduces the problem's dimensionality to the batch size and restricts optimization to the last linear layer, which surprisingly captures core second-order statistics of the full network. It requires only a small number of GPU-friendly iterations and has relative computational overhead that shrinks as model size or input grows.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 13, 04:00

**Background**: Stochastic optimizers like SGD and AdamW combine gradients from the current mini-batch with historical state such as momentum or adaptive moments. However, this standard practice can produce parameter updates that increase the loss of individual samples, a phenomenon termed per-sample interference. The paper proposes to explicitly minimize this interference by redefining the parameter update as an optimization problem, using a surrogate for computational efficiency.

## [SCDG: Training-Free Method for Generative Plagiarism Detection](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.03859) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces Source-Conditioned Description-Length Gain (SCDG), a training-free framework that detects generative plagiarism by contrasting a frozen language model's description length of a document with and without a candidate source. On PAN 2025 and PAN 2026 benchmarks, SCDG outperforms all baselines, achieving 0.94 F1 on pairwise detection and 0.83 nDCG@10 on multi-source retrieval. Generative plagiarism, where LLMs rewrite or synthesize content from sources, poses a growing threat to academic integrity and peer review. SCDG provides a unified, training-free signal that can detect source reuse even after extensive rewriting, offering a practical tool for plagiarism detection systems and potentially improving source attribution in academic and publishing workflows. SCDG is token-decomposable and directional, measuring incremental predictive evidence supplied by a candidate source. On a same-topic, same-event Multi-News test, the calibrated classifier predicts source reuse for only 0.125% of pairs, indicating robustness to topical overlap. The method requires no training and works with a frozen language model.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 13, 04:00

**Background**: Generative plagiarism detection is an underexplored challenge, distinct from general LLM-generated text detection, which focuses on AI involvement rather than source reuse. Traditional similarity-based methods struggle with extensive rewriting and multi-source synthesis. The description-length view of probabilistic prediction, rooted in Minimum Description Length (MDL) principles, suggests that relevant side information can reduce a target sequence's code length, which SCDG leverages.

## [Epistemic Transfer: New Framework for Evaluating AI Verification Tools](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.08882) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces a framework and evaluation protocol for measuring epistemic transfer, the effect of prior AI-assisted verification on later unassisted performance on new claims. It defines two quantities, the Epistemic Transfer Effect (ETE) and Tool-Removal Cost (TRC), and proposes a diagnostic space to categorize outcomes. This framework shifts evaluation focus from immediate tool performance to long-term user capability, which is crucial for AI tools used in critical domains like fact-checking and content moderation. It provides a practical method to assess whether AI tools build skills or merely provide temporary assistance, informing design and deployment decisions. The protocol combines answer-first and evidence-first AI conditions with active-practice and no-practice controls, delayed tests on held-out claims, and participant- and item-level analyses. The diagnostic space separates four outcomes: capability building, capability plus tool advantage, epistemic inertness or de-skilling, and verification on loan.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 13, 04:00

**Background**: AI-assisted verification tools are typically evaluated while the tool is present, measuring immediate accuracy or user trust. However, this ignores whether users retain skills after the tool is removed. Epistemic transfer addresses this gap by measuring delayed unassisted performance, similar to educational concepts of learning transfer. The framework uses ETE and TRC to quantify the tool's lasting impact.

The paper introduces AlignXada, a training-free meta-learning framework that adapts universal user preference summaries to task-specific ones via verbal reinforcement learning. Across 13 tasks and three models, it achieves an average gain of 3.82 points while retaining only 22.8% of original profile tokens. This addresses a practical scalability issue in LLM personalization by reducing context waste and cross-task distraction, potentially improving efficiency and effectiveness of personalized AI systems. It complements universal memory construction for lifelong personalized agents, offering a practical solution for adapting preferences across diverse tasks. AlignXada uses verbal reinforcement learning to iteratively optimize a textual refinement policy, which is reusable across tasks. The faithfulness analysis shows refined profiles remain grounded in source preferences, preserving task-relevant signals, and it outperforms RAG in 36 out of 39 task-model cells.

## [MPAR-Bench: New Benchmark for LLM Reasoning Breadth](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.10444) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces MPAR-Bench, a bilingual English-Chinese benchmark with 1,000 items that evaluates multi-point associative reasoning in LLMs, inspired by the cooperative game Just One. It uses a multi-agent clue-generation pipeline and human verification to create diverse clues for hidden targets. This benchmark addresses a largely unexplored dimension of reasoning—breadth—complementing the focus on reasoning depth. It reveals that current LLMs' reasoning depth does not automatically confer robust reasoning breadth, highlighting a gap in existing evaluation methods. The benchmark includes four perturbations—clue masking, order shuffling, distractor injection, and multi-step clues—which reduce accuracy by 9-18 percentage points in English and 5-12 in Chinese. Thinking mode improves standard accuracy but does not consistently reduce perturbation sensitivity, and case analysis shows extended reasoning can overturn correct hypotheses.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 13, 04:00

**Background**: Large language models have shown progress in reasoning tasks that require long inferential chains, known as reasoning depth. Reasoning breadth, however, involves exploring multiple semantic directions in parallel and integrating clues, which is less studied. The benchmark is inspired by the cooperative game Just One, where players give clues to guess a hidden word.

## [ENTLORE: Benchmarking Latent Organizational Reasoning in Enterprise QA](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.10679) ⭐️ 7.0/10

ENTLORE introduces a graph-grounded benchmark construction framework that reconstructs audited enterprise worlds from routine documents and organizational tables, providing 2,341 documents and 907 questions across three source types. It evaluates 56 model and access configurations, revealing that even with gold documents, 30.4% of latent questions remain unanswered. This benchmark addresses a gap in existing enterprise QA benchmarks by focusing on latent organizational reasoning, where target relations are implicit across heterogeneous sources. It highlights that enterprise QA depends not only on document recall but also on making implicit organizational relations usable, which could influence future research and system design. The framework uses versioned organizational conventions to certify derived relations in a truth graph, enabling complete golden answers and proof certificates. The aligned anonymized release exposes only the document corpus while withholding private structure and target relations, and structuring the released world as an induced entity graph or navigable knowledge base gives the strongest deployable results.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 13, 04:00

**Background**: Enterprise question answering typically involves retrieving internal documents and generating grounded answers, but routine records are work by-products where organizational relations remain implicit. Existing benchmarks often materialize predefined answer paths, testing composition of stated facts rather than recovery of target relations absent from the corpus. ENTLORE aims to evaluate the latter capability, termed latent organizational reasoning, using a graph-grounded construction framework.

## [Luminance-Agnostic Colorization Framework for Historical Photos](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.10798) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper proposes a luminance-agnostic image colorization framework that uses a foundation image-editing model and a mixed grayscale objective to handle both standard and orthochromatic grayscale inputs, improving robustness on historical photos. This addresses a key limitation of existing colorization methods that rely on fixed luminance, which fail on historical orthochromatic photos. By leveraging foundation models, it aligns with current AI trends and could improve restoration of historical imagery. The method formulates colorization as full-RGB image editing and introduces a mixed grayscale objective that trains under both standard luminance and red-insensitive grayscale formations. Experiments on COCO, ImageNet, and a multi-instance benchmark show competitive performance on standard inputs and robustness on orthochromatic inputs.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 13, 04:00

**Background**: Traditional colorization methods operate in Lab color space, predicting chroma while preserving the input luminance channel. However, historical orthochromatic photography is insensitive to red light, producing grayscale images that differ from natural luminance, which breaks the fixed-luminance assumption. Panchromatic film, in contrast, is sensitive to all visible wavelengths, matching natural luminance.

## [CUE-Bench: New Benchmark for Affective Stance in Chinese Discourse](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.10810) ⭐️ 7.0/10

CUE-Bench is a new Chinese benchmark for affective stance that captures implicit, polite, ironic, and mismatched emotional expressions, aiming to improve evaluation of deep emotion understanding. This benchmark addresses a gap in emotion understanding benchmarks by focusing on implicit and pragmatic aspects, which could lead to more robust NLP models for Chinese and other languages. It provides a structured way to evaluate models on deeper emotional reasoning, benefiting researchers and developers in affective computing. CUE-Bench constructs nine human interpretable affective stances from explicit-implicit polarity interaction and provides intent and fine-grained emotion annotations. Experiments show that incorporating Affective Stance improves fine-grained emotion recognition by 3.5 percentage points and pragmatic intent detection by 7.8 percentage points over strong baselines.

**Background**: Affective stance refers to a participant's display of emotional orientation toward a stance object, often conveyed indirectly through politeness, irony, or mismatched expressions. Existing emotion benchmarks typically annotate surface polarity or final emotion categories, lacking a structured account of how explicit expression, implicit affect, pragmatic intent, and fine-grained emotion interact. CUE-Bench aims to fill this gap by focusing on Chinese discourse, where such indirect expressions are common.

## [AI Policy Convergence and Divergence Across National and Regional Strategies](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.11006) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper analyzes 74 national and 3 regional AI strategies from a global scan of 205 UN member and non-member states, coding them for policy design elements (goals, approaches, principles) using a latent-inductive approach. It finds strong horizontal convergence on economic competitiveness, research support, and ethical AI use, but persistent divergence on human rights, participatory governance, and human-centric principles. This systematic analysis fills a gap in AI policy literature by examining underlying policy design elements and their horizontal and vertical convergence/divergence over time. The findings provide policymakers and researchers with a comprehensive evidence base for identifying emerging norms in AI policy design, informing future strategy development and updates. The study uses a latent-inductive coding approach organized around three functional policy design elements: goals, approaches, and principles. It compares horizontal (country-to-country) and vertical (region-to-country) convergence, finding that the African Union (AU) shows the highest vertical convergence, the EU aligns on regulatory and economic priorities but diverges on human-centric values, and the Nordic-Baltic Region displays mixed vertical convergence.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 13, 04:00

**Background**: Policy design analysis is a scholarly approach that examines the components of public policies, such as goals, tools, and implementation mechanisms, to understand how policies are structured and how they evolve. Latent-inductive coding is a qualitative method that interprets underlying meanings in textual data, going beyond surface content to identify implicit themes. This paper applies these methods to AI strategies, which are national or regional documents outlining plans for AI development and governance.

## [Edge-based Contiguous p-median Problem for Logistics Districting](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.11230) ⭐️ 6.0/10

This paper introduces the edge-based contiguous p-median (ECpM) problem, which partitions road networks into compact and contiguous territories. It presents two binary programming models: one using exponential cut set-based constraints with a branch-and-cut algorithm, and another using polynomial shortest-path contiguity (SPC) constraints solvable with standard solvers. The ECpM problem addresses a practical need in logistics and network optimization for partitioning road networks into manageable territories. The SPC-based model achieves up to 17x speedup over the cut set-based approach, making large-scale districting problems more tractable for real-world applications. The models were tested on road networks with over 2,700 nodes and nearly 3,400 edges, resulting in models with over 9.6 million binary variables. The SPC constraints are shown to be supervalid inequalities for the edge-based p-median (EpM) model, potentially cutting off some optimal solutions but improving computational efficiency.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 13, 04:00

**Background**: The p-median problem is a classic facility location problem that seeks to locate p facilities to minimize the total distance between demand points and their nearest facility. In the edge-based variant, demand is on edges (roads) rather than nodes, and contiguity ensures that each territory is a connected subgraph, which is important for logistics districting where territories must be geographically coherent. The paper also connects ECpM to the edge-based districting (EBD) problem, which adds a work balance criterion.

## [Uncertainty-Aware Ensemble Deep Learning for Skin Lesion Classification](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.11280) ⭐️ 6.0/10

This paper proposes an uncertainty-aware and explainable ensemble deep learning framework combining MaxViT-Tiny, ConvNeXt-Tiny, and EfficientNetV2-B0 with MC Dropout and Grad-CAM++ for multi-class skin lesion classification, achieving 96% accuracy on HAM10000. This work addresses key challenges in skin cancer diagnosis by integrating uncertainty estimation and explainability, which are crucial for trustworthy computer-aided diagnosis. The high accuracy and interpretability could improve clinical decision-making and patient trust in AI-based diagnostic tools. The framework achieves 99% ROC-AUC under uncertainty-aware filtering (entropy < 1.0, confidence >= 0.7), with macro-average precision, recall, and F1-score of 94%, 95%, and 95%, respectively. The ensemble combines a vision transformer (MaxViT-Tiny) with CNN-based models (ConvNeXt-Tiny and EfficientNetV2-B0), and MC Dropout estimates predictive uncertainty while Grad-CAM++ provides visual explanations.

**Background**: Skin lesion classification from dermoscopic images is challenging due to high intra-class variability, inter-class similarity, and class imbalance. Deep learning models often lack interpretability, which hinders their adoption in clinical settings. Ensemble learning combines multiple models to improve robustness, while uncertainty estimation and explainable AI techniques like MC Dropout and Grad-CAM++ help build trust by identifying unreliable predictions and highlighting influential regions.

## [Low-Magnification MUSE Imaging Matches 10x for Breast Cancer Margin Detection](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.11317) ⭐️ 6.0/10

A new study demonstrates that 4x magnification MUSE imaging, combined with deep learning or texture analysis, achieves diagnostic accuracy comparable to 10x magnification for breast cancer margin detection. Specifically, deep learning achieved 98.18% accuracy at both magnifications, while texture analysis achieved 96.67% accuracy at both. This finding could accelerate intraoperative margin assessment by enabling faster image capture and larger field of view without sacrificing accuracy, potentially improving surgical outcomes and workflow efficiency. It also suggests that lower-cost, lower-magnification systems could be clinically viable, broadening access to advanced imaging techniques. The study compared 4x and 10x MUSE images using patch-level classification with texture analysis (local binary patterns) and a Vision Transformer (ViT) deep learning model. Notably, texture analysis at 4x achieved 100% specificity versus 93.33% at 10x, while 10x yielded 100% sensitivity versus 93.33% at 4x, but overall accuracy was identical.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 13, 04:00

**Background**: MUSE (Microscopy with Ultraviolet Surface Excitation) is a slide-free fluorescence imaging technique that uses shallow UV penetration to rapidly generate histological images from fresh tissue without traditional sectioning. Texture analysis via local binary patterns (LBP) is a classic computer vision method for classifying image textures, while Vision Transformer (ViT) is a modern deep learning architecture that applies transformer models to image classification tasks.

## [Terminal Symmetry as Decision Resource for Anytime Verified Construction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.11318) ⭐️ 6.0/10

This paper introduces a decision-resource view of terminal symmetry for anytime verified construction, proposing a transport-refine-certify method with statewise refinement. The method, named SymBuild, achieves significant improvements in anytime AUC across CAD assembly, Mini-Programs, and exact-fill packing tasks. This work provides a novel theoretical framework that treats terminal symmetry as a reusable decision resource, potentially improving efficiency and verification in sequential construction tasks. It could benefit researchers and practitioners in formal methods, AI planning, and automated verification by offering a new way to leverage symmetry for anytime algorithms. The method decomposes into transport, refine, and certify steps, using an episode-fixed transported process structure and state-dependent residual rank. It attains a tight worst-case verifier-query bound and demonstrates a strict post-transition dynamic-static separation in a two-state construction. On 1,135 target-removal episodes from GRN OOD scenes, SymBuild achieves the lowest mean capped verifier cost at all three scales compared to GRN and CDGS-style planners.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 13, 04:00

**Background**: Anytime verified construction refers to algorithms that can provide a valid solution at any point during execution, with verification ensuring correctness. Terminal symmetry is a property where the final state exhibits symmetry even though the construction process is directed and history-dependent. The transport-refine-certify method leverages this symmetry to improve decision-making, using process evidence for directionality and statewise refinement to update decision relevance after transitions.

## [Socioduality: A New Framework for Human-AI Interaction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.11322) ⭐️ 6.0/10

The paper introduces 'socioduality', a relational process framework that captures the sequential, reciprocal, and history-carrying nature of human-AI interactions. It defines nested units (moves, episodes, pathways) and a minimum episode A1-B1-A2 requiring response and return contingency, with a frozen protocol calibrated on three unseen human-AI records. This framework offers a new analytical lens for human-AI interaction research, moving beyond endpoint-focused evaluations to preserve pathway information. It could influence interaction design and evaluation methodologies, providing a more nuanced understanding of how human and AI contributions co-evolve. The construct uses nested units: moves, confirmed sociodual episodes, linked pathways, and the broader interaction container. The protocol was calibrated on three unseen records, with exact convergence in two cases and one local multimodal unitisation difference in the third, with remaining disagreement at return-contingency boundaries.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 13, 04:00

**Background**: Human-AI interaction research often evaluates individual capabilities, combined performance, or final outputs, but these approaches do not preserve how one party's response becomes part of the conditions for the other's next contribution. Socioduality addresses this by proposing a relational process framework that captures the sequential and reciprocal nature of interactions, drawing on concepts from social psychology and human-computer interaction.

By improving global model accuracy and robustness, it could enhance the reliability of intelligent transportation systems and pave the way for more practical federated learning deployments in dynamic environments. The framework includes two algorithms: one for cloud server model updates and another for intra-cluster federated transfer learning. The data quality score mechanism is designed to prevent malicious vehicles from degrading the global model, and experiments on real-world datasets verify its effectiveness and efficiency.

## [Deep Learning Improves Relative Transfer Matrix Estimation for Multi-Microphone Speech Enhancement](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.11627) ⭐️ 6.0/10

This paper introduces three novel deep learning frameworks for estimating the Relative Transfer Matrix (ReTM) in multi-microphone speech enhancement, using time-domain and STFT-domain convolutional networks and an LSTM-based recurrent network. The proposed models achieve more accurate ReTM estimation than the existing covariance-based method across five objective metrics. This work is significant because it opens a new direction for ReTM estimation, which is crucial for speech enhancement in noisy environments, and demonstrates that deep learning can outperform traditional signal processing methods. It could lead to more robust and accurate speech enhancement systems for applications like hearing aids, hands-free devices, and video conferencing. The three proposed frameworks are supervised learning models: a time-domain convolutional network, a short-time Fourier transform (STFT) domain convolutional network, and an LSTM-based recurrent neural network. The experiments show that the deep learning models achieve more accurate ReTM estimation than the covariance-based method, and they also perform on par with the baseline for speech enhancement.

Traditionally, ReTM is estimated from the covariance matrices of multichannel recordings, but this paper explores deep learning as an alternative. ## [Consolidator Enables Persistent Routed Memory in Phasor Memory Networks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.11701) ⭐️ 6.0/10

The paper introduces Consolidator, a slot-local operator for Phasor Memory Networks (PMNet) that transforms routed short-term memory (STM) before accumulating it into long-term memory (LTM), enabling persistent routed memory across context boundaries without replaying source tokens. On a two-segment modulo-10 mapping task, this mechanism improves updated-mapping recall from 44.38% to 87.02% with only 12.35K trainable parameters. This work addresses a critical limitation in memory-augmented neural networks: persistence alone does not ensure that retained state influences future memory access. By demonstrating that learned consolidation can shape subsequent slot selection, it opens new avenues for building more efficient and context-aware AI systems that can maintain long-term memory without replaying past inputs. The backbone and memory interface are frozen, leaving only 12.35K Consolidator parameters trainable (0.041% of a 29.95M model). Learned consolidation outperforms forced identity accumulation by 21.40 percentage points without routing and 68.70 with routing, while immediate STM recall remains 89.90% in both conditions.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 13, 04:00

**Background**: Phasor Memory Networks (PMNet) are a novel architecture that uses unitary phasor representations to resolve memory volatility, enabling stable backpropagation through time. In memory-augmented neural networks, short-term memory (STM) is often copied into a slower long-term memory (LTM) to preserve state across context boundaries, but this persistence alone does not guarantee that the retained state influences subsequent memory access. The Consolidator mechanism addresses this by transforming routed STM before accumulation, and feeding the retained LTM into the hierarchical router to condition future slot selection.

## [DTW-GBC: Robust Noisy-Label Time-Series Classification](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.11704) ⭐️ 6.0/10

The paper introduces DTW-based Granular Ball Computing (DTW-GBC), which groups temporally similar training samples into granular balls and performs classification at the granule level. Two granular-ball construction strategies are proposed, and experiments on four benchmark datasets with symmetric label noise show that DTW-GBC mitigates performance degradation and requires fewer DTW comparisons than DTW-based 1-NN. This work addresses two critical issues in time-series classification: vulnerability to label noise and high computational cost during inference. By improving robustness and efficiency, DTW-GBC could make DTW-based classifiers more practical for real-world applications where noisy labels are common and computational resources are limited. The method leverages granular ball computing (GBC), which represents data as adaptive hyperspherical regions, to group similar time series. The two variants of DTW-GBC generally outperform DTW-based 1-NN in terms of robustness to label noise while substantially reducing the number of DTW comparisons needed during inference.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 13, 04:00

**Background**: Dynamic Time Warping (DTW) is a widely used distance measure for time-series classification, especially with nearest-neighbor (NN) classifiers. However, DTW-based NN classifiers are sensitive to mislabeled training samples and require many DTW computations during inference. Granular ball computing (GBC) is a technique that groups data into coarse-grained balls to improve efficiency and robustness in various learning tasks.

This paper proposes a HOPE-inspired Nested Learning architecture with Continuum Memory Systems and semantic caching for hallucination mitigation, evaluated on a hybrid benchmark of 310 prompts.
