<a id="item-313"></a>
## [P2MFDS: Privacy-Preserving Multimodal Fall Detection for Bathrooms](https://arxiv.org/abs/2506.17332) ⭐️ 6.0/10

The paper introduces P2MFDS, a dual-stream network that fuses millimeter-wave radar with 3D vibration sensing for privacy-preserving fall detection in bathrooms. It also presents a sensor evaluation framework and a large-scale multimodal dataset collected in real bathroom settings, to be released upon publication. This work addresses a critical gap in elderly care by combining privacy preservation with multimodal sensing, overcoming limitations of unimodal systems like WiFi, infrared, or mmWave alone. It could improve fall detection accuracy in complex bathroom environments, enhancing safety for the growing elderly population. P2MFDS uses a CNN-BiLSTM-Attention branch for radar motion dynamics and a multi-scale CNN-SEBlock-Self-Attention branch for vibration impact detection, uniting macro- and micro-scale features. The system reportedly achieves significant gains in accuracy and recall over state-of-the-art approaches, though specific metrics are not provided in the abstract.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 13, 04:00

**Background**: Fall detection is crucial for elderly safety, especially in bathrooms where over 80% of falls occur. Traditional unimodal systems, such as WiFi-based or infrared-based methods, suffer from environmental interference like multipath fading or temperature changes, reducing accuracy. Multimodal sensing, which combines multiple sensor types, can mitigate these issues, but privacy concerns with cameras or wearables limit adoption. This paper proposes a non-intrusive, privacy-preserving solution using radar and vibration sensors.

<a id="item-314"></a>
## [LLMs Generate Commonsense Axioms for NLI; Hybrid Approach Boosts Accuracy](https://arxiv.org/abs/2507.15100) ⭐️ 6.0/10

This study evaluates LLMs' ability to generate factual commonsense axioms for natural language inference (NLI) and proposes a reference-free LLM-as-Judge method to assess their factuality. Using Llama-3.1-70B and gpt-oss-120b on SNLI and ANLI benchmarks, the hybrid approach that selectively integrates highly factual axioms yields consistent accuracy gains of 3.87%-8.5%. This work addresses a critical gap in NLI by exploring whether LLMs can reliably generate commonsense knowledge, which is essential for human-like inference. The proposed reference-free evaluation method and hybrid prompting pipeline could improve NLI systems and reduce biases, benefiting applications like question answering and fact-checking. The study found a substantial performance gap: gpt-oss-120b generates predominantly accurate axioms, while Llama-3.1-70B produces more incorrect than correct ones. The hybrid approach selectively incorporates axioms based on judged factuality, and targeted commonsense knowledge helps models overcome a bias toward the Neutral class.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 13, 04:00

**Background**: Natural Language Inference (NLI) is a task that classifies whether a hypothesis entails, contradicts, or is neutral with respect to a premise, often requiring commonsense knowledge. LLM-as-Judge is a method where a large language model evaluates outputs of another model based on criteria, which is used here because commonsense axioms lack explicit references for standard factuality metrics. SNLI and ANLI are standard benchmarks for NLI, with ANLI designed to be more adversarial and challenging.

<a id="item-315"></a>
## [QANA: Quantization-Aware CNN Backbone for Neuromorphic Skin Lesion Classification](https://arxiv.org/abs/2507.15958) ⭐️ 6.0/10

The paper introduces QANA, a quantization-aware CNN backbone designed for stable conversion to neuromorphic processors, achieving 91.6% Top-1 accuracy on HAM10000 and 90.8% on a clinical dataset, with 1.5 ms and 1.7 mJ per image on BrainChip Akida. This work addresses key barriers to deploying CNNs on neuromorphic hardware for medical imaging, potentially enabling low-power, on-device skin lesion analysis in resource-constrained settings. It demonstrates significant accuracy and efficiency gains over existing converted SNN baselines, advancing edge AI in healthcare. QANA improves conversion robustness by bounding intermediate activations, aligning normalization with low-bit quantization, and replacing conversion-fragile components with spike-compatible transformations. It uses Ghost-based feature generation, spatially-aware efficient channel attention, and squeeze-and-excitation modules that can be quantized, folded, or lowered into integer graph operations.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 13, 04:00

**Background**: Neuromorphic processors offer event-driven, sparse computation for energy-efficient AI, but converting trained CNNs to spiking neural networks (SNNs) often fails due to unsupported operators, quantization distortion, and accuracy loss. Quantization-aware training (QAT) integrates weight precision reduction into training to mitigate such issues. This paper builds on these concepts to enable practical deployment on devices like BrainChip Akida.

<a id="item-316"></a>
## [LSTM with Custom Loss and Adaptive Online Learning for Electricity Price Forecasting](https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.16898) ⭐️ 6.0/10

This paper introduces an LSTM-based model with a novel custom loss function combining MAE, Jensen-Shannon Divergence, and a smoothness penalty, along with an adaptive online learning framework for day-ahead electricity price forecasting in California. The adaptive online learning achieves the best performance, reducing MSE, MAE, and RMSE by approximately 23.0%, 3.4%, and 12.2% compared to the static model. Accurate electricity price forecasting is crucial for grid operators, energy producers, and consumers in dynamic energy markets. This research provides a robust framework that improves prediction accuracy and adaptability, potentially enhancing decision-making and market efficiency. The model incorporates historical price data, weather conditions, and the energy generation mix as features. The custom loss function integrates MAE, Jensen-Shannon Divergence, and a smoothness penalty to improve accuracy and interpretability, especially during peak intervals.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 13, 04:00

**Background**: Electricity price forecasting is a challenging time series problem due to non-stationarity and dependence on exogenous factors. LSTM networks are well-suited for capturing long-term dependencies in sequential data. Adaptive online learning allows models to update incrementally with new data, maintaining relevance in changing market conditions. Jensen-Shannon Divergence measures similarity between probability distributions, and its use in loss functions can improve model calibration.

<a id="item-317"></a>
## [MicroAUNet: Lightweight Attention Network for Polyp Segmentation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2511.01143) ⭐️ 6.0/10

MicroAUNet is a novel lightweight attention-based segmentation network that combines depthwise-separable dilated convolutions with a single-path, parameter-shared channel-spatial attention block to enhance multi-scale boundary features. It also introduces a progressive two-stage knowledge-distillation scheme to transfer semantic and boundary cues from a high-capacity teacher model. This work addresses the trade-off between segmentation accuracy and computational efficiency in colonoscopy polyp segmentation, which is critical for real-time clinical applications. By achieving state-of-the-art accuracy under extremely low model complexity, MicroAUNet could enable more accessible and faster colorectal cancer screening tools. The model uses depthwise-separable dilated convolutions to reduce parameters and computations while expanding receptive fields, and a parameter-shared channel-spatial attention block to strengthen boundary features. The progressive two-stage knowledge distillation transfers both semantic and boundary cues, and the code is publicly available on GitHub.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 13, 04:00

**Background**: Colorectal polyps are precursors to colorectal cancer, and early segmentation is vital for reducing mortality. Traditional deep learning models for polyp segmentation often produce ambiguous boundaries or require heavy architectures, limiting real-time use. Depthwise-separable convolutions factorize standard convolutions into depthwise and pointwise steps, reducing computational cost, while dilated (atrous) convolutions increase receptive field without extra parameters. Attention mechanisms like channel-spatial blocks help the model focus on relevant features, and knowledge distillation transfers knowledge from a large teacher model to a smaller student model.

<a id="item-318"></a>
## [MMGrader: VLMs Fall Short of Humans in Assessing Mental Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.00056) ⭐️ 6.0/10

The paper introduces MMGrader, a concept-graph-based framework for inferring the quality of students' mental models from multimodal short answers. In evaluations with 9 openly available models, the best-performing VLM achieved only about 40% accuracy, significantly below human-level performance. This work highlights the current limitations of VLMs in complex educational reasoning tasks, suggesting that while they can assist teachers, they are not yet reliable for automated mental-model assessment. It underscores the need for further research to improve VLM capabilities in this domain, which could eventually enable scalable, personalized feedback in STEM education. MMGrader uses concept graphs to break down topics into concept links, establish relevancy mappings between questions and links, and provide a rubric for evaluation. The best models achieved a prediction error of 1.1 units and a scoring distribution fairly aligned with human scoring patterns, but accuracy remained around 40%.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 13, 04:00

**Background**: Mental models are internal representations of how students understand and relate concepts, crucial for assessing conceptual understanding in STEM. Inferring them from student answers requires deep reasoning, which is challenging for current AI models. MMGrader aims to automate this process using concept graphs, but the study shows that current VLMs are not yet up to the task.

<a id="item-319"></a>
## [PPOC-LL: Prototype Learning with Progressive Offset Correction for Medical Landmark Localization](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.09182) ⭐️ 6.0/10

The paper introduces PPOC-LL, a parameter-efficient model that combines prototype learning with progressive offset correction for accurate medical landmark localization. It employs a multi-scale dynamic perception strategy, a similarity-driven prototype learning mechanism, and an error-aware reliability regularization to achieve coarse-to-fine optimization. This work addresses the high computational cost of multi-stage refinement methods in medical landmark localization, offering a favorable trade-off between accuracy and model complexity. It could enable more practical deployment of automated landmark detection in clinical settings, benefiting quantitative analysis and downstream tasks. PPOC-LL was validated on two public and one private dataset spanning X-ray and ultrasound modalities, covering cephalometric, symphysis-fetal head, and fetal heart landmarks. The model introduces a novel error-aware reliability regularization via tolerance-based balancing to stabilize learning and improve performance.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 13, 04:00

**Background**: Medical landmark localization is a fundamental step for quantitative clinical measurement and downstream analysis. Traditional multi-stage refinement methods mitigate anatomical ambiguity but are computationally expensive. Prototype learning is a technique that captures representative patterns for robust prediction, and progressive offset correction refines predictions iteratively.
